## st-0014 — A Christmas Carol in Prose; Being a Ghost Story of Christmas
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
Scrooge is not a good man. He is a hard man. He does not like Christmas. He does not like good people. People do not like him. People stay away from him.

Scrooge has a small heat. It is in his office. His heat is small. Another man has a very small heat. This man is not warm. It is not warm outside. The day is not warm.

A young man visits Scrooge. He is Scrooge's family. He comes to the office. He says "Good Christmas!" Scrooge does not like this. Scrooge is not good.

Scrooge thinks Christmas is not good. It is a bad time. He does not like Christmas. He does not want Christmas. He just wants to work. He works all day. He works all night.

The young man tries to help. He wants Scrooge to feel good. But Scrooge does not change. He says no. He is too hard. He has no good feeling. He does not look happy.

Outside is very bad. The day is bad. It is not warm. The day is very not warm. But Scrooge does not care. He does not feel the low heat. He does not feel it. He just works.

Scrooge wants to be with no one. He wants to work with no one. He does not want to talk. He does not want to see people. He just wants to work. He wants to rest. He likes no sound.

But Scrooge's life changes. Something different happens. It is a big change. It happens soon. It changes his life. It changes his life always. It is a new life for him.

### A2
Scrooge was a man. He was a very cold man. He had a hard heart. He did not like Christmas time. He did not like happy people. People were afraid of Scrooge. They did not want to talk to him. Scrooge did not have many friends. He did not want to have friends. He only wanted to work and make money. He always thought about money.

Scrooge had an office in the city. He had a small fire in his office. His clerk, Bob Cratchit, worked for him. Bob Cratchit had a table in the same room. He had a very, very small fire. The office was very cold inside. It was very cold outside too. Snow was falling. The air was strong and cold. The day was almost over.

A young man came to the office. His name was Fred. Fred was a family member of Scrooge. Fred was a happy man. He said to Scrooge, "Merry Christmas!" He had a big smile. Scrooge did not like this. He did not like Fred's happy words. He did not like Christmas.

Scrooge thought Christmas was not good. He thought it was a bad time. He did not want to stop work. He did not want to think about Christmas. He just wanted to work all day. He wanted to make a lot of money. Christmas was a waste of his time. He did not feel happy about it.

Fred tried to talk more to Scrooge. He wanted Scrooge to feel happy. "Come to my house for Christmas dinner," Fred said. "We will have good food." But Scrooge said, "No!" He did not change his mind. He was too cold. He was too hard. He did not want to be with family.

The weather outside was very bad. It was snowing a lot now. The snow was very white. The air was very cold. The city was full of snow. People went home fast from work. They did not want to be cold. But Scrooge did not care about the bad weather. He just wanted to work. He did not feel the cold.

Scrooge just wanted to be alone. He did not want to talk to anyone. He did not want to see anyone. He just wanted to finish his work for the day. Then he wanted to go home and sleep. He did not like other people. He did not like happy sounds.

But Scrooge's life was going to change. Something strange was going to happen that night. It was going to happen very soon. This strange thing was going to change Scrooge. It was going to change his life forever. He did not know this yet. He was going to learn a lot.

### B1
Scrooge was a cold man. He was a hard man. He never felt love. He liked no one. Scrooge was busy with money. Money was very important. He worked all day. He counted money daily. He did not like Christmas. He disliked happy times. Many knew Scrooge. They were often afraid. He was not a good friend. He had no friends. His face was serious. He did not often smile. He did not spend money. He was careful with each piece of money. He kept his money close. He thought only of himself.

Scrooge worked in a big office. It was a dark, old place. He had a small fire. This fire kept him warm. It was not very big. It gave little heat. His worker, Bob Cratchit, worked near him. Bob was a kind, good man. Bob had a desk in the room. He also had a fire. Bob's fire was smaller. It gave very little heat. Bob was always cold. He put on more clothes. He tried to keep warm. His job was hard. Outside, the weather was very cold. It was Christmas night. The air was cold. People walked fast. They wanted to go home. They wanted to be warm. The city had much snow.

Then, Scrooge's young family member came. His name was Fred. Fred was a happy young man. He had a big smile. He came through the door. He looked at Scrooge with a good feeling. "Merry Christmas, Uncle!" Fred said with a loud voice. Scrooge looked at him coldly. He did not like these words. "Christmas? Nonsense!" Scrooge said. "What is Christmas?" "It is a waste of time!" Fred felt a little sad. But he kept smiling. "Christmas is a good day, Uncle," Fred said. "It is a time for family. It is a time for love. We should be happy today." Scrooge shook his head. He did not believe Fred.

Scrooge thought Christmas was a waste. He thought it was not good. "You lose money on Christmas," Scrooge said. "You do not work. People spend money. They do not earn money. If people do not work, they will not earn. This is bad for business." Fred tried to talk more. He wanted Scrooge to understand. "But Christmas brings a good feeling, Uncle," Fred said. "It makes people happy. It is a good feeling. Many love this time of year." Scrooge did not listen. He turned his face away. He just wanted to work. He wanted to make money. He did not want holidays. Holidays were not good for business. He had never liked them. He would never like them.

Fred still tried to make Scrooge happy. He spoke about Christmas dinner. "Come to our house tonight, Uncle," Fred said. "My wife will be there. We will have a good time. Please come." Scrooge looked at him coldly. "No, thank you," he said. "You are poor. Why are you happy? I do not understand." Fred said, "I am happy because of Christmas, Uncle. It makes my heart light." Scrooge did not understand this. He thought Fred was strange. He would not change his mind. He would not go to dinner. He was too cold inside. His heart was too hard for a good feeling. Fred saw it was no use. He said he must leave his family member. He left the office slowly. Scrooge was alone again.

The weather outside grew worse. It was very bad. More snow began to fall. The wind was strong and cold. The sky was dark. City lights began to give light. People walked fast through streets. They pulled coats close. They wanted to leave the cold. They looked for warm places. But Scrooge did not care. He stayed in his office. He did not feel cold. Or he did not want to feel it. He worked on his papers. He counted his money. He thought only of business. Nothing else was important. He did not look out.

Scrooge wanted to be alone. He wanted no one to talk. He did not like noise. He disliked people talking around him. He just wanted quiet. He wanted to work in peace. After work, he would go home. He would eat a small dinner alone. Then he would go to sleep. He did this every day. He had no friends to visit. He had no family to see. His life was simple. It was always the same. He thought this was a good life. A life with no trouble. He did not need other people. He did not need their help. He was fine alone.

But this Christmas night was different. Scrooge's life would change. He did not know this yet. Something strange was coming. It would happen very soon. It would happen in his old house. This thing would change Scrooge. It would change his mind. It would change his heart. His life would never be the same. It would change him for a long time. He would learn many things. He would see the past. He would see the future. Scrooge would meet visitors soon. They would show a new way to live. His life would become better.

### B2
Scrooge was a man with a cold heart. He was also a very hard man, who was known by many in the town. He did not like the holiday of Christmas at all. Many people were afraid of him. They knew he was not a kind person. His face often held a serious and firm look. He kept to himself most of the time. This was how his life was lived. If Scrooge had been more open, he might have had many friends.

Scrooge kept a small fire in his office space. The office was always kept cold. His employee, Bob Cratchit, had an even smaller fire. The air in the room felt very cold. It was very cold outside the building, which was old. Snow fell from the sky with no end. The wind made a sad sound around the windows. Scrooge did not seem to care about the low temperature. His employee shook from the cold as he worked.

Scrooge’s family member came to visit him. This was Fred, his sister’s son, who was a kind man. Fred said, 'Happy Christmas' to Scrooge. Scrooge did not like these words, which were full of joy. He did not like the holiday at all. His family member tried to be kind and warm. If Fred had not come, Scrooge would have been completely alone.

Scrooge believed Christmas was a waste of time. He thought it took people away from their important work. He did not want to celebrate it. He just wanted to work and earn money. He did not understand joy or good will. If he had understood joy, his life would be different. His days were filled only with his business.

Scrooge’s family member tried to make him feel happy. He spoke of the good things about Christmas. But Scrooge would not change his mind. He was too cold and too hard in his ways of thinking. He sent his family member away from the office. The kind words were not heard by Scrooge.

The weather outside was truly very bad. There was much snow on the ground everywhere. The air was very cold, a bitter cold. But Scrooge did not care about the cold or the snow. He only cared about his own work and profit. No warm feeling was offered by him. He just wanted to be left alone and sleep at home.

But Scrooge’s life was about to change. Something strange was going to happen soon to him. It would change Scrooge’s life forever. A series of important events would begin this night. He would never be the same after these events. This would be a big and lasting change. He was not prepared for what was coming.

If Scrooge had been a kind man, he would have been happy. But he was not kind at all to people. So, he was not happy in his life. He was alone on the night of Christmas, as always. He often felt a great sad feeling deep inside. His heart was closed to the world. His employee, Bob Cratchit, was also very cold.

Bob had a small fire for his work space, which was not much. But it was not enough heat for the long day. He wished Scrooge would give him more coal for the fire. He knew Scrooge had much coal in his own room. But Scrooge would not share his fire. If more coal had been given, Bob would have been warm.

If Scrooge’s employee had more coal, he would have been warm. But he did not have more coal to use. So, he was very cold all day long. He sat at his desk, shaking from the cold. His hands were blue from the low temperature. His work was done with great difficulty.

Scrooge’s family member came to visit him again. This was later in the day, as evening came. He brought some food and drink to share. He tried to make Scrooge happy with his visit. But Scrooge still had a difficult mood. He was not pleased by the kind offer. He did not welcome the food or the company.

If Scrooge had eaten the food, he might have felt better. But he did not eat the food, which was warm. So, he still felt bad inside himself. He just sat there, still and cold. He looked at his family member with a cold eye. His family member tried again to talk to him, but Scrooge pushed him away.

Scrooge’s life was truly not good for him. He felt cold inside himself, in his own heart. He often felt alone and very sad. He did not have any friends at all. If he had been a kind person, he would have had friends. But he was not kind, so he was alone on the night of Christmas.

Something strange was going to happen to Scrooge. It would change his life forever, in a big way. He would never be the same again, it was clear. This change would start tonight, after dark. He was not prepared for it at all. His future was about to be changed forever.

Scrooge would meet a strange visitor. This visitor, which was not of this world, would appear. This visitor would show him something new and important. It would make Scrooge change his ways and actions. This change would be great and much needed. It would affect everything in his life. A message would be clearly sent to him.

If Scrooge had not met the strange visitor, he would still be difficult. He would still feel bad and keep his cold heart. But he did meet the visitor, which was a surprise. So, he changed his life, day by day. He became a kind and happy man. This was a good change for all.

Scrooge’s employee was happy when Scrooge changed. He was glad that Scrooge was kind to him. He felt happy to work for Scrooge now, which was new. His work life was much better and warmer. More coal was given to his fire each day. If Scrooge had not changed, his employee would not have been happy.

Scrooge’s family member was also happy when Scrooge changed. He was glad that Scrooge was kind and open. He felt happy to visit Scrooge, which was a joy. Their family connection grew strong and real. This made Fred very pleased. If Scrooge had not changed, his family member would not have been happy.

Scrooge was happy when he changed his life. He felt good about himself and his new actions. He was glad to be kind to other people now. A great weight was lifted from his heart. He saw the world with new eyes and understanding. If Scrooge had not changed, he would not have been happy.

Scrooge’s life was good after he changed his ways. He was kind to everyone around him. He was happy in his heart and in his home. He had friends and family who loved him very much. He shared his money and his time with others. This was a new way of being, full of light.

If Scrooge had not changed, his life would not have been good. It would have been a poor and cold life. But he did change, which was wonderful. So, his life was good and full of meaning. He was glad that he had changed his ways. This made all the difference to him and to others.

### C1
Scrooge was a man with a heart as cold as the winter air that seeped through the old building's walls. His face, a map of stern lines and deep creases, often held a serious and firm look, deterring many from approaching him. Many people were afraid of him, knowing he was not a kind person, and his reputation as a hard man spread far and wide in the town. If Scrooge had been more open, he might have had many friends, but his closed nature made him a solitary figure. This was how his life was lived, in solitude, with no warmth to thaw the frost that had settled around his heart.

Scrooge kept a small fire in his office space, a meager attempt to ward off the chill that gripped the old building. The office was always kept cold, the air thick with the scent of old books and dust, and his employee, Bob Cratchit, had an even smaller fire to warm his workspace. The wind outside made a sad sound around the windows, like a mournful sigh, as snow fell from the sky with no end in sight. Scrooge did not seem to care about the low temperature, his gaze fixed on the papers in front of him, but his employee shook from the cold as he worked. The contrast between Scrooge's indifference and Bob's suffering was stark, a testament to the deep divide between them.

Scrooge's family member, Fred, his sister's son, came to visit him, bringing with him a warm smile and a kind heart. Fred said, 'Happy Christmas' to Scrooge, but the words fell flat, like a rejected gift, as Scrooge's face remained impassive. Scrooge did not like the holiday at all, viewing it as a waste of time, a distraction from the important work of earning money. His family member tried to be kind and warm, but Scrooge's cold demeanor made it difficult for Fred to penetrate the frost that surrounded him. If Fred had not come, Scrooge would have been completely alone, a solitary figure in a sea of people celebrating the holiday.

Scrooge believed Christmas was a frivolous indulgence, a time when people abandoned their duties and responsibilities to indulge in idle pursuits. He thought it took people away from their important work, and he did not want to celebrate it, preferring instead to focus on his business. He did not understand joy or good will, his heart closed to the world, and his days were filled only with the pursuit of profit. If he had understood joy, his life would be different, filled with warmth and connection, but as it was, he was a cold and hard man. His employee, Bob, was a reminder of the human cost of Scrooge'ssingle-minded pursuit of wealth.

Scrooge's family member tried to make him feel happy, speaking of the good things about Christmas, of love and kindness and generosity. But Scrooge would not change his mind, his heart too cold and hard to be swayed by Fred's words, and he sent his family member away from the office. The kind words were not heard by Scrooge, falling on deaf ears, as he remained unmoved and unrepentant. The weather outside was truly very bad, with much snow on the ground everywhere, and the air was very cold, a bitter cold that seeped into the bones. But Scrooge did not care about the cold or the snow, his only concern his own work and profit, as he sat alone in his office.

But Scrooge's life was about to change, something strange and unexpected about to happen to him, something that would alter the course of his life forever. A series of important events would begin this night, events that would shake Scrooge to his core and challenge his long-held beliefs. He was not prepared for what was coming, his heart and mind closed to the possibility of change, but change was coming nonetheless. The wind howled outside, like a chorus of restless spirits, as the snow fell softly, covering the town in a thick layer of white. Scrooge's life would never be the same again, as he stood on the threshold of a new era, one that would bring him joy and sorrow in equal measure.

If Scrooge had been a kind man, he would have been happy, surrounded by friends and family who loved him, but he was not kind at all to people. So, he was not happy in his life, his days filled with a deep sadness, a sense of emptiness that he could not fill, no matter how hard he tried. His heart was closed to the world, and his employee, Bob Cratchit, was also very cold, struggling to survive in a world that seemed determined to crush him. Bob had a small fire for his work space, which was not much, but it was not enough heat for the long day, and he wished Scrooge would give him more coal for the fire. But Scrooge would not share his fire, his heart too cold to consider the suffering of others.

Scrooge's family member came to visit him again, later in the day, as evening came, bringing with him some food and drink to share. He tried to make Scrooge happy with his visit, but Scrooge still had a difficult mood, his heart closed to the possibility of joy or connection. If Scrooge had eaten the food, he might have felt better, but he did not eat, and so he still felt bad inside himself, his heart heavy with sorrow. He just sat there, still and cold, his eyes fixed on some distant point, as his family member tried again to talk to him, but Scrooge pushed him away. The silence between them was oppressive, a heavy weight that threatened to crush them both.

Scrooge's life was truly not good for him, his heart cold and hard, his spirit crushed by the weight of his own making. He felt cold inside himself, in his own heart, and he often felt alone and very sad, a deep sadness that seemed to have no end. He did not have any friends at all, and if he had been a kind person, he would have had friends, but he was not kind, so he was alone on the night of Christmas. The darkness outside seemed to press in on him, a physical manifestation of his own inner emptiness, and he felt small and insignificant in the face of it. But still he held on to his cold heart, refusing to let go, even as the world around him seemed to be moving on without him.

Something strange was going to happen to Scrooge, something that would change his life forever, in a big way, and he would never be the same again. A series of events would unfold, events that would challenge everything Scrooge thought he knew about himself and the world around him. He would meet a strange visitor, a visitor who would show him something new and important, something that would make Scrooge change his ways and actions. This change would be great and much needed, a transformation that would affect everything in his life, and Scrooge would be given a chance to redeem himself. The question was, would he take it, or would he remain forever trapped in his cold and hard heart.

Scrooge would become a kind and happy man, a man who would spread joy and warmth to those around him, and his employee, Bob, would be happy when Scrooge changed. He would be glad that Scrooge was kind to him, and he would feel happy to work for Scrooge, which was new, as his work life would be much better and warmer. More coal would be given to his fire each day, and Bob would be warm and content, his heart full of gratitude for the change in Scrooge. Scrooge's family member, Fred, would also be happy when Scrooge changed, glad that Scrooge was kind and open, and he would feel happy to visit Scrooge, which was a joy. Their family connection would grow strong and real, a bond that would last a lifetime, and Scrooge would finally find the happiness and connection he had been missing for so long.

Scrooge was happy when he changed his life, feeling good about himself and his new actions, as a great weight was lifted from his heart. He saw the world with new eyes and understanding, and he was glad to be kind to other people now, his heart full of warmth and generosity. If Scrooge had not changed, he would not have been happy, and his life would have been a poor and cold one, devoid of love and connection. But he did change, and his life was good and full of meaning, as he shared his money and his time with others, and he was glad that he had changed his ways. This made all the difference to him and to others, as Scrooge finally found his place in the world, and he lived happily ever after.

---

## st-0015 — The Wonderful Wizard of Oz
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
Dorothy lives in Kansas. She lives with Uncle Henry and Aunt Em. They live in a small gray house. The house is old. It has a low top. It has a small front porch. A small room is under the house. The family goes there during a storm.

Uncle Henry works the land. He works from morning until night. Aunt Em is thin and serious. Dorothy has a little black dog. His name is Toto. Dorothy loves to play with Toto. Toto makes her feel very good.

One day, Uncle Henry looks above. He sees a very big storm. The sky is black. The wind is very strong. Aunt Em does not feel good. She tells Dorothy to go under.

But the storm comes now. The small house starts to move. The strong wind takes the house. The house moves up and away. Dorothy does not feel well. She holds Toto very close. She waits to see what happens.

The house is high in the light. It is very high. Dorothy looks out of the window. She sees the land far down. The house turns around and around. Dorothy feels very bad.

Now, the house starts to fall. Dorothy is still not well. The house lands with a big sound. Dorothy is not well but she is okay. She goes outside the house. She wants to see the new place.

She is in a great land. There are many colors. The light is very clear. Dorothy is well and she feels good. She sees this great new place.

The house is on a bad witch. Now, the bad witch is dead. The people of the land feel good. They thank Dorothy for this. They ask Dorothy to stay.

Dorothy wants to stay. But she also wants to go home. She wants her family in Kansas. She wants to see them again. She asks the people for help.

### A2
Dorothy lived with Uncle Henry and Aunt Em. They lived in a small house. It was on the great Kansas land. The house was small and dark. It had a low top. It had a little front door. There was a small space under the ground. This space was for very bad days of strong air. The family could go there.

Uncle Henry worked hard on the land. He worked from morning until night. Aunt Em was a kind woman. She was small and serious now. She lived on the land for many years. Dorothy loved to play. She played with her small black dog. His name was Toto. Toto made her laugh a lot.

One day, Uncle Henry looked up. He saw a very bad day coming. The air was very strong. The air was dark. Aunt Em felt great fear. She told Dorothy to go fast. She told her to go to the space.

They could not get to the space. The strong air got very bad. The house started to move. Strong air moved the house up. It carried the house away. The house went high in the air. Dorothy felt great fear. She held Toto very close. She waited to see what would happen.

The house moved high in the air. Dorothy felt like she was going up. She looked out the window. The ground was far below her. The house was moving around and around. Dorothy felt very bad from this.

After some time, the house fell. Dorothy felt fear. The house did not fall hard. Instead, it landed with a big sound. Dorothy was okay. But she felt very bad. She got out of the house fast. She wanted to see the new place.

She was in a beautiful land. There were green trees. There were plants of many colors. These plants were in all places. She heard happy sounds. The light was very beautiful. Dorothy was happy to be not dead. It was a very beautiful place.

She looked around. The house landed on a bad woman. The bad woman was dead. The people of the land were happy. They thanked Dorothy very much. She killed the bad woman. They asked her to stay with them.

Dorothy was happy to stay there. But she also wanted to go home. She missed Uncle Henry and Aunt Em. She wanted to see them again soon. She asked for help from the people. She wanted to find a way home.

### B1
Dorothy lived with her Uncle Henry and Aunt Em. Their house was small. It was on the great land of Kansas. The house was gray. It had a low roof. There was a little porch in front of the house. Inside, the house was also small. There was a small hole in the ground outside. This hole was a storm cellar. The family would go into this cellar. They went there if a very big storm came. This kept them safe from bad weather.

Uncle Henry was a farmer. He worked very hard. He worked from morning until night. Aunt Em was a kind woman. But she had become thin. She had also become very serious. This was because she had lived on the land for many years. Life on the land was hard. Dorothy loved to play with her little black dog. His name was Toto. Toto was a small dog. He made Dorothy laugh every day. He was her best friend.

One day, Uncle Henry looked at the sky. He saw a big storm coming. The sky was dark and gray. The wind was blowing very hard. It made a loud noise. Aunt Em felt afraid. She knew the storm was dangerous. She told Dorothy to go to the storm cellar. It was important to go there fast.

But they could not get to the cellar in time. The storm got much worse. The house started to shake a lot. Strong winds lifted the house up. It rose into the air. The wind carried it far away. Dorothy was very scared. She held on to Toto hard. She waited to see what would happen. She did not know what to do.

The house was carried high up. It went very far into the air. Dorothy felt like she was going up in a balloon. This was a strange feeling. She looked out the window. She saw the ground far below her. Everything looked very small. The house was spinning around and around. Dorothy got dizzy from the movement. She felt sick.

After a while, the house started to fall. Dorothy was scared again. She thought it would crash with great force. But it did not fall to the ground. Instead, it landed with a big crash. The crash made a loud sound. Dorothy was shaken. But she was okay. She got out of the house slowly. She wanted to see where she was now. She looked around carefully.

She found herself in a beautiful land. It was very different from Kansas. There were green trees everywhere. Colorful flowers grew all around. Many birds were singing sweet songs. A bright sun was shining in the sky. Dorothy was very happy to be alive. She was happy to see such a beautiful place. It felt like a dream.

She looked around again. She saw that the house had landed on a witch. The witch was dead now. The people of this new land were very happy. They had waited a long time for this. They came to Dorothy. They thanked her for killing the bad witch. They asked her to stay with them for a while. They said she was a hero. "If you stay with us," they told her, "we will be very happy."

Dorothy was happy to stay for a short time. But she also wanted to go home. She has missed her Uncle Henry very much. She has missed Aunt Em a lot. She wanted to see them again soon. She felt a little sad. She asked the people of the land for help. She wanted to find a way home to Kansas. She did not know the way.

### B2
Dorothy lived in the middle of the great flat land of Kansas. She lived there with her Uncle Henry and her Aunt Em. Their house was very small. The wood of the house had been burned gray by the hot sun. There was a small wooden step in front of the door. Under the house, there was a small hole in the ground. This hole was a safe room under the ground. The family could go there if a very strong wind storm came. If a storm had come, they would have run there immediately.

Uncle Henry was a farmer who worked hard from morning until night. He never laughed and he looked very serious. Aunt Em was also very thin and she did not smile anymore. When she first came to this flat land, she was a young and happy wife. However, the wind and the sun had taken all the color from her face. Now she was gray and quiet, just like the land around her. Dorothy was a little girl who brought life back to the house. She had a little black dog named Toto. Toto had long, soft hair and small black eyes that shone with joy. Dorothy played with him all day and she loved him very much. He was the one who made her laugh when everything else was gray.

One day, Uncle Henry stood on the step and looked at the sky. The sky was turning a dark and ugly gray. He could hear the low sound of the wind from the south. He knew that a dangerous wind storm was coming. He went to look after the animals on the farm. Aunt Em came to the door and saw the danger too. She was frightened by the dark clouds. She called out to Dorothy, telling her to run to the safe room under the ground. The wind began to blow very hard. It shook the small house. Dorothy ran inside to get her dog. Toto had jumped under the bed because he was afraid of the noise. Dorothy had to find him before she could run to safety.

Aunt Em had already gone down into the room under the ground. She called for Dorothy to follow her. But Dorothy was still inside, holding Toto in her arms. Before she could walk to the door, a strange thing happened. The wind hit the house with great force. The house shook so hard that Dorothy fell on the floor. Then, the house was lifted up into the air. It rose slowly, like a big bag of gas rising into the sky. It was carried by the wind to the very top of the storm. The house was held by the wind for a long time. It was moved miles and miles away from Kansas.

At first, Dorothy was very scared. She thought the house would fall and be destroyed. She felt her head spin as the house turned around and around. The spinning made her feel quite sick. She lay down on her bed and held Toto tight. The dog did not like the movement and he ran around the room. He made a lot of noise, but Dorothy remained quiet. After some time, she realized that they were not falling. The house was being carried gently, as if it were a boat on a quiet river. If she had looked out the window, she would have seen only dark clouds. She decided to close her eyes and wait. Eventually, she fell asleep, despite the noise of the wind outside.

Dorothy slept for many hours. When she finally woke up, she noticed that the house was no longer moving. There was no wind, and the air was very quiet. Suddenly, the house landed with a big crash. The shake was so strong that Dorothy was thrown onto the floor. However, she was not hurt. She sat up and listened carefully. She could hear the sweet sound of birds singing outside. She also saw bright sunlight coming through the window. She went to the door and opened it. Toto followed her closely, his tail moving with excitement. She looked out and was very surprised by the sight.

She was no longer in the gray land of Kansas. Instead, she was in a beautiful country that was full of life. There were tall green trees with sweet fruits. The ground was covered with grass and flowers of many colors. Bright yellow, red, and blue flowers were growing everywhere. The sun was shining with bright light, and the sky was a clear blue. This was a place that she had never seen before. It was a wonderful change from the dry, gray fields of her home. If her aunt had been there, she would have been amazed by the beauty.

As she stood there, she saw some people coming toward her. They were small people, and they wore strange clothes. They looked at Dorothy with great respect. Then, she noticed something under her house. Two feet, wearing silver shoes, were sticking out from under the wood. The people explained that her house had landed on top of an evil magic woman. This woman had ruled their land with fear for many years. Now, because of the house, the evil magic woman was dead. The people were very happy to be free from her power. They thanked Dorothy for saving them from this terrible person.

Dorothy was surprised to hear this. She had not intended to kill anyone. She explained that it was an accident caused by the storm. However, the people still praised her. They offered her food and beautiful gifts. Dorothy thanked them, but she felt a bit sad. She thought about her Uncle Henry and her Aunt Em. They would be worrying about her. She knew she must find a way to get back to Kansas. If she stayed here, her family would think she was dead. She asked the people if they knew the way to her home.

The people shook their heads. They told her that Kansas was a very far place. It was separated from their land by a huge desert. No one had ever crossed that desert and survived. They told her about another powerful person who lived in a great city. He was a good magic man who might be able to help her. If anyone could help her, it would be him. Dorothy decided that she must travel to this city to ask for his assistance. She prepared for her long journey with Toto by her side. She hoped she would see her family again soon.

### C1
Dorothy lived in the midst of the vast, flat land of Kansas, where the sun beat down relentlessly. She resided with her Uncle Henry and Aunt Em in a small, weathered house, its wooden facade grayed by the scorching sun. A tiny wooden step led up to the door, and beneath the house, a small, cozy hole in the ground served as a safe room, a refuge from the fierce storms that often swept across the plains. The family would hasten to this underground sanctuary at the first sign of a tornado's approach, seeking shelter from the tempest's fury. Uncle Henry, a diligent farmer, toiled from dawn till dusk, his face a mask of seriousness, his eyes narrowed against the glare of the sun.

Aunt Em, once a happy and vivacious young wife, had been worn down by the harsh conditions of the flat land, her face now as gray and barren as the landscape itself. The wind and sun had drained the color from her cheeks, leaving her a solemn, quiet figure, a shadow of her former self. Dorothy, a lively and spirited young girl, brought a spark of life to the house, her laughter and smile a beacon of hope in the desolate landscape. She had a constant companion in her little black dog, Toto, whose soft, shiny coat and bright, inquisitive eyes shone with joy, bringing a sense of wonder to her daily routine.

One day, as Uncle Henry stood on the step, gazing up at the sky, he noticed a dark, foreboding cloud gathering on the horizon, its leading edge a deep, ugly gray. The air was heavy with anticipation, the wind whispering an ominous warning, its low, mournful sighs carrying on the breeze. He knew that a storm was brewing, its approach heralded by the distant rumble of thunder, a low, menacing growl that seemed to vibrate through every cell of his body. Aunt Em, too, sensed the danger, her eyes widening with fear as she beheld the darkening sky, its clouds twisting and writhing like living serpents.

As the wind began to pick up, Aunt Em called out to Dorothy, urging her to seek shelter in the safe room beneath the house, her voice tight with anxiety. Dorothy, however, was hesitant to leave, her eyes fixed on Toto, who had scurried under the bed, his tail tucked between his legs, his eyes wide with fear. She knew she had to find him, to comfort him, before she could join her aunt in the safety of the underground room. But before she could grab Toto and make a dash for the door, a sudden, violent gust struck the house, sending her tumbling to the floor.

The house creaked and groaned, its wooden frame shuddering under the force of the wind, which howled and whistled, a deafening cacophony that threatened to consume everything in its path. Dorothy, dazed and disoriented, clung to Toto, her heart racing with fear, her mind reeling from the sheer intensity of the storm. As the house rose into the air, lifted by the tornado's fury, Dorothy felt her stomach drop, her senses reeling from the rollercoaster motion, her world turned upside down. She lay down on the bed, holding Toto tight, her eyes closed, her body rigid with fear, as the house spun and twirled, a helpless, hapless thing, at the mercy of the tempest.

The storm raged on, its fury unabated, the house twisting and turning, a small, insignificant speck, lost in the vortex of the tornado. Dorothy, however, began to feel a sense of detachment, a strange, eerie calm, as if she were floating above the chaos, watching it all unfold with a sense of remote, detached curiosity. The house, too, seemed to be moving with a strange, almost gentle, motion, as if it were being borne aloft on a great, invisible wave, its progress smooth, unhurried, and serene. As the hours passed, Dorothy's fear gave way to a deep, dreamless sleep, her body exhausted, her mind numb, her spirit drained.

When she awoke, the house was still, the storm having passed, leaving behind an oppressive, unsettling silence, a sense of stillness that was almost palpable. Dorothy sat up, rubbing her eyes, her mind foggy, her body stiff, her senses slowly coming back online. As she looked around, she realized that the house was no longer moving, its motion stilled, its wooden frame creaking softly, as if it, too, were stretching, yawning, and shaking off the remnants of the storm. She stood up, a little unsteadily, and made her way to the door, Toto by her side, his tail wagging tentatively, his eyes fixed on her face.

As she opened the door, a warm, golden light spilled in, illuminating the room, and Dorothy's eyes widened in wonder, her gaze drinking in the beauty of the landscape. The house had been transported to a land of breathtaking beauty, a realm of vibrant colors, lush green trees, and flowers of every hue, a kaleidoscope of colors that danced and swirled before her eyes. The air was filled with the sweet, melodious songs of birds, their trills and chirrups a joyous, uplifting music that seemed to match the rhythm of her heartbeat. Dorothy's heart swelled with joy, her spirit soaring, as she beheld the beauty of this strange, new world, a world that seemed to be full of wonder, full of magic.

As she stood there, drinking in the beauty of the landscape, Dorothy noticed a group of people approaching, their faces filled with a deep, abiding respect, their eyes fixed on her with a sense of awe and reverence. They were small, delicate creatures, dressed in strange, colorful garments, their clothes a riot of colors, a tapestry of textures and hues that seemed to shimmer and shine in the sunlight. Dorothy, feeling a sense of wonder, a sense of trepidation, watched as they drew near, their faces filled with a deep, unspoken gratitude, their eyes shining with a sense of hope, of expectation. And then, she saw it, the reason for their approach, the reason for their reverence: the house had landed on top of a dark, malevolent figure, a figure that seemed to embody the very essence of evil.

The people explained that the figure was an evil magic woman, a creature who had ruled their land with fear, her power unchallenged, her malevolence unrelenting. They told Dorothy that she had freed them from the magic woman's grasp, that she had brought them peace, and with it, a sense of hope, of renewal. Dorothy, however, felt a sense of unease, a sense of disquiet, for she had not intended to harm anyone, her actions driven by chance, by circumstance, rather than design. She explained that the storm had brought the house to this place, that she was merely a passenger, a hapless traveler, blown off course by the winds of fate.

The people, however, would not be swayed, their gratitude, their admiration, unwavering, unshakeable. They offered her food, and gifts, and praise, their words, their actions, a testament to their deep, abiding respect. Dorothy, though, felt a pang of sadness, a sense of longing, for she knew that she had to find a way back to Kansas, to her uncle, to her aunt, to the only home she had ever known. She asked the people if they knew the way back, but they shook their heads, their faces filled with a sense of regret, of sorrow.

They told her of a powerful magic man, a wise, and just, and benevolent ruler, who lived in a great city, a city of wonder, a city of magic. They said that he might be able to help her, that he might be able to guide her on her journey, to show her the way back home. Dorothy, filled with a sense of hope, a sense of determination, decided to embark on the journey, to seek out the magic man, to ask for his help. And so, with Toto by her side, she set off, ready to face whatever challenges lay ahead, ready to brave the unknown, to follow her heart, to find her way home.

## st-0016 — The Shadow House
Kaynak: ozgun

### A1
Emily visits her friend Max today. He has a new home. The house is very big. The house is also very old. Emily looks at the large house. She walks to the front door.

Max opens the door for Emily. He shows Emily around the house. They walk into many rooms. Emily feels good to see Max. She likes his new place.

The next day, Emily is there. She walks and she gets lost. She does not know the way. The house has many doors. These doors lead to different rooms. Every room looks new to her.

She finds Max in the house. They try to find a paper. They want a plan of the rooms. But they can not find any plan. They do not have a book.

At night, they hear bad sounds. The sounds come from the walls. The big house seems to change now. It is not the same house.

Emily feels bad about the house. She thinks the house is not right. But Max is very still. Max says the house is good. He says it is just a sound.

But Emily knows what she sees. She sees the rooms change. The house is different every day. She is sure of this fact.

They decide to walk together now. They want to find the reason. They go into the black rooms. They look at every wall.

As they walk, the rooms change. A door is not there now. The house plays a game with them. They walk and walk again. They can not leave the house.

### A2
Emily visited her friend Max at his new home. The house was very big. It was also very old. The house had many dark windows. Emily looked at the large building. She went to the front door. Max opened the door with a smile. He welcomed his friend inside.

Max showed Emily around the old house. They walked through many cold rooms. Emily felt very happy to see her friend. She had not seen Max for a long time. They talked about his new life here. Max loved the big, quiet place. But Emily felt a little cold inside the house. The walls were too high and dark.

The next day, Emily got lost in the house. She wanted to find her friend Max. But she could not find the right way. She opened a small blue door. It did not lead to his room. Instead, it led to a dark, cold room. She opened another door. This door led to a long, low room. The doors led to completely different rooms now. Emily began to feel fear.

Emily found Max and told him about the doors. They decided to look for a plan of the house. "A plan will help," Max said. They looked in the room with many books. They looked inside old boxes and under tables. They looked through many old papers. But they could not find a plan in any place. "We do not have a plan," Emily said. "We are going to get lost again."

At night, they went to sleep. But they heard different sounds in the dark. The sounds came from the walls. They heard a low sound like a step. It sounded like someone was walking. But there was no one there. The house seemed to be changing. The walls seemed to move. It was a very long night for them. They could not sleep well.

In the morning, Emily felt a lot of fear. But Max did not feel fear. "Do not worry," Max said. "Old houses always make these sounds." "The walls are just moving a little." He smiled at her. He did not think there was a problem. "We are okay here," he said. But Emily did not believe him.

But Emily knew what she saw. She saw the rooms change. The house was different every day. Yesterday, the room was short. Today, the same room was very long. She saw a blue door yesterday. Today, that door was not there. Something was very wrong with this house. She could feel it in her heart.

"We must look at the house together," she said. Max agreed to go with her. They wanted to find the reason for the changes. They started to walk through the house. They held hands because they were cold. They walked into the dark. They wanted to see everything.

As they walked, the rooms changed around them. The walls moved around them. The doors opened and closed by themselves. They could not go back to the start. It was like the house was playing a game. It wanted to keep them inside. Max finally felt fear too. "Where are we?" Max asked. But the house did not answer.

### B1
Emily decided to visit her old friend, Max. He has recently bought a new home in the country. She has never seen such a large building before. The journey was long, but she was excited to see his new place. When she arrived, she stood in front of the building. The house was very big and looked extremely old. It had dark wood and large windows that seemed to watch her.

Max opened the door with a wide smile on his face. He was very happy to welcome his friend. Emily felt really glad to see him after such a long time. Max immediately showed Emily around the large house. They walked through many different areas together. He showed her the kitchen, the living room, and her bedroom. She loved the high walls and the old style of the building. Seeing her friend make a new start in this place made her feel warm inside.

The next day, Emily woke up early. She wanted to find the kitchen to make some hot tea. However, she quickly got lost in the huge house. She opened a wood door, expecting to find the steps. Instead, the door led to a completely different room. It was a dark library that she had not seen before. She tried another door, but it led to a small room. None of the doors seemed to go where they should.

Emily finally found Max in the main hall. She told him about her strange experience. They decided that they needed a map of the house. Emily and Max tried to find a map in every room. They searched inside old desks and looked through many books. They checked the boxes in the kitchen and looked in the library. But they could not find a map anywhere. It was as if no map of this house has ever existed.

When night came, the house became very quiet. But then, they started to hear strange noises. It sounded like the walls were moving. Emily did not sleep in her bed, listening closely. She heard soft steps and deep sounds behind the walls. It felt like the whole structure was changing around them. The sounds continued for hours until the morning light appeared.

At breakfast, Emily was very scared. She told Max about the terrible noises. However, Max remained completely calm. He smiled and told her not to worry. He said that old houses always make strange noises. "It is just the wood of the house settling," Max explained. He believed there was a simple, natural reason for everything. But his words did not make Emily feel any better.

But Emily knew what she saw with her own eyes. She knew that the house was different every day. Yesterday, the blue room was next to her bedroom. Today, that same door led to a long, dark hall. The windows were in different positions too. If she walked down the steps, she arrived in a new place. The changes were real, and she could not ignore them.

Finally, Emily convinced Max that something was wrong. They decided to explore the house together. They wanted to find the reason for these strange changes. "If we do not find the reason, we will get lost here," Emily said. They took a piece of paper to write down what they saw. They started at the front door and began their walk. They were ready to solve the problem of this changing house.

As they walked, the rooms shifted around them. They turned a corner and found themselves in the garden. But they had not opened any outside doors. They turned back, but the hall had disappeared. Now, there was only a strong wall behind them. It was like the house was playing a trick on them. The house was alive, and it did not want them to leave.

### B2
Emily traveled to the country to visit her friend Max at his new home. The house was big and old, standing alone on a high hill. It was surrounded by dark trees that moved slowly in the cold wind. Emily had known Max since they were children, and she had always liked this strong friendship. She felt happy to see him when he opened the heavy front door with a wide smile. Max helped her with her bags and led her inside the warm living room. A bright fire was burning in the room, which made the place feel comfortable. However, Emily could still feel a cold draft coming from the corners of the room. Max explained that he had recently bought the property and was still learning the history of the place. He was very excited to show her around his new place. He believed that this house, which was built in the last century, was perfect for him.

The next morning, Emily woke up early and decided to explore the building. She wanted to find the kitchen to make some hot coffee before Max got up. She opened her bedroom door and stepped into the long, quiet hall. She walked down the path, expecting to find the stairs at the end. Instead, she found herself in a different section of the house. The doors in this hall led to different rooms that she had not seen during her first day. She opened a white door, hoping to find the stairs, but it only led to a small, empty bedroom. She turned back to return to her room, but the hall now looked completely different. The doors that she had just passed seemed to have changed position. She realized that she was lost in the house, and a sudden wave of fear came over her. If she had brought her mobile phone, she would have called Max for help. She began to call his name with a loud voice, hoping that he would hear her.

After some time, she heard Max calling her name from a distance. He found her standing in a narrow hall, looking pale and scared. He laughed gently and said that it was easy to get lost in such a large place. Emily tried to explain that the doors had actually changed, but Max did not believe her. He thought she was simply confused because she was tired from the journey. To make her feel better, they decided to look for some information about the building. Emily and Max tried to find a map of the house that could help them understand the structure. If they had a map, they would easily find the way around the many halls. They went to the library, which was a large room filled with old books and papers. They searched through boxes on a big desk and looked behind the pictures on the walls. But they could not find one. They only found some old letters and a small key that did not seem to fit any lock.

During the day, they spent time talking and working in the garden. Max was very happy, but Emily could not stop thinking about her experience in the hall. She kept watching the doors, wondering if they were moving when she was not looking. That night, they heard strange noises that seemed to come from the walls and the floors. The sounds were louder and more frequent than they had been on the previous night. A heavy sound was heard from the floor above her room. Emily was scared, but Max was calm as he listened to the sounds. He said it was just the house settling, which was normal for such an old structure. He told her not to worry and to try to get some sleep. But Emily knew what she saw, and she was sure that the house was changing every day. If she had stayed at her own home, she would have slept well without any fear.

The next morning, Emily decided to prove her theory to Max. She led him to the living room and pointed to a window. The window, which had shown the beautiful garden yesterday, now looked out onto a solid stone wall. Max stared at the stone wall in silence, his face turning pale with surprise. He finally realized that Emily was telling the truth about the changes. A strange sound was heard from the hall, and they both turned to look. They decided to explore the house together to find the reason for the changes. They wanted to know what was causing the rooms to shift. Max took a light, and Emily carried the small key they had found in the library. They stepped into the hall, ready to face whatever mystery lay ahead of them.

As they walked, the rooms shifted around them in a silent and strange dance. It was like the house was playing a trick on them, trying to keep them lost. They would walk through a door and find themselves in a place they had never seen before. The air became colder as they went deeper into the center of the building. If they did not stay close together, they would easily be separated by the moving walls. Suddenly, they saw a small wooden door at the end of a narrow passage. The door was different from all the others, and it had a small hole for the key. Emily realized that this was the door that the small key would open. She gave the key to Max, who put it into the hole and turned it slowly. The door opened with a soft sound, revealing a hidden room in the heart of the house.

Inside the room, they found a beautiful model of the entire house. The model was made of wood and had tiny rooms that were moving by themselves. They realized that the house was built by a man who loved complex games. The movements of the house were controlled by a system of gears beneath the floor. Max found a small switch on the side of the model and turned it off. Immediately, the strange noises stopped, and the house became completely still. They walked back to the living room and saw that the window now showed the garden again. They both felt a great sense of relief as they looked out at the green trees. Max thanked Emily for her help and her strong support during the adventure. If she had not insisted on exploring, they might never have solved the mystery. They were happy to know that the house was finally safe and stable.

### C1
Emily traveled to the country to visit her friend Max at his new home, a big and old house standing alone on a high hill. It was surrounded by dark trees that moved slowly in the cold wind, their branches creaking softly. Emily had known Max since they were children, and she had always liked this strong friendship. She felt happy to see him when he opened the heavy front door with a wide smile. Max helped her with her bags and led her inside the warm living room, where a bright fire was burning.

A bright fire was burning in the room, which made the place feel comfortable and cozy. However, Emily could still feel a cold draft coming from the corners of the room, which made her shiver. Max explained that he had recently bought the property and was still learning the history of the place. He was very excited to show her around his new place, which he believed was perfect for him. He told her that the house was built in the last century, and its old charm was part of its appeal.

The next morning, Emily woke up early and decided to explore the building, searching for the kitchen to make some hot coffee. She opened her bedroom door and stepped into the long, quiet hall, where the only sound was the creaking of the old floorboards. She walked down the path, expecting to find the stairs at the end, but instead, she found herself in a different section of the house. The doors in this hall led to different rooms that she had not seen during her first day, and she felt a growing sense of unease. She realized that she was lost in the house, and a sudden wave of fear came over her.

She opened a white door, hoping to find the stairs, but it only led to a small, empty bedroom. She turned back to return to her room, but the hall now looked completely different, and she felt disoriented. The doors that she had just passed seemed to have changed position, and she was starting to feel panicked. If she had brought her mobile phone, she would have called Max for help, but it was back in her room. She began to call his name with a loud voice, hoping that he would hear her.

After some time, she heard Max calling her name from a distance, and she felt a wave of relief wash over her. He found her standing in a narrow hall, looking pale and scared, and he laughed gently. He said that it was easy to get lost in such a large place, but Emily tried to explain that the doors had actually changed. Max did not believe her, thinking she was simply confused because she was tired from the journey. To make her feel better, they decided to look for some information about the building, hoping to find a map or a plan.

They went to the library, which was a large room filled with old books and papers, and they searched through boxes on a big desk. They looked behind the pictures on the walls, but they could not find a map or any useful information. They only found some old letters and a small key that did not seem to fit any lock, but they decided to keep it anyway. During the day, they spent time talking and working in the garden, trying to enjoy the beautiful scenery. Max was very happy, but Emily could not stop thinking about her experience in the hall, and she kept watching the doors.

That night, they heard strange noises that seemed to come from the walls and the floors, and the sounds were louder and more frequent than they had been on the previous night. A heavy sound was heard from the floor above her room, and Emily was scared, but Max was calm as he listened to the sounds. He said it was just the house settling, which was normal for such an old structure, but Emily knew what she saw. She was sure that the house was changing every day, and she felt a growing sense of unease. If she had stayed at her own home, she would have slept well without any fear, but now she was trapped in this strange place.

The next morning, Emily decided to prove her theory to Max, and she led him to the living room. She pointed to a window, which had shown the beautiful garden yesterday, but now looked out onto a solid stone wall. Max stared at the stone wall in silence, his face turning pale with surprise, and he finally realized that Emily was telling the truth. A strange sound was heard from the hall, and they both turned to look, feeling a sense of trepidation. They decided to explore the house together to find the reason for the changes, and to uncover the mystery of the shifting rooms.

They walked through the halls, and the rooms shifted around them in a silent and strange dance. It was like the house was playing a trick on them, trying to keep them lost, and they had to stay close together to avoid getting separated. The air became colder as they went deeper into the center of the building, and they could feel a malevolent presence lurking in the shadows. Suddenly, they saw a small wooden door at the end of a narrow passage, and the door was different from all the others. It had a small hole for the key, and Emily realized that this was the door that the small key would open.

Emily gave the key to Max, who put it into the hole and turned it slowly, and the door opened with a soft sound. They found a hidden room in the heart of the house, and inside the room, they found a beautiful model of the entire house. The model was made of wood, and it had tiny rooms that were moving by themselves, revealing the secret of the shifting house. They realized that the house was built by a man who loved complex games, and the movements of the house were controlled by a system of gears beneath the floor. Max found a small switch on the side of the model and turned it off, and immediately, the strange noises stopped.

The house became completely still, and they walked back to the living room, feeling a great sense of relief. They saw that the window now showed the garden again, and they both felt happy to know that the house was finally safe and stable. Max thanked Emily for her help and her strong support during the adventure, and he realized that he could not have solved the mystery without her. They were happy to know that they could enjoy their time in the house without the fear of the shifting rooms. The experience had brought them closer together, and they appreciated the value of their friendship.

As they looked out at the green trees, they felt grateful for the adventure they had shared, and they knew that they would never forget it. The house, which had once seemed so ominous, now felt like a warm and welcoming home, and they were eager to explore its secrets. They decided to learn more about the history of the house, and to uncover the stories of the people who had lived there before. The house, which had once been a source of fear, had become a source of fascination, and they were excited to see what other secrets it held. As they stood together, looking out at the garden, they knew that their friendship would last a lifetime.

The house, which had once seemed so full of mysteries, was now a place of wonder and discovery, and they were grateful to have shared the experience. They knew that they would always treasure the memory of their adventure, and the bond that they had formed as they worked together to solve the mystery. As they stood there, they felt a sense of peace and contentment, knowing that they had faced their fears and overcome them. The house, which had once been a source of fear, had become a symbol of their friendship, and the adventures that they would share in the future. And as they walked away from the window, they knew that they would always have a special place in their hearts for the shadow house.

The experience had taught them that even in the darkest of places, there is always the possibility for discovery and growth. And as they looked to the future, they knew that they would face any challenge that came their way, as long as they had each other. The house, which had once seemed so ominous, had become a reminder of the power of friendship and the importance of facing their fears. They knew that they would always be there for each other, no matter what lay ahead, and that their bond would only continue to grow stronger. And as they walked away from the house, they knew that they would always cherish the memory of their time in the shadow house.

## st-0017 — The Lost Hotel Room
Kaynak: ozgun

### A1
Emily gets to an old house. Much water falls from the night. The house is very big and old. The man at the table does not speak. He gives Emily a small thing. It is to open the door. The room is four hundred and eight. The paper says this room is not there. The man points to a black way. Emily walks into the black night.

She finds the door at the end. The room is very old and small. It has no way to see out. A small light has many old things. It is not a good place. The phone has no service.

She wants to leave the room now. But the way is very black now. She closes the door. The house has no sound. She is the only person there. She feels life in the body.

She hears a sound from next door. It is room four hundred and nine. Something big moves across the room. Emily puts the head against the side. A bad sound says the name. It tells Emily to come.

Emily is not good now. She is feeling very bad. The sound next door makes a sound. She takes the things in the hand. She opens the door. Black things move on the sides. They look like bad hands.

She runs through the house. The house is now very large. The doors have no numbers. She sees many different signs. She hears people call the name. The house is changing.

She sees a light at the end. She is in the main room again. Everything has many old things. She looks into a thing. She sees the face. A high figure is behind the woman. The figure has no face. It holds the thing for the door.

Emily tries to call out for help. But she has no sound. The figure speaks to Emily now. She is a part of the house. She can never leave. She is in a bad place.

The front door is not open. Emily walks back to the room. She is now a black thing. She is part of the history. She tells the new people. She speaks through the sides.

### A2
Water fell from the air when Emily arrived at the hotel. It fell very much. The front room had no sound. The manager did not look up from his table. He gave her a hard key. The paper said room four hundred and eight did not exist. The man pointed toward a long way. She walked down the cold way. The numbers were not in order. She wished she was at another hotel.

She found her door at the end of the way. The top was low there. She opened the door with the key. The room was dark and old. She dropped her things and felt fear. The door closed behind her. There were no windows. The air was very cold. She could see the air from her face. Her phone had no service.

She decided to go back to the front table. She opened the door, but the way was dark. Someone turned off the lights. She closed her door again. It would not open from the outside. She hoped the morning would bring a reason. The hotel had no sound. She did not hear other people. She only heard her own heart.

Late at night, a sound started next door. It was room four hundred and nine. Something hard moved on the floor. Then she heard a sound like water. Emily put her head against the wall. The manager said she was alone. Suddenly, the sound stopped. A voice said her name through the wall. It was a low voice. It told her to come.

She felt fear and fell on the bed. The voice began to laugh. It was a bad sound. She had to leave. She took her things and ran. Her hands moved with fear. She opened the door. Dark areas moved on the walls. They were like cold hands.

She ran to find a way out. The hotel grew larger around her. She went into the building. The doors had no numbers. She saw red signs that were not normal. Voices were not happy. The hotel lived. It wanted to keep her. She should have looked at the paper more.

Suddenly, she saw a light. She ran toward it. She reached the front room. The tables were not clean. The windows had boards over them. No one was there. The paper was not there. She looked at her face in a thing on the wall. She saw a dark space behind her. A large figure with no face stood there. It held her key.

She tried to call out. Nothing was there. She felt cold air. The voice said she was part of the hotel now. She would never find the door. She was lost. The night would never end.

The door was closed. The key was lost for all time. Emily walked back to room four hundred and eight. This was her new home. Her body became a small dark area. She was a part of the history. When the next person arrives, they will hear her. She will talk through the walls. She will tell them to leave now.

### B1
The rain was falling hard when Emily finally arrived at the dark hotel. She had booked this old place online. The lobby was completely silent. The manager did not look up. He slowly handed her a metal key. According to the paper map on the desk, room four hundred and eight did not exist. She asked for directions. The silent man pointed toward a long, narrow passage that went into the dark. She walked down the cold hall. The numbers on the doors did not follow any clear order. If she leaves now, she will find a safe hotel in the city.

Finally, she found her door at the end of a dark corner. The roof there was very low. The heavy key turned with a loud sound. It opened a dark bedroom that had not been used for fifty years. She dropped her bags on the floor. She felt immediate worry as the door shut behind her. There were no windows in the room. The only light came from a small light covered in dust. The air was cold, and she could see her own breath. She checked her phone. There was absolutely no service in this quiet room. She felt a growing fear.

She decided to go back to the front desk. But when she opened the door, the passage was completely dark. Someone had turned off all the lights in the hall. She closed her door and locked it quickly. She hoped that the morning would bring a simple explanation. She lay on the hard bed and listened to the deep silence. The building felt completely empty. If there are other guests, she will hear them. Instead, she only heard the beat of her own heart. It seemed to grow louder with every minute.

Late at night, a strange noise came from room four hundred and nine. It sounded like someone was pulling a heavy box across the floor. Then, she heard a slow, wet sliding sound. Emily put her ear against the wall to listen. The manager had told her that she was alone in the hotel. Suddenly, the noise stopped. She heard a soft whisper directly through the wall. It was a cold voice that repeated her name. The voice called her to come closer to the door.

Afraid, she backed away until she fell on the bed. The voice next door began to laugh. It was a terrible sound that did not seem human. She knew she must leave before she lost her mind. She grabbed her bags and ran to the door. Her hands shook so much that she almost dropped the key. She opened the door. The dark passage was no longer empty. Long, thin shadows were moving along the walls toward her.

She ran in the opposite direction to find a way down. However, every turn took her deeper into the giant hotel. The building seemed to grow larger around her. The doors she passed had no numbers. Some doors were painted with strange, red signs. She heard soft crying and whispering behind almost every door. It felt as if the hotel itself was alive. It was changing its shape to keep her lost inside. She has seen many bad places, but this is the worst.

Suddenly, she saw a bright light at the end of a long hall. She ran toward it with all her strength. She emerged into the main lobby. But the lobby was completely different now. The tables and chairs were covered in thick dust. The windows were blocked by wood. The desk was empty. The paper map was gone. A mirror stood in its place. Emily looked into the mirror. She did not see her own face. Instead, she saw a dark space behind her. A tall figure with no face was standing there. It was holding her metal room key.

She tried to shout, but no sound came from her mouth. She turned around, but nothing was there. Yet, she could feel its cold breath on her skin. The voice whispered that she was now part of the hotel. She belonged here just like everyone else. She realized with terrible pain that she was trapped. She will never find the door to the outside world. If she does not escape, she will stay here forever.

The front door of the hotel was locked from the outside. The key was lost forever. Emily walked slowly back to room four hundred and eight. This was her new permanent home. As she walked, her steps became silent. Her body began to fade into a gray shadow. She was no longer a normal guest. She has become a part of the dark hotel. When the next guest arrives, they will hear a soft voice. It will be Emily, warning them to run away.

### B2
The rain was falling heavily when Emily finally arrived at the old, dark hotel that she had booked online. The lobby was completely silent, and the manager, who did not even look up, slowly handed her a metal key. According to the paper map which was on the desk, her room, number four hundred and eight, did not exist. When she asked for directions, the silent man simply pointed toward a long, narrow passage that led into the darkness. She walked down the cold hall, but the numbers on the wooden doors did not seem to follow any clear order. If she had known how strange this place was, she would have chosen a different hotel in the city center.

Finally, she found her door at the very end of a dark corner where the roof was extremely low. The heavy key turned with a loud sound, revealing a dark bedroom that had not been used for decades. She dropped her bags on the floor, feeling an immediate sense of worry as the door shut tightly behind her. There were no windows in the room, and the only light came from a small electric light that was covered in dust. The air was so dry and cold that she could see her own breath as she sat on the bed. After checking her phone, she realized with growing panic that there was absolutely no service in this deep, quiet room.

She decided to go back to the front desk, but when she opened the door, the passage was completely dark. It seemed that all the lights in the hall had been turned off by someone while she was inside. She closed her door again and locked it, hoping that the morning would bring some simple explanation for everything. As she lay on the hard bed, she listened to the deep silence of the hotel, which felt completely empty. If there were other guests in this building, she would hear their voices. Instead, the only sound was the constant beat of her own heart, which seemed to grow louder with every passing minute.

Late at night, a strange noise started to come from the room next door, which was number four hundred and nine. It sounded like someone was dragging a heavy wooden box across the floor, followed by a slow, wet sliding sound. Emily stood up and put her ear against the wall, trying to determine what was happening on the other side. She knew that the room next door should be empty, because the manager had told her she was alone here. Suddenly, the dragging noise stopped, and a soft whisper seemed to speak directly into her ear from through the wall. It was a cold voice that repeated her name over and over again, calling her to come closer to the door.

Frightened, she backed away from the wall until her legs hit the edge of the bed and she fell down. The voice next door began to laugh, a low and horrible sound that did not belong to any human being. She realized that if she stayed in this room any longer, she would lose her mind completely from the fear. She grabbed her bags and ran to the door, her hands shaking so much that she almost dropped the key. When she finally opened it, she saw that the dark passage was no longer empty as it had been before. Long, thin shadows were moving along the walls, sliding toward her room like cold fingers reaching out of the dark.

She ran in the opposite direction, hoping to find the stairs or a lift that could take her down. However, every passage she took only led her deeper into the giant hotel, which seemed to grow larger around her. The doors she passed had no numbers now, and some of them were painted with strange, red symbols. She could hear soft crying and whispering coming from behind almost every single wooden door that she ran past. It felt as if the hotel itself was alive, changing its shape to keep her lost inside its walls forever. If she had checked the map more carefully at the front desk, she might have seen the warning signs.

Suddenly, she saw a bright light at the end of a long hall and ran toward it with all her strength. She emerged into the main hall, but it was completely different from the place she had entered earlier that evening. The furniture was covered in thick dust, and the beautiful windows were now blocked by solid wooden boards. The front desk was empty, and the paper map that she had left there was gone, replaced by a mirror. When Emily looked into the mirror, she did not see her own face, but rather a dark space behind her. In that dark space, a tall figure with no face was standing right behind her, holding her metal room key.

She tried to scream, but no sound came from her throat as she turned around to face the dark figure. There was nothing there, yet she could feel its cold breath on her skin and hear its soft voice whispering. The voice told her that she was now part of the hotel, just like everyone else who had checked in. She realized with terrible pain that she would never find the door that led back to the outside world again. If she had never entered this building, she would still be safe at home in her own warm bed. Instead, she was trapped in this lost hotel, where the night never ended and there was no way out.

The front door of the hotel was now locked from the outside, and the key was lost forever. Emily walked slowly back down the long passage toward room four hundred and eight, her new permanent home. As she walked, her own steps became silent, and her body began to fade into a thin, gray shadow. She was no longer a guest who was visiting the hotel, but rather a part of its dark history. When the next guest arrives on a rainy night, they will hear a soft voice whispering through the walls. It will be Emily, warning them to run away before they also become lost in the hotel forever.
