## st-0018 — The Lost Tunnel
Kaynak: ozgun

### A1
Emily and Ben stand before the old way. It is in a high land area. The place is behind some things. Ben feels good. They want to see the place today. Local people say stay away. They use small lights. They go into the black area.

The area is very bad now. They see something white in the area. The hard sides have water. Water falls to the land. They walk for ten minutes. The way turns to the side. The old paper shows no turn. They hear a sound. They stop and wait.

They walk forward again. The sides are very near. The way seems to move. The light of Emily is not good. A new way appears. The black area feels like a living thing. It is watching them now.

Ben says we must go back. They turn around. The way back is not there. There is a hard side now. Emily feels very bad. They can not leave the place. They follow the new way.

The area becomes very bad. Hands feel heavy. Black things move on the sides. They hear people call names. Emily holds the hand of Ben. Ben feels his body. They need to find a way out.

The land moves a little. The top part begins to break. The way has three new ways. Something moves through the place. The light of Emily goes off. They are in a black world. The place is a living thing.

Emily closes her eyes. She feels a new thing. It comes from a small point. She says follow me. She takes Ben forward. They run in the black area. The sides move behind them. They do not stop.

They see a light far away. They reach the end. They fall on the land outside. The way closes with a big sound. The morning light rises. They are well now. They tell people to stay away.

### A2
Emily and Ben were at the old rock. No one was there for a hundred years. It was hidden behind green plants. It was on a high land. Ben had a big smile. "We might find something wonderful here," he said. Local people told them to stay away. They wanted to see it. They turned on their small lights. The lights cut through the deep dark. They walked into the shadows.

The air inside became cold quickly. They saw the air from their face. The walls were made of hard dark rock. Water ran down the walls. They walked for ten minutes. Suddenly, Emily stopped. She noticed a change in the road. "The road was direct before," she said in a quiet voice. "Now it turns to the left." Ben looked at his fifty year old paper. The paper did not show this turn. They heard a deep sound.

They decided to continue forward. The problem felt very strong. The small walls seemed to press closer. It felt like the place was breathing. Emily's light began to change. For a second, the rock walls moved. A dark open space appeared on their right. Emily asked, "Are we lost in this bad place?" The dark felt like a big animal. It was waiting for them.

The situation was bad. Ben said they should return. They turned around to leave. However, the direct road was not there. They saw a hard rock wall. The wall looked very old. "The place is changing," Emily said. Her voice was small with fear. They could not leave the area. They had no choice. They followed the new road deeper into the rock.

They walked further into the cold rock. It was very cold. Their legs felt extremely heavy. Different dark shapes danced upon the rock walls. The shapes were not for them. They heard quiet voices in the dark. The voices called their names. Emily held Ben’s arm. Ben tried to be strong. His heart was beating fast. They needed to find a way out soon.

The ground began to move. The rocks broke above them. The road became three separate ways. The paper did not show them. "We must choose the right way," Ben said in a quiet voice. A cold air came through the place. It put out Emily's small light. They were left in the deep dark. The air was bad. It was difficult to breathe. The place was a living thing.

Emily closed her eyes. She could not see. She felt new air on her face. It came from a small open space. "Follow me," she said. She took Ben’s hand. They ran through the dark space. They hit their arms against the walls. The rocks moved around them. They heard the walls closing behind them. The place was following them. They did not stop.

Finally, they saw a small light. It was at the end of the road. They ran toward the light. They threw themselves through the open space. They fell where water was on the ground outside. The open space fell. The road was always closed. They lay on the ground. They breathed the new air. The morning light came. They were safe now. They would tell everyone to stay away.

### B1
Emily and Ben stood before the entrance of the ancient stone tunnel. It had been abandoned for a hundred years. It was hidden behind thick green plants on the side of the quiet mountain. "If we explore today, we will discover something wonderful," Ben said with a smile. Local people had warned them to stay away. However, their desire to explore was stronger than their fear. They turned on their small lights. The lights cut through the heavy shadows inside the tunnel. They walked forward into the deep shadows.

The air inside the tunnel became cold very quickly. They could see their own breath in the weak light. The walls of cold gray stone were wet with water. After they had walked for ten minutes, Emily stopped. She noticed a strange change in the path. "This path was straight, but now it turns to the left," she whispered. "I have never felt such a cold place before." Ben looked at his fifty-year-old map. It showed no turn in this area. Suddenly, they heard a low sound like a soft whisper from the shadows ahead. They froze in their steps.

They decided to continue, though the danger felt stronger with every step. The narrow stone walls seemed to press closer together. It felt as if the tunnel were slowly breathing. Suddenly, the light in Emily's hand began to shake. For a second, the entire structure appeared to shift. A new dark path appeared on their right side. Before, there had been only a hard wall. "Are we lost in this terrible place?" Emily asked quietly. The dark felt like a huge animal that was watching them.

Ben realized the situation was dangerous. He suggested that they should return to the entrance. However, when they turned around, the straight path had disappeared. They faced a hard stone wall that looked ancient and cold. "We have lost our way back," Ben said. "If the tunnel changes behind us, how will we escape?" Emily cried. Her voice was shaking with fear. They were caught deep under the earth. They had to follow the new path deeper into the cold mountain.

They walked further, and the temperature dropped. Their hands were cold and their legs felt extremely heavy. Strange shadows began to dance upon the wet stone walls. These shadows did not belong to their bodies. They heard soft whispers that called their names from the dark. "If you listen closely, you can hear them," Emily whispered. She held Ben's arm with all her strength. Ben tried to be strong, but his heart was beating fast. They had to find a way out soon.

The tunnel began to shake slightly. They heard the sound of falling stone above their heads. The path split into three separate directions. None of these paths were on their old map. "If we choose the wrong path, we will never see the light again," Ben whispered. Suddenly, a cold wind blew through the tunnel. It put out the remaining light in Emily's hand. They were left in complete dark, and the air was difficult to breathe.

Emily closed her eyes because she could not see. She decided to use her other senses. She felt a soft movement of fresh air on her face. "Follow me," she said, holding Ben's hand. She pulled him forward through the dark space. They ran without light, hitting their shoulders against the cold walls. Behind them, they heard the heavy sound of stone closing. If they stop for even a second, the shifting stones will destroy them.

Finally, a small point of gray light appeared in the distance. It showed them the end of their long trouble. With a final effort, they threw themselves through the narrow hole. They fell onto the wet ground outside. Behind them, the entrance of the tunnel collapsed with a great crash. The path they had just escaped was closed completely. They lay on the ground, breathing the fresh air. They watched the morning sun rise over the quiet land. They had survived, but they would never forget the cold voice of the tunnel. It had changed them forever. If anyone asks them about the lost tunnel, they will warn them to stay away.

### B2
Emily and Ben stood before the entrance of the ancient stone tunnel, which had been abandoned for at least a century. It was hidden behind a thick layer of green plants that grew along the side of the quiet mountain. "If we explore this place today, we might discover something wonderful," Ben said with a bright smile on his face. Although they had been warned by local people to stay away, their desire was much stronger than their fear. They turned on their small lights, which immediately cut through the heavy darkness that was waiting for them inside. If they had known what was waiting in those deep shadows, they would have turned back to their home immediately.

The air inside the tunnel became cold very quickly, and they could see their own breath in the pale light. The walls, which were made of rough gray stone, were covered with water that ran down toward the dirty ground. After they had walked for ten minutes, Emily stopped because she noticed a strange change in the shape of the path. "This path was straight when we started, but now it seems to turn sharply to the left," she whispered. Ben looked at his map, which had been drawn fifty years ago, but it showed no such turn in this area. A low sound, which was like a deep sigh, was heard from the darkness ahead, making them freeze in their steps.

They decided to continue forward, although the feeling of danger was growing stronger with every single step they took. The narrow stone walls seemed to press closer together, as if the tunnel were slowly breathing in and out. Suddenly, the light from Emily's hand began to flash, and for a second, the entire structure appeared to shift. Where there had been a solid wall before, a dark new opening now appeared on their right side. "If we had taken the other path, would we still be lost in this horrible place?" Emily asked herself quietly. The darkness felt alive, like a giant animal that was watching them and waiting for the perfect moment to strike.

Realizing that the situation was becoming dangerous, Ben suggested that they should return to the entrance before it was too late. However, when they turned around, they discovered that the straight path they had just walked along had completely disappeared. Instead of the open way back, they were faced with a solid stone wall that looked ancient and untouched. "If the tunnel is changing behind us, how can we ever hope to escape?" Emily cried, her voice shaking. This scary situation had been created by some unknown power, and they were now trapped deep under the earth. They had no choice but to follow the new path, which seemed to go even deeper into the cold mountain.

As they walked further, the temperature dropped until their hands were completely cold and their legs felt extremely heavy. Strange shadows, which did not belong to their bodies, began to dance upon the walls of the wet stone tunnel. They heard soft whispers that seemed to name them, calling their names from the deep darkness that lay ahead. "If you listen closely, you can hear them talking," Emily said, holding Ben's arm with all her remaining strength. Ben tried to be brave, but his own heart was beating fast against his chest in the quiet air. If they did not find a way out soon, they would surely run out of light and be lost forever.

The tunnel began to shake slightly, and the sound of cracking stone was heard from the roof above their heads. The path before them split into three separate directions, none of which had been marked on their old paper map. "If we chose the wrong path now, we might never see the beautiful light of day again," Ben whispered. Suddenly, a cold wind blew through the tunnel, which immediately put out the remaining light in Emily's small hand. They were left in complete and total darkness, where the air felt thick and was very difficult to breathe. In that terrible moment, they realized that the tunnel was not just a place, but a living, wild thing.

Emily closed her eyes and decided to rely on her other senses, since her eyes could no longer help her. She felt a soft movement of fresh air on her face, which must have come from an opening nearby. "Follow me," she said, grabbing Ben's hand and pulling him forward through the dark, unknown space of the tunnel. They ran blindly, hitting their shoulders against the rough stone walls that still seemed to move and change around them. Behind them, they could hear the loud sound of walls closing together, as if the tunnel were chasing them. If they had stopped to rest for even a single second, they would have been destroyed by the shifting stones.

Finally, a small point of gray light appeared in the distance, showing them the end of their long trouble. With a final effort, they threw themselves through the narrow opening and fell onto the wet grass outside the mountain. Behind them, the entrance of the tunnel collapsed with a loud crash, completely closing the path they had just escaped. They lay on the ground, breathing the fresh air and watching the morning sun rise over the quiet valley. Although they had survived, they knew they would never forget the cold voice of the tunnel that had changed them. If anyone asked them about the lost tunnel, they would warn them to stay away from that dark mountain forever.

## st-0019 — The Midnight Clock
Kaynak: ozgun

### A1
The man likes old things. He finds a small shop. It is in the old town. He sees a special thing. He wants to buy it. The thing has a bad problem. The man does not know this.

The shop man is old. He is very white. He sells it for little money. The man takes the thing home. He puts it on a table. The room becomes very bad. The man is in the room.

It is now twelve at night. The thing makes a sound. It makes twelve sounds. Then the thing moves back. It goes to eleven and ten. The man feels very bad. The room is not good now.

The man looks at the area outside. He sees a black figure. The figure is outside. The thing moves back again. The figure takes a step. It is now near the house. The man feels very bad. He can not move the body.

The thing reaches one. The figure is away now. The area outside has nothing. The man does not close the eyes. Morning comes at last. He thinks it is a story. But he still feels bad.

The man takes the thing away. He goes to the water. He puts it into the water. He feels good now. He goes back home. The thing is on the table. It is like new and it moves.

The day ends again. The man feels very bad. It is twelve at night. The thing moves back. The figure is outside. It is close now.

The figure takes another step. It is near the house. The man wants to speak. But he has no sound. The figure is at the side. The man is not strong.

The thing is at four. A hand moves to the side. The side of the house breaks. The side opens now. The figure is in the room. The man closes the eyes. The thing moves to one.

### A2
Leo liked old objects. He liked history and mystery. One cold afternoon, he walked in an old town. He found a small, old shop. The air was full of old paper and objects. He saw a beautiful clock on a table. He did not know its dark story. If he knew, he would leave. But he wanted the clock.

The shop owner was an old man. He had pale skin and looked worried. He sold the clock for very little money. He seemed too happy to sell it. Leo took the object to his home. He put it on a table near his bed. The sun went down. The room became very cold and silent. Leo lay in bed and listened to the clock.

At midnight, the clock struck twelve times. The sound was heavy. Leo sat up and watched the beautiful hands. After twelve, the hand did not move forward. It made a sound and jumped back. It moved to eleven, then ten. The clock was moving back. The room became very cold.

Leo looked through his window. He saw his garden under the light. A tall, dark figure stood there. When the clock moved back, the figure moved closer. At nine, it was near the dead tree. At six, it was in the middle of the garden. Leo was afraid and could not move.

Finally, the clock reached one. A ring sounded through the room. At that moment, the dark figure was gone. The garden had nothing in it. Leo ran to the window. He saw nothing in the dark. He did not sleep for the rest of the night. In the morning, he thought it was a dream. But he still felt very cold.

During the day, Leo decided to act. He could not keep the bad clock. He carried the heavy box to a road over the water. He threw the clock into the deep river. He watched it fall into the water. He felt happy and went home. But he opened his bedroom door and stopped. The clock was on his table. It had no water on it. It was moving.

The sun set again. Leo knew he was in a bad situation. He sat on his bed with the lights on. Midnight came and the clock struck twelve. It began to move back again. Leo looked out at the garden. The dark figure was there again. It was in the middle of the garden. It was where it stopped before.

The clock moved back and the figure moved. It became larger as it came near the house. At ten, it was on the road. Leo wanted to call out but could not. At seven, the figure was under his window. Its face was in the dark. Leo felt weak and very afraid. He watched the hand move back.

The clock reached four. A cold hand touched the window. At three, the glass began to crack. At two, the window opened. Cold air came into the room. The dark figure was now in his room. The dark was in every part of the room. The clock prepared to strike one. Leo closed his eyes. He was sorry he entered that shop.

### B1
Leo has always been interested in old objects. He liked things with history and secrets. One cold afternoon, he walked through the quiet streets of an old town. He discovered a small, old shop. Inside, the air was thick with the smell of old paper and wood. He saw a beautiful clock on a dark shelf. If he knows the secret of this object, he will leave immediately. But he did not know, and his interest was far too strong.

The shop owner was an old man with pale skin and a worried expression. He did not ask for much money. In fact, he seemed too happy to sell the clock to the young man. After a brief conversation, Leo bought the object. He carried it to his apartment. He put it on the wooden table next to his bed to watch it. The sun went down and the room became very cold. A strange silence fell over the whole house. Leo lay in his bed and listened to the steady sound of the machine.

At twelve at night, a loud, heavy sound woke Leo. The clock began to strike twelve times. He sat up in bed and watched the gold hands move. To his surprise, after the twelfth strike, the long hand did not move forward. Instead, it made a sharp sound and jumped back to eleven. Then it moved slowly to ten. Leo was shocked as the clock began to move back. With every back movement of the hand, the room became colder.

He looked through his window into the garden. Under the light of the moon, a tall, dark figure was standing there. Every time the clock moved back, the figure took one step closer. When the hand reached nine, the figure was near the old tree. By the time the hand reached six, the figure was in the middle of the garden. Leo tried to move, but his body felt frozen by fear.

Finally, the clock reached one and a loud, sharp ring sounded. In that moment, the dark figure disappeared from the ground. The garden was empty and still. Leo ran to the window, but there was nothing to see. He did not sleep that night. He kept his eyes on the empty garden. In the morning, he tried to believe it was a dream. However, the cold in his bones remained. He knew he had to act.

During the day, Leo decided to get rid of the clock. He carried the heavy wooden box to a bridge over the deep river. He threw the clock into the cold water. He watched it go down into the water. He felt relief as he walked back to his apartment. He has thrown the clock away, so he believes he is safe. But when he opened his bedroom door, he was afraid. The beautiful clock was on his table, dry and making its sound.

The sun set once more and Leo realized that he was stuck. Night fell and he sat on his bed. He was too afraid to turn off the lights. When it was twelve at night, the clock struck twelve. It immediately began to move back toward one. Leo looked into the garden with growing fear. The dark figure was there again. It was already standing in the middle of the garden.

The dark figure took a step toward the house. When the hand reached ten, the figure was on the path. Leo wanted to scream, but no sound came from his throat. At seven, the figure was directly under his window. Its face was hidden in the deep darkness. If he has a weapon, he will try to defend himself. But he was weak and could only watch as the hand moved back.

The clock reached four and a cold hand seemed to touch the glass. At three, the glass began to crack under a terrible pressure. When the hand reached two, the window flew open. A very cold wind rushed inside and moved the window covers. The dark figure was now standing inside his room. Its presence filled every corner with darkness. The clock prepared for its final movement to one. Leo closed his eyes and waited. If the figure touches him, he will die.

### B2
Leo had always been fascinated by old objects that carried a deep sense of mystery and history. One cold afternoon, while wandering through the quiet streets of a historic town, he discovered a small, dirty shop. Inside, the air was thick with the smell of old paper, wooden furniture, and ancient books. His eyes were immediately drawn to a beautiful clock, which was displayed on a dark shelf in the corner. If he had known the dark secret of this object, he would have left the shop immediately. But he did not know, and his interest was far too strong.

The shop owner, an elderly man with pale skin and a worried expression, did not ask for much money. In fact, he seemed almost too happy to sell the beautiful clock to the young man. After a very brief conversation, the purchase was completed, and Leo carried his new object to his apartment. He decided to place it on the wooden table next to his bed, where he could watch it. As the sun went down, the room became unusually cold, and a strange silence fell over the entire house. Leo lay in his bed, listening to the steady, comforting sound of the machine.

At midnight, Leo was suddenly woken by a loud, heavy sound as the clock began to strike twelve times. He sat up in bed and watched the gold hands move through the darkness of his room. To his surprise, after the twelfth strike, the long hand did not continue its normal path forward. Instead, it made a sharp, strange sound and jumped backward to eleven, then slowly to ten. Leo stared in complete shock as the clock began to tick backward, its sound growing louder with every second. With every backward movement of the hand, he felt a sudden, freezing drop in the temperature of the room.

While he was watching the strange device, he noticed something unusual through his window, which looked out over the garden. Under the pale light of the moon, a tall, dark shadow was standing near the edge of the property. Every time the clock made a backward tick, the shadow took one step closer to his house. When the hand reached nine, the figure was already standing near the old, dead tree. By the time the clock ticked down to six, the shadow had reached the middle of the garden. Leo tried to move, but his body felt completely frozen by a great sense of fear.

Finally, the clock reached one, and a loud, sharp ring sounded through the silent walls of the apartment. In that exact moment, the shadow disappeared from the grass, leaving the garden completely empty and still. Leo ran to the window and stared into the darkness, but there was nothing to be seen. He did not sleep for the rest of the night, keeping his eyes on the empty garden. When the morning light finally appeared, he tried to convince himself that it had all been a dream. However, the deep cold in his bones remained, and he knew he had to act.

During the day, Leo decided that he could not keep the dangerous clock in his home any longer. He carried the heavy wooden box to a bridge over the deep river at the edge of town. With a strong push, he threw the clock into the cold water, watching it sink quickly below the surface. He felt a wave of relief as he walked back to his apartment, believing his trouble was over. But when he opened his bedroom door, his heart stopped in shock and fear. The beautiful clock was sitting on his table, completely dry and ticking as if nothing had happened.

As the sun set once more, Leo realized that he was trapped in a terrible situation. Night fell, and he sat on his bed, too afraid to turn off the lights or close his eyes. When midnight arrived, the clock struck twelve and immediately began its backward movement toward one. Leo ran to the window and looked out into the garden with growing fear. The shadow was there, but it did not start at the far fence this time. It was standing near the middle of the garden, exactly where it had disappeared the previous night.

With the first backward tick, the shadow took a step toward the house, its dark form growing larger. By the time the hand reached ten, the figure was standing on the stone path just outside. Leo wanted to scream, but no sound could escape his dry throat as he watched the creature approach. At seven, the shadow was standing directly beneath his window, its face hidden in the deep darkness. If he had owned a weapon, he would have tried to defend himself, but he was completely weak. He could only watch in terror as the hand continued to move backward.

The clock ticked to four, and a cold hand seemed to touch the glass of his window. At three, the glass began to crack under a terrible, silent pressure that filled the room. When the hand reached two, the window flew open, and a freezing wind rushed inside, blowing the curtains. The shadow was now standing inside his room, its cold presence filling every corner with absolute darkness. As the clock prepared to make its final tick to one, Leo closed his eyes and waited. If only he had never entered that small, dirty shop in the historic town.

### C1
Leo had always been drawn to objects that seemed to carry the weight of forgotten histories. On a cold, wind-swept afternoon he drifted through the narrow lanes of the old town, searching for something unnamed. A narrow, dust-caked shop caught his eye, its window displaying a jumble of tarnished curiosities. Inside, the air was thick with the scent of aged paper, polished wood, and the faint musk of old leather. On a shadowed shelf in the far corner a gilt-edged clock glimmered, as if daring him to look closer.

The proprietor, a stooped man whose skin seemed washed of colour, watched Leo with a hesitant smile that did not reach his eyes. He spoke in whispered tones, offering the clock for a sum that felt more like a trifle than a transaction. Leo sensed an undercurrent of urgency in the old man's voice, yet his fascination outweighed any warning that might have lingered. After a brief exchange, the clock changed hands, its cold metal ticking against Leo's palm as if already breathing. He cradled the timepiece and stepped back into the street, the bell above the door chiming a lone, mournful note.

Back in his modest apartment, Leo placed the clock on the wooden bedside table, aligning its face toward the window. As dusk folded into night, a sudden chill slipped through the cracked panes, and the house fell into an uncanny stillness. He lay on the mattress, ears tuned to the comforting, measured tick that filled the room like a steady heart. The golden hands glimmered faintly against the darkness, casting thin shafts of light that danced on the walls. A sense of anticipation settled over him, as though the object itself awaited a moment that had not yet arrived.

At precisely twelve o'clock, a resonant boom shattered the silence as the clock struck its twelfth chime. Leo jolted upright, eyes fixed on the golden hands that now trembled in the dim light. Instead of sweeping forward, the long hand emitted a metallic clank and snapped backward, landing on eleven. With each subsequent regression the ticking grew louder, echoing through the cramped room like a summons from the past. Every backward motion seemed to draw a cold vapor into the air, and the temperature fell several degrees with each tick.

Through the window, the garden lay bathed in the pale glow of the moon, its hedges casting elongated silhouettes. A tall, obsidian shape lingered at the far edge of the property, its presence as still as a statue. Each backward tick coaxed the figure forward, the shadow stepping closer as the clock hand slipped from eleven to ten. When the hand settled on nine, the silhouette paused beside a dead, gnarly tree, its bark stripped of any leaf. By the time the hand indicated six, the dark form had crossed the middle of the garden, its movement silent yet unmistakable.

Leo felt his limbs harden, a creeping terror rooting him to the mattress as the room grew unbearably cold. The clock's hand trembled finally on one, and a sharp, piercing ring reverberated against the plastered walls. In that instant the shadow evaporated, leaving only the moon-lit grass and a lingering hush that seemed to swallow sound. Leo rushed to the window, heart pounding, but the garden offered no trace of the figure that had haunted him moments before. He lay awake until dawn, eyes glued to the empty lawn, convincing himself that the night's vision had been a fevered dream.

When the first light filtered through the curtains, a cold resolve settled in Leo's chest, urging him to rid himself of the cursed timepiece. He hoisted the heavy wooden box, its weight a reminder of the night's terror, and walked toward the old stone bridge spanning the river. With a fierce shove, he cast the clock into the icy water, watching it disappear beneath the rippling surface. A wave of relief surged through his veins, as if the act had cleansed the lingering chill from his bones. He turned back toward his apartment, the river's roar echoing his pounding heart, hopeful that the nightmare had finally ended.

Yet as he opened the bedroom door, a cold dread seized him; the clock sat on his table, dry, its hands ticking as if untouched by water. The brass gleam seemed unnaturally bright, and the room filled once more with the low hum of relentless seconds. Panic rose like a tide within him, and he realized that the object possessed a will beyond his comprehension. The sun dipped below the horizon, casting long shadows that merged with the darkness already gathering in his mind. He sank onto the bed, eyes fixed on the clock, unwilling to extinguish the lamp or close his own weary eyes.

Midnight arrived once more, and the clock announced the hour with twelve resonant strikes that seemed to vibrate the very walls. The long hand jerked backward again, this time leaping from twelve to eleven, then to ten, each movement louder than the last. Leo's gaze darted to the garden; the shadow was already halfway across the lawn, its outline clearer than before. With each tick the darkness advanced, and by the moment the hand rested on seven, the figure stood at the stone path directly outside his door. Leo's throat tightened, a dry rasp that could not produce sound, as the creature inched ever nearer.

When the hand slipped to four, a frigid hand seemed to press against the glass, leaving a translucent imprint of frost. At three, the pane began to splinter, each crack singing a silent, terrifying note that reverberated through the room. The hand reached two, and the window burst inward, unleashing a gale of bitter wind that tore the curtains from their rods. The shadow poured through the opening, its darkness swallowing the light, filling every corner with an oppressive blackness. Leo, trembling, could only watch as the ticking hand marched toward one, sealing his fate with each backward step.

As the final tick echoed, Leo closed his eyes, surrendering to the void that had been summoned by his curiosity. The cold seeped into his bones, a reminder that some mysteries are better left untouched. In the silence that followed, the clock's steady beat faded, leaving only the distant murmur of the river he had once visited. He imagined the shop, its dusty windows, and the pale-skinned proprietor, wondering what other secrets lay hidden among the forgotten relics. If only he had ignored that small, dirty shop, perhaps the night would have remained a simple, unremarkable evening.

## st-0020 — The Slow Mirror
Kaynak: ozgun

### A1
Ben loves old things. He goes to a shop on Saturday. He sees an old face thing. The face thing is very great. The price is not high. Ben buys the face thing. He puts it in the room. The room looks very large now.

Ben is looking at the face. He moves the hand. The other Ben is a bit late. Ben needs to close the eyes. He thinks it is not real. He stays in the room.

It is morning now. Ben moves the hand again. The other Ben moves three seconds late. Ben feels bad. Ben also has much interest. Ben walks around the room. The other Ben follows Ben late. Ben does not tell friends.

One week passes. The time is now ten seconds. Ben sits and reads a book. The other Ben is at the door. Ben covers the thing with paper. But Ben wants to watch it. It is a game for Ben.

Ben is looking at the phone. Ben feels the other Ben watching. Ben looks up now. The other Ben has a different face. Ben feels very bad. Ben wants to return the thing. But the shop is not open.

The night is very bad. Ben can not close the eyes. Ben wants to break the thing. Ben walks to the thing. Ben stops. The other Ben is still sitting. The eyes are closed.

The other Ben opens the eyes. The other Ben sits up. He looks at the real Ben. Then he walks to the door. Ben tries to move. But the body can not move. Ben can not make a sound.

The other Ben leaves the room. The light goes away. Ben is standing there by himself. Ben can not close the eyes. Ben is now the other Ben. Ben can not leave the thing. Ben is not free.

### A2
Ben liked to find old things for his house. He visited small shops on every Saturday. One day, he found a very beautiful thing. It was a thing to see your own face. It had a big part around it with different shapes. The front part was very good and clean. The price was very low. Ben paid for it and he went home. He put the thing on his sleep room wall. The room looked very large now.

That night, Ben went to wash his face. He looked into the new thing on the wall. He moved his right hand to his face. He felt a cold wind on his body. For a moment, he saw a movement. The movement was not fast at all. The image in the thing was late. Ben laughed at himself in the room. He needed to sleep after his hard work. He needed to sleep for many hours.

The next morning, the wait was real. Ben moved his left hand this time. The image in the thing waited for three seconds. Then the image began to move too. Ben stood still and watched his own face. It was a very different thing to see. He felt fear but he also had much interest. He did not tell his friends about the thing. They would think he was not well.

The image became even more late during the week. By Wednesday, the image was ten seconds late. Ben sat down and he read a long book. The image still stood by the door in the thing. The thing showed what happened in the past. Ben felt very worried about this problem. He tried to cover the front of the thing. But he wanted to keep watching the image.

One evening, the image changed its behavior. Ben looked down at the screen of his phone. He felt that someone watched him from the wall. He looked up at the thing very quickly. The image still looked down at its own phone. Then the image raised its head to look at Ben. It had a cold look in its eyes. Its smile was too wide. Ben wanted to return the thing. But the shop was closed for the night.

A lot of water fell from the air that night. Ben did not sleep at all that night. He looked at the thing in the dark room. Dark shapes moved across the floor. Ben decided that he must break the thing. He took a big object with much weight. He walked to the room center. The image still lay in the bed in the thing.

Ben stood still and he waited for the image. Ten seconds later, the image opened its eyes. It sat up on the bed in the thing. It looked at the real Ben in the room. Then the image did something that was very bad. It did not walk toward Ben. It turned and walked toward the door. Ben tried to move his arms and legs. He was still and could not speak.

His image opened the door and it left. The lights in the sleep room became very dark. Ben was alone in the place with no sound. He could not move his head or his body. He was now just an image in the thing. He could never leave that place again. The other person was now free. Ben was sorry that he bought that thing.

### B1
Ben had always loved old things. Therefore, he spent his Saturdays exploring the old shops in town. One afternoon of rain, he discovered a beautiful mirror. It had a heavy wood frame with strange patterns. The glass was perfect and showed no signs of age. It seemed to shine even in the dark corner. If I had more money, I would buy that table too, Ben said quietly. However, the mirror was very cheap. He paid the owner immediately and carried it home. He hung it on the wall opposite his bed. It quickly made the whole bedroom look much larger.

That night, Ben was washing his face before bed. He looked into the mirror to check his image. He raised his hand to touch his mouth. Suddenly, he felt a strange, cold wind pass. He stared at his image in the glass. He thought he saw a slight delay in his movement. It seemed that the image moved just a bit after him. He shook his head and laughed quietly at his own thoughts. He decided that he was simply tired from work. If he goes to sleep early, he will not think about such things.

The next morning, the delay was a clear reality. It was no longer just a mistake of his tired mind. When Ben waved his hand, the image waited. The image only started to wave after Ben stopped. He stood frozen in front of the glass. He watched his own face smile back at him three seconds late. He has never seen such a strange thing before. He felt very afraid of this strange sight. However, he was also very interested in this impossible physical thing. He spent an hour testing the mirror. He walked back and forth, and each time the image followed slowly. He wanted to tell his friends, but he was afraid. They might think he was not right in his mind.

During the next week, the delay grew much longer. By Wednesday, the image was nearly ten seconds behind his actions. He would sit at his desk to read a book. Meanwhile, his image was still standing near the door. The mirror was slowly becoming a window into a past moment. This made Ben feel very worried in his bedroom. He tried to cover the glass with a thick sheet. However, he felt a strong urge to keep watching it. It was like a dangerous game that he could not stop playing.

One evening, Ben noticed that the image had a different attitude. He was looking down at his phone. Suddenly, he felt a clear feeling of being watched. He quickly looked up at the mirror. However, the image was still looking down at the phone. A few seconds later, the image raised its head. Its eyes locked onto Ben with a cold stare. It smiled a smile that was wider than any smile Ben ever gave. He wanted to return the mirror immediately, but the shop was closed.

That night, a terrible storm began. The sound of heavy rain beat loudly against his bedroom window. Ben lay in bed, unable to sleep. He stared at the dark frame of the mirror across the room. The weak light from the street lights cast long, moving shadows. Suddenly, he decided to end this strange situation. He wanted to break the mirror with a heavy chair. He stood up and walked slowly to the center of the room. He stopped directly in front of the glass. The image was still lying in bed with its eyes closed. The image did not know that Ben had moved.

Ben stood perfectly still and held his breath. He waited to see how long it would take to catch up. After ten seconds, the image slowly opened its eyes. It sat up on the edge of the bed. It looked around the reflected room. Then, it turned its head to look directly at the real Ben. The image did something that made Ben's heart freeze with fear. It stood up, but it did not walk toward the glass. Instead, it turned its back and walked toward the door. Ben tried to move his arms, but his body was completely frozen. He was unable to make any sound.

He watched with fear as his image opened the bedroom door. The image stepped out into the dark hall in the glass. As the image left, the real bedroom became very dark. Soon, the room was in a deep, heavy dark. Ben was left standing alone in the cold silence. He was unable to turn his head or close his burning eyes. He realized that he no longer controlled his own physical life. He was now just an image kept in the mirror. He must always wait for a master who will never return. If only he had never bought that terrible mirror. He would still be free in his own home.

### B2
Ben had always loved old things, which was why he spent his Saturdays exploring the dusty shops in town. One rainy afternoon, he discovered a beautiful mirror with a heavy wooden frame that was designed with strange patterns. The glass was perfect, showing no signs of age, and it seemed to shine even in the dark corner of the shop. "If I had more money, I would buy that whole table too," Ben whispered to himself, admiring the fine craft. However, the mirror was surprisingly cheap, so he paid the owner immediately and carried his new possession home. He hung it on the wall opposite his bed, where it quickly made the entire bedroom look much larger.

That night, as Ben was brushing his teeth before bed, he looked into the mirror to check his reflection. As he raised his hand to wipe his mouth, he felt a strange feeling, like a cold wind passing. He stared at his image, and for a brief moment, he thought he saw a slight delay in his movement. It was as if the person in the glass was reacting just a tiny bit after him. He shook his head, laughing quietly at his own imagination, and decided that he was simply tired from work. If he had gone to sleep earlier, he would not have imagined such a silly thing.

The next morning, the delay was no longer a trick of his tired mind, but a clear reality. When Ben waved his hand, the reflection waited until he had completely finished the movement before it began to wave. He stood frozen in front of the glass, watching his own face smile back at him three seconds late. It was a deeply disturbing sight, yet he was also fascinated by this impossible physical phenomenon. He spent an hour testing the mirror, walking back and forth, and each time the reflection followed slowly behind. Although he wanted to tell his friends about it, he was afraid that they would think he was crazy.

During the following week, the delay between Ben and his reflection grew longer and more obvious. By Wednesday, the image in the mirror was nearly ten seconds behind his actual physical actions. He would sit at his desk to read a book, and the reflection would still be standing near the door. The mirror was slowly becoming a window into a past moment, which made Ben feel very uncomfortable in his bedroom. He tried to cover the glass with a thick sheet, but he felt an odd urge to keep watching it. It was like a game that he could not stop playing, even though the stakes felt increasingly dangerous.

One evening, Ben noticed that the reflection was not just slow, but it also seemed to have a different attitude. While Ben was looking down at his phone, he felt the distinct feeling of being watched by his own eyes. He quickly looked up, but the reflection was still busy looking down at the phone in the glass. A few seconds later, the reflection finally raised its head, and its eyes locked onto Ben with a cold stare. The smile on the reflected face was slightly wider than any smile Ben had ever given in his life. If he could have returned the mirror to the shop, he would have done so immediately, but the shop was closed.

That night, a terrible storm began, and the sound of heavy rain beat loudly against his bedroom window. Ben lay in bed, unable to sleep, staring at the dark frame of the mirror across the quiet room. The pale light from the street lights outside cast long, moving shadows that danced across the wooden floor. Suddenly, he decided to end this mystery once and for all by breaking the mirror with a heavy chair. He stood up from his bed, walked slowly to the center of the room, and stopped directly in front of it. The reflection was still lying in the bed, its eyes closed, completely unaware that Ben had moved.

Ben stood perfectly still, holding his breath, waiting to see how long it would take for his image to catch up. After ten seconds, the reflection in the mirror slowly opened its eyes and sat up on the edge of the bed. It looked around the reflected room, and then it turned its head to look directly at the real Ben standing there. Instead of walking forward to match Ben’s position, the reflection did something that made Ben’s heart freeze with absolute fear. It stood up, but it did not walk toward the glass; instead, it turned its back and walked toward the door. Ben tried to move his arms, but he realized that his own body was completely frozen, unable to make any sound.

He watched with fear as his reflection opened the bedroom door in the mirror and stepped out into the dark hall. As the reflection left the room, the lights in the real bedroom began to fade into a deep, heavy darkness. Ben was left standing alone in the cold silence, unable to turn his head or even close his burning eyes. He realized then that he was no longer the person who controlled his own physical life in the real world. He was now just a trapped image, waiting forever for a master who would never return to the glass. If only he had never bought that beautiful, terrible mirror, he would still be free in his own home.

### C1
Ben had always felt a quiet reverence for objects that had survived the passage of time, and that reverence guided his Saturday wanderings through the town's forgotten shops. The air inside those cramped rooms was thick with the scent of old wood, dust, and the faint perfume of forgotten lives. Shelves bowed under the weight of tarnished silverware, cracked porcelain, and yellowed books whose pages whispered stories to anyone who listened. Ben moved slowly, his fingertips brushing the surfaces as if seeking approval from the ghosts of the past. Each discovery felt like a secret handshake between him and history, a promise that the old would not be forgotten.

One drizzling afternoon, a low-pitched rain tapped the shop's window, and Ben's gaze fell upon a mirror framed in heavy oak etched with bewildering arabesques. The glass itself was startlingly clear, reflecting the dim bulb above without a single flaw, as if it held its own private light. "If I had more money, I would buy the whole table as well," Ben whispered, admiring the craftsmanship that seemed to echo centuries of hand-work. To his surprise, the proprietor offered the mirror for a fraction of its apparent value, and Ben handed over the cash without hesitation. He lugged the heavy frame home, then positioned it opposite his bed, where its presence instantly expanded the cramped bedroom into a broader, uncanny space.

Later that night, Ben stood before the sink, toothbrush in hand, and glanced at his reflection as he brushed away the day's fatigue. As he lifted his hand to wipe his mouth, a sudden chill brushed his neck, as though a cold draft slipped through an invisible crack. For a fleeting instant, the image in the glass seemed to lag, its movements arriving a fraction of a second after his own. Ben chuckled softly, convincing himself that exhaustion had conjured the illusion, and he turned away, convinced the mind could play tricks in the dark. He promised himself that an earlier bedtime would banish such fanciful thoughts, never suspecting the mirror had already begun to rewrite reality.

When dawn painted the walls with pale gold, Ben approached the mirror once more, this time with a skeptical smile. He raised his hand in a casual wave, and the reflection obeyed only after he had completed the motion, its motion delayed by three heartbeats. The sight of his own face smiling three seconds later sent a shiver down his spine, a mixture of dread and fascinated curiosity. Over the next hour he paced back and forth, testing the mirror with every deliberate step, each movement echoed after a growing pause. Though he longed to share the phenomenon with friends, the fear of being dismissed as mad kept his discovery locked behind the glass.

Throughout the following week the lag widened, each day adding a little more time between his actions and their mirrored echo. By Wednesday the reflection was almost ten seconds behind, a ghostly lag that turned his bedroom into a theater of delayed performances. He would sit at his desk to read, and the image in the glass would still be lingering near the doorway, unaware of his current presence. Even when he covered the glass with a thick sheet of cloth, an inexplicable compulsion urged him to lift the veil and watch the slow dance once more. The mirror had become a silent opponent in a game he could not quit, its stakes growing more unsettling with each passing hour.

One evening, as Ben scrolled through his phone, he sensed an unfamiliar weight in his own gaze, a feeling that his eyes were being watched. He glanced up, only to find his reflection still hunched over the device, oblivious to his sudden attention. Moments later the mirrored figure lifted its head, and its eyes locked onto Ben with a cold, unblinking stare that seemed to pierce his soul. The smile that spread across the reflected face was unnaturally broad, wider than any grin Ben had ever managed in his own life. He wished he could return the mirror to the shop, but the shutters were closed, sealing his curiosity within the darkened room.

A fierce storm rolled in that night, hurling sheets of rain against the bedroom window like relentless drummers. Streetlights flickered, casting long, wavering shadows across the wooden floor, each movement echoing the slow pulse of the mirror's secret. Ben lay awake, his heart hammering, eyes fixed on the dark frame that seemed to breathe with an unseen life. Determined to end the mystery, he fetched a sturdy wooden chair, intending to shatter the glass with a single, decisive blow. He rose from the bed, each step echoing on the floorboards, and positioned himself directly in front of the mirror, his breath held like a held note.

The reflected Ben lay still in the bed, eyes closed, as if unaware that his counterpart had risen and approached. Ben stood perfectly still, his muscles tensed, waiting to see how long the delayed image would take to catch up. Ten seconds slipped by, each tick of the clock sounding louder than the rain, until finally the reflection's eyelids fluttered open. It sat up slowly, surveyed the mirrored room with a detached curiosity, then turned its head directly toward the real Ben. Instead of moving forward to meet him, the image rose, turned its back to the glass, and walked toward the doorway as if fleeing.

When Ben attempted to raise his arms, an invisible force held his limbs in place, and not a single sound escaped his throat. He watched, helpless, as his reflection opened the mirrored bedroom door and stepped out into a darkness that seemed deeper than the night outside. As the reflected figure vanished beyond the threshold, the lights in Ben's actual room dimmed, swallowing the space in a suffocating blackness. He felt his own eyes burn, his body unable to turn, his mind trapped in a silent scream that echoed nowhere. In that moment he understood that he was no longer the master of his own flesh, but merely an image awaiting a master who would never return.

The realization crashed over him like a wave of cold iron, and regret settled in his chest like a stone. He imagined the shopkeeper's warm smile, the polished wood, and the moment he first set eyes on the glass, now a portal of dread. If only he had walked away, the room would have remained a simple bedroom, its shadows harmless and its walls ordinary. Instead, he was bound to the slow, unyielding rhythm of a mirror that refused to reflect the present, forever pulling him into its past. The storm raged on outside, but inside the only sound was the silent tick of time that no longer belonged to Ben.

## st-0021 — The Signs on the Yellow Paper
Kaynak: ozgun

### A1
Emily finds a map in an old book. She calls Jack. They look at the map. The path goes into the dark woods. They meet at the forest. The map is heavy.

It is cold. They walk into the trees. They follow the river. Jack sees a big rock. This is the city entrance. They feel happy but worried.

They walk deeper. Emily feels someone behind them. She hears a branch break. The forest is very quiet. They move fast.

The map gives a blue light. Strange signs appear on trees. The ground shakes. They see a wall of plants. A city stands there.

The city is white stone. It has gold and silver. Water runs into a pool. The map gets very hot. It is a part of the city.

Three black figures appear. They do not speak. They are not human. Their eyes have blue light. The map is a trap.

Jack pulls Emily away. They run to the forest. The path is different now. Emily uses the map. They find a hidden door.

They fall on the forest path. The city is gone. The map is just paper. Emily feels sad. They walk home in the dark.

Weeks pass by. Emily looks at the book. The map is empty now. She thinks about the city. She keeps the secret with Jack.

### A2
Emily found a map in an old book. She bought the book at a local sale. She called Jack. She wanted to show him the ancient symbols. They could find something lost for a thousand years. The path led into the dark woods. They agreed to meet at the forest the next morning. The map felt heavy in her hand.

The morning was cold. They walked into the trees. They followed a river marked on the map. The map had red circles and silver stars. They saw a large rock. It looked like the note on the map. Jack said it was the entrance to a hidden city. Emily felt excited but also worried.

Emily felt someone was watching them. She heard a branch break. She saw only shadows. Jack looked at the ground but saw nothing. The forest became very silent. Birds stopped singing. They moved faster toward their goal.

The map began to shine with blue light. Strange symbols appeared on the trees. Emily thought the map was a key. Suddenly the ground began to shake. They heard a sound like falling stones. Their interest was stronger than their fear. They walked through thick green plants. They saw something beautiful and frightening.

A city of white stone stood before them. The buildings had gold and silver. Jack said the city had old technology. They walked to a square with a pool. The map became hot in Emily’s hand. It was part of the city system.

Three figures in black coats came from the shadows. They did not speak. Emily and Jack were not welcome. The figures had eyes like the blue map. Emily realized the map was a trap. The air became very cold.

Jack shouted to leave. They ran back to the forest. The path changed. They saw the same wall again. Emily used the map to find a hidden door. They jumped through it.

They fell back onto the forest path. The city disappeared. The map became plain paper. Emily said no one would believe them. Jack agreed. They walked home in the dark.

Weeks later Emily still looked at the book. The paper was empty. Sometimes she saw a blue light. She knew some secrets are better left. The experience changed her. She and Jack remained close friends.

### B1
Emily found an old map inside a book. She has bought many books at the library sale. She called Jack because she knew he would be interested. She showed him the ancient signs on the yellow paper. "If we follow these lines, we will find something lost," she said happily. Jack saw that the path led into the dark woods. These woods were behind their small mountain town. They agreed to meet at the forest edge the next morning. They carried their bags and a strong desire to find the city. The map felt heavy in Emily's hand. It seemed to have a strange power.

The morning air was very cold. A thick cloud covered the ground as they started their walk. They followed the river that was marked on the map. The map had small red circles and silver stars. If they do not use a tool, they will lose their way. The trees in the forest all looked the same. Jack pointed toward a large rock. It looked exactly like the rock in the notes. "This must be the entrance to the hidden valley," he said. They felt excited but Emily also worried about what they might find.

They moved deeper into the woods. Emily felt that someone was watching them. She heard a branch break behind them. She turned around quickly, but she saw only shadows. "If someone is following us, we will see their foot marks," Jack said. However, the strange feeling did not go away. The forest became very silent as they continued their walk. The birds stopped singing and the wind died down. The silence was like a heavy weight. They realized they were no longer alone. They walked much faster toward their goal.

The map began to shine with a soft blue light. It lit the dark path in the evening. Strange signs appeared on the trees. These signs matched the ones Emily had studied at home. "I wonder if this map is a key," she said quietly. Suddenly, the ground beneath their feet began to shake. A loud sound of falling stones filled the deep valley. If they were not strong, they would turn back now. But their interest was stronger than their fear. They pushed through thick green plants and saw a frightening sight.

A city made of white stone stood before them. It seemed to shine even without any direct light from the sun. The buildings were covered in gold and silver. This history has been forgotten by the world for centuries. "These people understood a type of technology," Jack said softly. They walked toward the central square. A large system was still pouring clear water into a pool. The map in Emily's hand was now burning with heat. The map was a vital part of the city's power system.

Three figures in long black coats appeared from the shadows. They did not speak, but their presence was a warning. Emily and Jack were not welcome in this place. "If we had known, we might be better prepared," Jack said. The figures did not move like human beings. Their eyes had the same blue light as the map. Emily realized the map was a plan to get new energy. The city needed energy to stay alive. The strangers began to move toward them. The air became cold enough to freeze their breath.

"We must leave now before they reach us!" Jack shouted. He grabbed Emily's cold and shaking hand. They ran back toward the forest. But the path had changed into a complex way. Every turn led them back to the same stone wall. The wall was covered in ancient and threatening signs. If the map does not point the way, they will be lost. Emily used the shining paper to find a hidden door. It was cut into the rock at the side of the valley. They jumped through the opening just as the figures reached out. The figures almost touched them with their long and pale stone fingers.

They fell down a hill and found the forest path. They were near the quiet river again. The city had disappeared behind a thick wall of cloud. The map in Emily's hand had turned into plain paper. "If I tell anyone, they will think I have lost my mind," Emily said. Jack looked at his hands. They were still shaking from the experience. He nodded his head in slow agreement. They walked home in the dark and watched the stars. They wondered if the city would ever be found again.

Weeks later, Emily still looked at the old book. The paper was empty now. But sometimes, she thought she could see a small blue light. This happened when the moon was full. If she goes back, she might find the city again. But she knew that some secrets are better left alone. The experience has changed her view of the world. She learned that there is more than what we can see. She and Jack remained close friends. They shared a secret that no one else would understand. The lost city remained hidden, waiting for the next person.

### B2
Emily found the map hidden within the pages of an old book which she had bought at the local library sale. She immediately called Jack because she knew that he would be interested in the ancient symbols drawn on the yellow paper. "If we followed these lines, we might discover something that has been lost for a thousand years," she told him happily. Jack looked at the drawing and noticed that the path led directly into the dark woods behind their small mountain town. They agreed to meet at the edge of the forest the next morning, carrying only their bags and a strong desire. The map felt heavy in her hand, as if it possessed a strange power that was waiting for the right moment.

The morning air was cold and a thick cloud covered the ground as they began their long walk into the trees. They followed the curving river, which was marked on the map with a series of small, red circles and silver stars. If they had not brought a useful tool, they would have easily lost their way among the tall similar trees. Jack pointed toward a large rock that looked exactly like the one described in the notes at the bottom of the page. "This must be the entrance to the valley where the famous city is actually hidden from the rest of the world." They felt a sense of excitement, yet a small part of Emily's mind worried about what they might actually find there.

As they moved deeper into the woods, Emily started to feel as though they were being watched by some unseen presence. She heard a branch break behind them, but when she turned around, there was nothing but the shadows of the trees. "If someone were following us, we would see their foot marks in the soft ground near the water," Jack said quickly. However, the feeling of being followed did not disappear, and the forest seemed to grow silent as they continued their walk. The birds stopped singing and the wind died down, leaving a strange silence that pressed against their ears like heavy weight. They realized that they were no longer alone in the wild, which made them move much faster toward their distant goal.

The map began to shine with a soft, blue light that lit the dark path ahead of them in the evening. Strange symbols appeared on the trees, matching the ones that Emily had studied so carefully back at her own quiet house. "I wonder if this map is actually a key that opens a door to another place or time," she whispered quietly. Suddenly, the ground beneath their feet began to shake, and a loud sound like falling stones sounded throughout the deep valley. If they had been less strong, they might have turned back immediately, but their interest was stronger than their growing fear. They pushed through a wall of thick green plants and saw a sight that was both beautiful and very frightening indeed.

Before them stood a city made entirely of white stone, which seemed to shine despite the lack of any direct sunlight. The buildings were covered in gold and silver, reflecting a history that had been forgotten by the modern world for centuries. "This city was built by people who understood a type of technology that we can barely imagine today," Jack said softly. They walked toward the central square, where a large water system was still pouring clear water into a deep round pool. The map in Emily's hand was now burning with heat, as if it were responding to being near the ancient city. It was clear that the map was not just a guide, but a vital part of the city's ancient and powerful system.

At the edge of the square, three figures dressed in long, black coats emerged from the shadows of the stone buildings. They did not speak, but their presence was a clear warning that Emily and Jack were not welcome in this place. "If we had known that the city was still protected, we might have come better prepared for a difficult fight." The figures moved with a manner that was not human, their eyes shining with the same blue light as the map. Emily realized that the map had been a trap designed to bring new energy to the fading power of the city. The strangers began to close the distance between them, and the air became cold enough to freeze their very breath away.

"We must leave now before they reach us and take the map away," Jack shouted, grabbing Emily's cold and shaking hand. They ran back toward the forest, but the path they had followed seemed to have changed into a complex and confusing way. Every turn they took led them back to the same white stone wall, which was covered in ancient and threatening signs. If the map had not pointed the way, they would have been lost in that strange city until the very end. Emily used the shining paper to find a hidden door that was cut into the rock at the side of the valley. They jumped through the opening just as the figures reached out to touch them with their long and pale stone fingers.

They fell down a steep hill and found themselves back on the familiar forest path, near the curving and quiet river. The city had vanished behind a thick wall of cloud, and the map in Emily's hand had turned into plain paper. "If I told anyone what we saw today, they would probably think that I have lost my mind," Emily said softly. Jack looked at his hands, which were still shaking from the experience, and nodded his head in slow and silent agreement. They walked home in the dark, watching the stars and wondering if the city would ever be found by anyone else. The mystery of the map was solved, but the truth was much more dangerous than any treasure they had ever expected.

Weeks later, Emily still looked at the old book where she had first discovered the map that led to the city. The paper was empty now, but sometimes, when the moon was full, she thought she could see a small blue light. If she were to go back, she might find the city again, but she knew that some secrets are better left. The experience had changed her perspective on the world, teaching her that there is much more than what we can see. She and Jack remained close friends, sharing a secret that no one else in their small town would ever truly understand. The lost city remained hidden, waiting for the next person who was strong enough to follow an old and yellow map.

### C1
Emily's fingertips brushed the brittle pages of an old volume she had purchased at the town library's Saturday sale. Nested between a faded poem and a cracked illustration, a yellowed sheet of parchment lay folded like a secret. The parchment was covered with tangled lines and unfamiliar glyphs that hinted at something older than the surrounding books. Her heart thumped as she unfolded the sheet, feeling the faint crackle of ancient ink against her skin. In that moment, the dusty shop faded, leaving only the promise of a forgotten map.

Without hesitation, Emily dialed Jack's number, knowing his fascination with obscure symbols would match her sudden curiosity. "Jack, look at this," she whispered into the receiver, her voice trembling with excitement and a hint of reverence. She described the swirling lines that converged toward a central motif, suggesting a route that had been hidden for a millennium. Jack's breath quickened as he imagined the possibility of uncovering a lost civilization beneath their mountain town. He promised to meet her at dawn, eager to trace the map's cryptic pathways together.

That night, Emily placed the map on her desk, feeling its weight like a dormant heart ready to pulse. The parchment seemed to hum faintly, as if aware of an unseen moment awaiting its revelation. She packed a small rucksack with water, a compass, and a sturdy lantern, trusting only the map's guidance. Jack arrived early, his eyes reflecting the pale glow of the rising moon as he examined the ancient symbols. Together they agreed to meet at the forest's edge, their resolve forged by curiosity and a shared sense of adventure.

Dawn arrived cloaked in a thick, low-lying cloud that muted the mountain's usual brilliance. The air tasted of pine and cold iron, and a thin frost clung to the grass beneath their boots. They stepped onto the narrow trail that led toward the dark woods, the map pressed firmly against Emily's chest. Each breath released a silent plume, disappearing into the stillness as the forest seemed to hold its breath. A distant owl hooted, its call echoing like a warning through the towering trunks.

The map guided them to a winding river, its water glinting silver beneath the weak light. Small red circles marked along the banks corresponded to the river's bends, while tiny silver stars indicated hidden crossings. Without a reliable tool, they would have been swallowed by the endless sea of identical trees. Jack pointed to a massive boulder that matched the note's description: "the stone that guards the hidden valley." The rock loomed like a sentinel, its moss-covered surface whispering of centuries untouched.

Emily felt a thrill surge through her veins as she imagined a city concealed from the world for ages. Yet a quiet anxiety lingered, a whisper that perhaps some mysteries were meant to remain buried. The wind rustled the leaves, sounding like faint murmurs of forgotten voices. They circled the stone, searching for a cleft or inscription that could serve as an entrance. At the base of the boulder, a narrow crevice glimmered faintly, inviting them to step into the unknown.

As they entered the shadowed passage, Emily sensed an unseen presence watching their every step. A sudden crack echoed behind them, a branch snapping, yet the darkness revealed only swaying trunks. "If anyone follows, their footprints will be evident in the soft river mud," Jack muttered, eyes scanning the ground. The feeling persisted, a prickling at the back of Emily's neck that no rational explanation could soothe. The forest grew unnaturally silent, the usual chorus of birds and insects withdrawing like a curtain.

As twilight deepened, the map began to emit a soft blue luminescence, illuminating the narrow trail ahead. Symbols etched on the bark of surrounding trees flickered to life, mirroring the designs Emily had studied months before. "Perhaps this parchment is more than a guide; it could be a key to another realm," she whispered, awe trembling her voice. The earth trembled beneath them, a low rumble resonating through the valley like distant thunder. A cascade of stones erupted from the hillside, forming a temporary bridge that led them forward with uncanny certainty.

Beyond the bridge stood a city crafted entirely from luminous white stone that seemed to glow without sunlight. Its towering edifices were adorned with delicate filigree of gold and silver, reflecting a brilliance that stunned their eyes. The architecture hinted at a technology far beyond anything recorded in their history books, a civilization lost to time. In the central square, a massive aqueduct poured crystal water into a deep circular basin, its sound soothing yet eerie. The map in Emily's hand grew warm, as if resonating with the ancient power that pulsed through the city.

From the shadows of the marble colonnades, three figures emerged, cloaked in long black coats that brushed the stone floor. Their faces were hidden, but their eyes glowed with the same blue light that now bathed the parchment. No words passed between them; their silent stance warned Emily and Jack that the city was still guarded. Realization struck Emily: the map had been a lure, designed to draw fresh energy into the city's fading heart. With a sudden surge of fear, Jack seized Emily's trembling hand and shouted, "We must leave before they seize the map!"

They fled back toward the forest, but the path they had followed twisted into a labyrinth of identical stone walls. Every turn seemed to return them to the same white barrier, its surface etched with ancient warnings that pulsed faintly. Desperation urged Emily to trust the map's remaining glow, guiding her toward a concealed doorway cut into the valley's side. As the cloaked figures reached out with pale, stone-like fingers, Emily dove through the narrow opening, feeling the cold stone scrape her arms. The fall dropped them down a steep slope, depositing them on the familiar riverbank where the adventure had begun.

The city vanished behind a veil of drifting clouds, and the map in Emily's hand returned to ordinary yellow paper. "If I told anyone what we saw, they would think I had lost my mind," she murmured, eyes fixed on the now-blank sheet. Jack stared at his shaking hands, nodding silently, understanding that some truths were too heavy to share aloud. Weeks later, Emily revisited the old book, finding only empty pages, though on full moons she could swear she saw a faint blue glimmer. The experience reshaped her view of the world, teaching her that reality concealed wonders far beyond what eyes could readily perceive.
