## st-0023 — The Lost City
Kaynak: ozgun

### A1
Emily and Jack walk in the forest. The path is very hard to see. Jack sees a small way forward. They see old marks on the floor. The marks lead to a new place. They follow the marks together.

They see a city of white stone. The city has gold towers. The streets are very quiet. Flowers grow in the gardens. The city seems very old.

They find a temple in the center. A map is on a stone table. Jack knows the map is a key. The map has hard puzzles.

They move three pieces of glass. Light hits the temple door. The heavy door opens. They see pictures on the walls. The people have great knowledge.

They find a blue stone inside. The stone has the city power. The stone is weak now. The walls shake. They must find a code.

Emily uses symbols from the wall. She presses the symbols. The light returns. The city is safe.

They find many books. Jack wants to take a book. Emily wants to keep the secret. They leave the books.

The map shows a new way home. They walk through the city. They feel very lucky. The sun goes down.

They reach the car. They look back. The city is gone. They drive home.

### A2
Emily and Jack walked through the deep green forest. The air was cool. They saw a small path between two big rocks. They found old marks on the ground. The marks pointed to a place not on any map. They decided to follow the path.

They walked into a valley. They saw a city of white stone and gold. It looked beautiful under the sun. Tall towers reached the sky. The city was very quiet. Beautiful flowers grew in the gardens.

In the center, they found a small temple. They saw a small box on a stone table. Inside was a map on a piece of metal. Jack knew the map was a key. They studied the map for a long time. It had puzzles about the stars.

The first puzzle used light from the sun. Emily used the map to find the right angles. She moved three pieces of glass. A loud sound came from the stone doors. The doors opened. They saw pictures of the history of the people.

They found a room with a soft blue light. A large stone gave power to the city. The stone was weak. The walls began to shake. They had to act fast. They used the pictures to find a code.

The code used symbols for water, fire, wind, and earth. Emily pressed the symbols. Suddenly, the stone began to glow. The ground stopped moving. The blue light filled the streets. The city was safe again.

They found a room with many books. Jack wanted to take one book. Emily said the city must be a secret. They did not want the government to find it. They decided to leave the books. They kept their memory of the adventure.

The map showed a new path away. They walked back to the main road. The city was very peaceful. They promised to return one day. The sun was red and orange.

They reached their car. The mountain was covered in clouds. The city was gone. Jack wondered if it was real. Emily held the small stone box. They drove home in silence.

### B1
Emily and Jack walked through the deep green forest during their long summer holiday trip. The forest was high on the mountain side. The air was cool and fresh, but the path was hard to find. The thick trees made the way very dark. Suddenly, Jack noticed a narrow opening between two massive rocks. It seemed to lead toward a hidden valley below the peak. They looked closely at the ground and found strange stone marks. These marks were very old. They pointed directly toward a place that was not on any modern map. They decided to follow the trail. They wondered what secret could be hidden in such a wild region.

As they moved deeper into the valley, the trees began to clear. They saw a sight that was absolutely beautiful. A whole city made of white stone and gold stood before them. It shone brightly under the afternoon sun. The buildings were unlike anything they had ever seen in history books. The city had tall towers that reached the sky. No noise came from the streets. The entire place had been empty for many hundreds of years. However, the gardens were full of colorful flowers. This suggested that some force was still keeping the plants alive. They felt wonder as they stepped onto the main street. The street was covered with smooth stones of a silver color.

In the center of the city, they found a small temple. Powerful ancient trees stood in a circle around it. There was a stone table in front of the temple. A small box was left open on the table. It looked as if someone had just walked away. Inside the box lay a map on a thin piece of metal. Jack realized that the map was not just a guide. It was a key that they needed to unlock the temple. If they have arrived early, they will find the door. Thick clouds might cover the entrance later. They studied the map and noticed several complex puzzles about the stars.

The first puzzle required them to move three large pieces of glass. They had to make the light from the sun shine into the temple. Emily was very good at solving difficult problems. She used the map to calculate the correct angles. The light hit the final glass and a loud sound moved through the square. The heavy stone doors began to open. They entered a large hall with pictures on the walls. The pictures described the history of the ancient people. These people had used a special kind of energy from the earth. It was clear that the city was a place of great knowledge. It was protected from the world by mystery and peace.

Deep inside the temple, they discovered a large room. It was filled with a soft blue light from a large stone. This stone was the source of power for the entire city. However, the stone was currently becoming weak. The walls began to shake. According to the map, the city would only remain steady with a code. They had to enter the code into the base machine. Jack and Emily knew they had to act quickly. The city was starting to disappear around them like a dream. If the energy fails, the city will be lost forever. They would be trapped within a place that no longer existed.

The code used several marks that represented the elements of nature. These marks were for water, fire, wind, and the earth. Emily carefully pressed the marks in the correct order. She followed a poem she had read on the wall. For a moment, nothing happened. The room was so silent that they could hear their own hearts. Suddenly, the stone began to glow with a strong light. The ground stopped moving as the energy returned. The city was saved. The blue light spread through the streets and the walls shone with life. They felt a great sense of success. They have preserved a piece of history that is truly unique.

They explored the rest of the temple. They found a room with many books in a strange language. Jack suggested that they should take one book back with them. He wanted to show it to a professor at the university. However, Emily felt that the city should remain a secret. The presence of too many people might destroy it. They realized that the city had chosen them to be its guards. This role would require great responsibility. If the world knows about this place, the government might take the energy. They decided to leave the books where they were. They only took the memory of their great adventure with them.

Before they left, the map began to change. It showed a new path away from the city. This path was much easier than the first one. It led them back toward the main road. They walked slowly through the city one last time. They admired the great detail of the buildings and the peaceful gardens. It was a place where time seemed to stand still. They felt lucky to have experienced such an event. They promised to return if the city called to them again. The sun was beginning to set in the sky. The clouds were red and orange as they reached the valley edge.

When they finally reached their car, the mountain was covered in thick clouds. The hidden valley disappeared from their view. They looked back, but there was no sign of the white towers. The golden roofs they had seen before were gone. Jack wondered if the city would still be there tomorrow. Maybe it only appeared to those looking for adventure. Emily smiled and held the small stone box as a sign. It was the secret they now shared. They drove home in silence and thought about the ancient people. The experience had changed them both. It gave them a new way to look at the world.

### B2
Emily and Jack walked through the deep green forest that covered the high mountain side during their long summer holiday trip. The air was cool and fresh, but the path they followed had become increasingly difficult to see among the thick trees. Suddenly, Jack noticed a narrow opening between two massive rocks that appeared to lead toward a hidden valley below the peak. If they had not been looking closely at the ground, they would have missed the strange stone marks on the floor. These marks were very old, yet they seemed to point directly toward a place that was not on any modern map. They decided to follow the trail, wondering what kind of secret could be hidden in such a remote and wild region.

As they moved deeper into the valley, the trees began to clear, revealing a sight that was absolutely beautiful and quite unexpected. A whole city made of white stone and gold stood before them, and it shined brightly under the afternoon sun. The architecture was unlike anything they had ever seen in history books, with tall towers that seemed to reach the sky. No noise came from the streets, and it appeared as though the entire place had been empty for many hundreds of years. However, the gardens were full of colorful flowers, which suggested that some force was still maintaining the life of this city. They felt wonder as they stepped onto the main street, which was covered with smooth stones of a silver color.

In the center of the city, they found a small temple that was surrounded by a circle of ancient trees that looked powerful. On a stone table in front of the temple, a small box was left open, as if someone had just walked away. Inside the box lay a map that was drawn on a thin piece of metal, showing the plan of the city. Jack realized that the map was not just a guide, but a key that would be necessary to unlock the temple. If they had arrived a day later, the thick clouds might have covered the entrance, making it impossible to enter. They studied the map carefully, noticing that it contained several complex puzzles that were related to the stars.

The first puzzle required them to move three large pieces of glass so that light from the sun would shine into the temple. Emily, who was very good at solving difficult problems, managed to calculate the correct angles by using the map. As the light hit the final glass, a loud sound moved through the square, and the heavy stone doors began to open. They entered a large hall where the walls were covered with pictures that described the history of the people there. These people had used a special kind of energy that was derived from the earth itself to build their wonderful home. It was clear that the city was a place of great knowledge and peace, protected from the world by mystery.

Deep inside the temple, they discovered a large room that was filled with a soft, blue light from a large stone. This stone was the source of power for the entire city, but it was currently becoming weak, causing the walls to shake. According to the map, the city would only remain steady if a specific code was entered into the base machine. Jack and Emily knew they had to act quickly because the city was starting to disappear around them like a dream. If the energy failed, the city would be lost forever, and they would be trapped within a place that no longer existed. They began to work together, using the information they had gathered from the ancient pictures to find the right code.

The code consisted of several symbols that represented the elements of nature, such as water, fire, wind, and the earth. Emily carefully pressed the symbols in the order that was suggested by the poem she had read on the wall. For a moment, nothing happened, and the silence in the room was so heavy they could hear the beat of their hearts. Suddenly, the stone began to glow with an intense light, and the movement of the ground stopped as the energy returned. The city was saved, and the blue light spread through the streets, making the white stone walls shine with life. They felt a great sense of achievement, knowing that they had preserved a piece of history that was truly unique.

As they explored the rest of the temple, they found a room that contained many books written in a strange language. Jack suggested that they should take one book back with them to show to a professor who studied ancient cultures. However, Emily felt that the city should remain a secret, as the presence of too many people might destroy it. They realized that they had been chosen by the city to be its guards, a role that would require responsibility. If the world knew about this place, the government might try to take control of the energy for their own purposes. They decided to leave the books where they were and only take the memory of their great adventure with them.

Before they left, they noticed that the map they had used was beginning to change, showing a new path away. This path was much easier than the one they had taken to arrive, leading them back to the main road. They walked slowly through the city one last time, admiring the great detail of the buildings and the peaceful gardens. It was a place where time seemed to stand still, and they felt lucky to have experienced such an event. They promised each other that they would return one day, but only if the city called to them again. The sun was beginning to set, painting the sky with red and orange colors as they reached the valley edge.

When they finally reached their car, the mountain was covered in thick clouds, making the hidden valley disappear. They looked back, but there was no sign of the white towers or the golden roofs they had seen before. Jack wondered if the city would still be there tomorrow, or if it only appeared to those looking for adventure. Emily smiled, holding the small stone box that she had kept as a sign of the secret they now shared. They drove home in silence, each thinking about the mysterious city and the ancient people who had built it. The experience had changed them both, giving them a new perspective on the mysteries that still exist in our world.

### C1
Emily and Jack trudged through the deep green forest that clung to the high mountain side, their summer holiday stretching into an endless trek. The air was cool and fresh, carrying the faint scent of pine and damp earth, while the narrow trail disappeared beneath tangled trunks. Sunlight filtered through the canopy in thin shafts, painting the mossy floor with shifting gold, yet the path grew increasingly hard to discern. Jack halted, his eyes narrowing as he spotted a narrow cleft between two massive rocks, a mouth-like opening that seemed to gape toward a hidden valley. A sudden chill brushed their skin, and Emily felt an uncanny pull, as if the forest itself whispered that they were about to uncover something ancient.

On the ground, half-buried stone markings glimmered faintly, their weathered engravings forming a pattern that pointed toward a place absent from any modern map. The symbols, though eroded, resembled constellations and geometric shapes, suggesting a guide left by hands long vanished. Emily knelt, brushing away centuries of dust, and felt a faint vibration, as if the stones themselves were breathing. Jack traced the line with his fingertip, noticing that it led deeper into the shadowed gorge, toward a darkness that promised both danger and wonder. Without a word, they agreed to follow the trail, each step echoing a quiet pact to uncover the secret hidden in this remote wilderness.

The forest thinned gradually, and the oppressive canopy gave way to a sudden clearing that revealed a sight both breathtaking and impossible. Before them rose a city of white stone and gold, its facades gleaming under the afternoon sun like a mirage of mythic proportions. Towering spires stretched upward, their silhouettes slicing the sky, while silent streets lay empty, as though time itself had paused within the walls. Yet gardens burst with color, roses and lilies swaying gently, hinting that an unseen hand still tended the blossoms, preserving life in a forgotten realm. Emily felt her breath catch, a mixture of awe and trepidation, while Jack's pulse quickened, his mind racing with questions about the city's origins.

They stepped onto the main avenue, its surface smooth and silver-gray, reflecting the bright sky as if it were a river of light. The air was filled with a faint hum, a melodic resonance that seemed to emanate from the stone itself, inviting them deeper. At the heart of the city stood a modest temple, encircled by a ring of ancient trees whose trunks bore scars like old battle wounds. In front of the altar lay an open wooden box, its lid tipped as if someone had just stepped away, revealing a thin metal sheet with a map. The map was not merely a guide; its intricate lines formed a puzzle that promised to unlock the temple's hidden chambers, should they decipher it in time.

Jack traced the map's symbols with a fingertip, noting that the first challenge involved three massive glass slabs that needed to be angled toward the sun. Emily, whose mind delighted in solving riddles, calculated the precise angles, recalling the position of the sun at that hour from the map's tiny sun-dial. She whispered the numbers to Jack, who lifted the first slab, its surface catching the light and scattering prisms across the stone courtyard. When the third glass settled into its final position, a resonant boom echoed through the square, and the massive stone doors shivered before grinding open. Beyond the threshold, a vaulted hall unfolded, its walls adorned with frescoes that narrated the rise and fall of a civilization powered by the earth itself.

The paintings showed people harnessing luminous crystals, channeling their glow into sprawling conduits that fed the city's heart, a colossal stone pulsing with blue fire. Emily sensed a growing instability; the stone's glow flickered, and the frescoes hinted at a looming collapse should the hidden code not be entered soon. According to the map, a specific sequence of elemental symbols-water, fire, wind, earth-had to be pressed into a recessed console at the chamber's core. Jack found the console, a circular slab of dark stone etched with the four sigils, each waiting for a careful touch. Emily recalled a poem inscribed on a wall panel, its verses describing the order of the elements as a river flowing to the sea.

She whispered the first line, and together they pressed the water symbol, feeling a cool ripple travel through the stone, as if the city inhaled. The fire sigil glowed crimson, and a warm gust brushed their faces, confirming that the second element had been accepted. When they activated the wind, a soft sigh swept the hall, and the earth sigil pulsed, resonating with the deep blue stone at the center. A moment of deafening silence followed, the kind that makes a heartbeat audible, as the city seemed to hold its breath. Then the stone erupted in radiant light, the blue fire steadied, and the trembling walls ceased, as if the ancient power had been restored.

The city around them glowed anew; white walls shimmered with life, golden roofs caught the sun's last rays, and the gardens sang with color. Emily felt a surge of triumph, yet a shadow of responsibility settled over her, for they had become custodians of a secret that could change the world. Jack, still breathless, suggested they gather some of the strange books lying on stone shelves, eager to share their discovery with a specialist. Emily hesitated, remembering the fragile balance that kept the city hidden, fearing that outside eyes might exploit the energy for selfish ends. In a silent agreement, they left the volumes untouched, choosing instead to carry only the memory of the marvel and the promise to protect it.

As they turned to leave, the map on the stone table began to shimmer, its lines rearranging to reveal a new, smoother route back to the mountain path. The newly drawn trail bypassed the treacherous gorge, winding gently through meadowland, promising a swift return before nightfall. They walked slowly, savoring each detail-the delicate carvings on doorframes, the scent of jasmine in the gardens, the distant call of a lone hawk. Emily glanced back once more, feeling the city's quiet gratitude settle like a warm ember in her chest. They promised each other that, should the city ever call them again, they would answer, for the bond forged there was deeper than any ordinary adventure.

At the valley's edge, the sun sank low, painting the sky in scarlet and amber, while the distant mountains gathered shadows like looming giants. When they finally reached their car, a thick veil of clouds rolled over the peak, concealing the hidden valley and erasing the city's silhouette from view. Jack stared at the empty horizon, wondering if the city would still stand tomorrow or if it existed only for those daring enough to seek it. Emily clutched the small stone box she had kept, its weight a quiet reminder of the secret they now shared, a promise etched in metal. In the silence of the drive home, each contemplated the mystery of the ancient people, their hearts forever altered by the glimpse of a world beyond ordinary maps.

## st-0024 — The Midnight Library Key
Kaynak: ozgun

### A1
Max is a student. He is a young man. He loves the old town place. This place has many books. He reads books every day. One Tuesday he finds one thing. The thing is in an old book. The thing is small. It is not big. It is a new key for him.

The key feels not cold. Max wants to open a way. He looks for one hour. He finds a small door. It is in a room. The room is not seen by others. Max uses the key now. The door opens with a sound. Max goes into the room.

He sees many things that tell time. All the things are at a stop. The time is the same for all. The room has a certain feel. Max looks at the things. Some are of one color. Some are of another color. He does not know who is there. He hears a low sound. The door closes now. The door is not open. Max is in the room. He looks at his phone. There is no way to talk. He cannot leave the room. He is a part of the story.

He stays in the room. He waits for a long time. He reads more books there. The room is his home now. He feels good in the room. He likes the calm life. The time does not move. He is still a student. He learns about the past. He is glad in the room. He reads books all day. He stays in the room more. He likes the room a lot. The room is a good place. He stays for a long time. The story continues in the room.

### A2
The student was a young man. He loved the old town house for books. He spent every afternoon there. He reads many books during the day. One Tuesday he found a small key. The key was inside an old book about history. The book was on a wood board. He took the key in his hand. The key felt not warm. He wondered what the key could open. He looked for an hour. He looked around the room. Then he saw a small box. It was behind some seats. The seats were near the back wall.

The student moved the seats. He pulled the box away from the wall. The key fit into the opening on the box. He turned the key. The box opened with a sound. The student saw many clocks inside. All the clocks stopped at the same time. It felt like time stopped in this place. He walked into the room. He felt a not warm air on his face. He did not know what was inside. The situation was very important. He did not want to leave. The student looked at the clocks. They were all different. Some were very visible. Some were from a tree. He wondered who put them there.

Suddenly he heard a sound. The door closed. It was closed again. He was inside with the clocks. He could not go out. He checked his phone. There was no signal. He looked for a way out. He was now part of the history of the house. He had to stay there. He would wait for someone to come. He would hope for the best. He would read more books. The story of the student was now a new part of history. He was a strong person. He would continue to study the clocks. The house was old and not loud. He felt steady in the room. He had many clocks to watch. Time was not a problem now. He would stay until someone found him. This was his new life. He was happy to read more. He had a lot to see. The house was big and old. He would learn about the clocks each day. He felt that this was his home now. He would not go away. He would just wait for a friend. The clocks were his new family. He was ready for the next part of his life. The story of the house would continue. He was glad to be there.

### B1
Leo was a student who loved the old town library. He spent every afternoon reading books in a quiet corner. One Tuesday, he found a small key inside an old book about history. The key felt cold and heavy in his hand. He wondered what the key could open. After searching for an hour, he noticed a large box. It was hidden behind a row of chairs in the room below the floor. Leo moved the chairs. He pulled the box away from the wall. The key fit perfectly into the lock. When he turned it, the door opened with a sharp sound. Inside the room, Leo saw many clocks on the walls. All of the clocks had stopped at the same time. It was as if time had ended in this place. He walked inside and felt a strange wind on his face. If he had known what was inside, he might not have opened the door. However, the story was too important to ignore. Leo looked at the clocks and realized they were all different. Some were made of gold, while others were made of wood. He wondered who had placed them there so long ago. Suddenly, he heard a soft sound from behind him. The door had closed and was now locked again. He was held inside with the quiet clocks. He checked his phone, but there was no signal. If he could find another way out, he would take it. He realized that he had become a part of the library history now. Leo looked around the room to find a way to escape. He had not felt so afraid in his entire life. He touched the gold clocks to see if they could move. They did not move at all. He walked toward the walls to check every single clock again. Perhaps one clock had a hidden mark to open the door. He checked every single wall in the room. He found nothing to help him leave. He sat on the floor and thought about his family. He hoped someone would come to the library soon. The room was very dark and cold. He kept thinking about the key he found. If he had left the book on the table, he would be safe now. But he had taken the key. He had to wait for the morning to see if anyone came. The time passed very slowly in the room. He closed his eyes and tried to stay calm. He knew that he must wait for a chance to leave. The library was a place he loved, but now it was a bad place. He hoped that the morning would bring a way out. He had learned a lesson about history and old books today. He would never take anything from a book again. He felt tired as he sat on the cold floor. The room was filled with secrets from the past. Every minute felt like an hour to the young student. He tried to remember the story from the book he had read. The story had been about a library that disappeared. Leo hoped he would not disappear as well. He thought about his parents at home. They would worry if he did not come home for dinner. He looked at the clocks one more time. The gold clocks were very beautiful in the dim light. He wondered if they were made of real gold. He stood up and looked for any mark on the wall. There was nothing that could help him. He felt sad that he had taken the key. He should have been more careful with his choice. The library was usually a happy place for him. Now he felt only fear in the dark room. He hoped the morning light would help him. If he could only reach the door, he would be free. He waited for any sign of a person. The silence was heavy in the room. He looked at his watch, but it also had stopped. Time had no meaning in this secret room. He would wait until the sun rose outside. Maybe then someone would hear his voice. He had to stay strong until the new day came. He promised himself to be careful in the future. The library held many stories, but he had found one too many. He closed his eyes again to rest his mind. He was ready to leave the moment the door would open. His life as a student had changed in one short day.

### B2
Leo was a student who loved the old town library. He spent every afternoon reading books in the quiet corner. One Tuesday, he found a small silver key inside an old book about history. The key felt cold and heavy in his hand. He wondered what the key could open. After searching for an hour, he noticed a wood lock box hidden behind a row of chairs in the lower floor room. Leo moved the chairs and pulled the box away from the wall. The silver key fit perfectly into the lock. When he turned it, the door opened with a loud sound. Inside the room, Leo saw many clocks on the walls. All of the clocks had stopped at the exact same time. It was as if time itself had decided to end in this place. Leo was a young man who had always been interested in history. He had spent many hours in the library because he liked the silence there. The library was a large place with many rooms. If he had known that the room was locked, he would have brought a light. The air in the room was cold and felt still. He walked inside and felt a strange movement of air on his face. If he had known what was inside, he might not have opened the door. However, the mystery was too important to ignore. He looked at the clocks and realized they were all different. Some were made of gold, while others were made of simple wood. He wondered who had placed them there so long ago. Suddenly, he heard a soft whisper from behind him. The door had closed and was now locked again. He was trapped inside with the silent clocks. He checked his phone, but there was no signal. If he could find another way out, he would take it. He realized that he had become a part of the library history now. The room was filled with shadows that danced on the walls. He stood still and listened to the silence. It was a heavy silence that seemed to fill the room. He thought about the people who had built this room. Were they scientists who had wanted to control time? The possibility made him feel both interested and worried. He walked around the room and touched each wall. He searched for a hidden switch or a secret handle. The walls were made of stone and felt very cold. He needed to leave before the evening began. If he did not leave, his family would worry about him.

Leo sat on the floor and thought about his situation. The room was very dark, but he could still see the clocks. He decided that he must find a way to escape. He looked for a small window or a hidden door. There was nothing except the silent clocks. He tried to push the door, but it would not move. It seemed that the lock was very strong. Leo felt afraid, but he tried to keep his mind clear. He remembered a book he had read about the history of the town. The library had once been a place where scientists studied time. Perhaps this room was their secret laboratory. If he could understand the function of the clocks, he might find a way out. He began to touch each clock one by one. He felt a small button on the side of one gold clock. When he pushed it, a secret door slowly opened in the wall. He was so happy that he could leave the room. He walked out and into the main part of the library. He was safe, but he would never forget the secret room. The library remained a place of mystery for him. He knew that he would continue to study the history of the town. He was a student who had survived a strange experience. The silver key was still in his hand. He would keep it as a reminder of his time inside the secret room. The library was his home and he felt lucky to be there. He walked back to his desk and opened his book again. He felt that he had a special connection to the past now. The mystery of the clocks would stay with him forever. He would return to the library again tomorrow. The town had many secrets that were waiting to be found. Leo was ready to solve them one by one. He was no longer just a student. He was a seeker of truth in a world of mystery. The library was the perfect place for his new life. He felt that everything was possible now. His heart beat fast as he thought about the next adventure. The silver key would always be a part of his story. He walked toward the main hall and smiled. The evening light was beautiful outside. He had learned much today. The world felt larger than it had this morning. He was happy to be a part of this history. He closed his book and walked out of the library. His journey had only just begun. He walked past the large main door. The air outside was fresh. He looked up at the stars. He felt that the library would always hold more secrets. He was ready to find them. Many more days would be spent in that place. He was not afraid of the mystery anymore. He was a person who wanted to learn. The past was a key to the future. He felt sure of that now. The night was quiet and calm. He walked home with a light heart. The town was asleep. Only the library remained awake in his mind. He knew he would go back. The story of the clocks was just the first part. He would find more keys. He would find more doors. The world was full of wonder. He was glad to be a part of it. He felt strong. He felt wise. The library was his life now. He would keep his promise to the town. He would solve every mystery. His future was clear. He walked into the night. Everything was ready for him. He was ready for everything.

### C1
Leo, a third-year university student, cherished the old town library as his sanctuary from the clamor of campus. Every afternoon he slipped into the far-right corner, where dust motes floated lazily in shafts of filtered sunlight. The silence there felt like a thick blanket, inviting him to lose himself among yellowed pages and forgotten voices. He loved the way the old wooden tables creaked gently, a reminder that the building itself remembered each visitor. On that particular Tuesday, a faint rustle of paper signaled the beginning of an adventure he could not yet imagine.

While leafing through a battered volume on medieval trade routes, Leo's fingers brushed against a cold, silver key tucked between the pages. The key was heavier than its size suggested, as if it carried the weight of an unseen story. He turned it over, noting the intricate filigree that seemed to echo a forgotten language. A sudden curiosity seized him: what door, what chest, what secret might this relic unlock? He slipped the key into his pocket, feeling the metal press against his thigh like a promise.

Determined to discover the key's purpose, Leo roamed the lower floor, his eyes scanning every shadowed nook. Behind a row of battered chairs he noticed a faint outline of a wooden lock box concealed by dust. He pushed the chairs aside, the squeak echoing through the empty corridor, and pulled the box from the wall. The silver key slipped into the lock with a satisfying click, and the lid burst open with a resonant clang. A rush of stale air escaped, carrying the scent of old pine and forgotten metalwork.

Beyond the box lay a narrow doorway that led into a dimly lit chamber, its walls lined with dozens of clocks. Each clock, whether gilded, mahogany, or iron-bound, displayed the same frozen minute: twelve o'clock, March twelfth, 1897. The hands were caught mid-stroke, as if time itself had paused to listen to a secret. A thin layer of dust settled on the glass faces, yet the air felt unnaturally still, pressing against Leo's skin. He sensed a faint vibration, like a heartbeat echoing from the room's very foundation.

Leo, whose fascination with history had begun in childhood, had spent countless afternoons within these vaulted aisles. The library's labyrinthine layout, with its hidden alcoves and forgotten chambers, seemed designed for secrets to hide. Regretting that he had entered without a lantern, he felt a chill crawl up his spine as the temperature dropped. A sudden draft brushed his cheek, carrying faint whispers of pages turning that seemed to originate from nowhere. If he had known what lay beyond the door, he imagined he might have approached the mystery with more caution.

As soon as he stepped fully inside, a soft whisper rose behind him, and the door slammed shut with a thud. The lock clicked into place, sealing him within a chamber where the only sound was the mute ticking of frozen clocks. He fumbled for his phone, only to discover that no signal penetrated the stone walls. Shadows stretched across the floor, flickering like silent dancers, while a heavy silence settled like a physical weight. Despite the rising panic, Leo reminded himself that curiosity had always driven him, and he would not surrender to fear.

He wondered who had arranged this collection of clocks, perhaps scholars obsessed with measuring eternity. The notion that scientists once used the library as a laboratory for temporal experiments both thrilled and unsettled him. He ran his hands along the cold stone walls, searching for any concealed lever or hidden panel. The silence pressed harder as the minutes ticked in his mind, reminding him that the evening was approaching. He knew that if he remained trapped past dusk, his family would begin to worry, adding urgency to his search.

Sitting on the cold floor, Leo allowed his thoughts to settle like dust in a quiet archive. He recalled a forgotten manuscript that described the town's nineteenth-century experiment with synchronized chronometers. With steady hands, he examined each clock, feeling for irregularities, until his fingertips brushed a tiny button on a golden timepiece. Pressing the concealed button, a narrow panel in the stone wall creaked inward, revealing a secret doorway bathed in pale light. A rush of relief flooded him as he stepped through, emerging into the familiar main hall of the library.

The silver key still clinked softly against his palm, a tangible reminder of the hidden world he had escaped. He glanced back once, noting the now-closed portal, and felt a surge of gratitude for the mystery's mercy. Returning to his usual desk, he reopened the history book, its pages now tinged with personal significance. The library, once merely a refuge, had transformed into a gateway where past and present intertwined. Leo resolved to study the town's hidden chronicles with renewed vigor, believing that every locked door concealed a lesson.

He lingered by the large stained-glass window, watching the evening light melt into amber across the courtyard. The rhythm of his heart quickened as he imagined future explorations, each key perhaps leading to another concealed chamber. He tucked the silver key into his notebook, feeling its cold metal against the paper as a talisman. Outside, the town's bells began to toll, their distant resonance weaving through the quiet streets like a warning. Yet the impending darkness felt less threatening now, for he carried the certainty that curiosity could illuminate any shadow.

He stepped out of the library into the crisp night, the air scented with lilac and distant sea salt. Above him, a constellation of stars flickered, each point a silent witness to his newfound resolve. He felt the world expand, as if the library's secret had unlocked a door inside his own mind. The town lay asleep, its lamps dim, while his thoughts roamed the corridors of history he had yet to chart. He promised himself that every mystery, no matter how silent, deserved the patience of a careful seeker.

Back home, he placed the silver key on his desk, where it gleamed like a tiny beacon of adventure. He reflected on how the library, once a simple refuge, had become the axis of his future quests. The night outside whispered promises of undiscovered rooms, of clocks still waiting to be set free. He slipped beneath his blankets, his mind already tracing the paths he would follow at sunrise. In the quiet dark, the silver key rested beside his notebook, a quiet reminder that history is a door waiting to be opened.

Leo is a student. He loves the old school library. It is a quiet place. He reads there in peace.

One day, he finds a clock. It is behind a large book. The clock has wood and gold. The hands move on their own.

The note is about a room. The room is old and hidden. It is closed for many years. Leo wants to find the room.

He must solve the mystery. He does it before twelve. He believes in the secret. It changes the school history.

The clock reaches twelve. Leo finds the last clue. He pushes a heavy shelf. He sees a door behind it.

The room has old records. It has maps of the town. Leo feels very proud now. He knows the truth.

### C1
Leo, a third-year literature student, cherished the old school library as a sanctuary from the bustle of corridors. The vaulted ceilings, stained glass windows, and rows of dust-covered tomes created a quiet that seemed to pause time itself. He loved the faint scent of aged paper, a perfume that reminded him of distant histories waiting to be uncovered. Most afternoons he settled at a worn oak table, spreading his notebook and a stack of novels like a pilgrim before a shrine. The library's silence, broken only by the occasional rustle of pages, gave him the space to think clearly about the stories he loved.

One bright afternoon, while reaching for a thick volume on the top shelf, Leo's hand brushed against an unfamiliar slab of dark wood. Tucked behind a towering column of encyclopedias, the slab concealed a small, ornate clock whose surface was polished to a mirror-like sheen. Its frame, carved from ebony, bore delicate vines that curled around golden hands which seemed to move without any visible force. A faint ticking echoed through the surrounding shelves, as though the clock were counting moments that no one else could hear. Leo felt a sudden chill run down his spine, an instinctive warning that this discovery was far from ordinary.

As he examined the clock more closely, the golden hands glided past the numerals with a fluid grace that defied the rigid mechanics of ordinary timepieces. Without warning, the clock emitted a soft, resonant chime that rang at an hour none of the school's bells ever marked. The sound reverberated against the high windows, scattering dust motes that glittered like tiny stars in the dim light. Intrigued, Leo noted the exact moment of the chime, aware that the clock seemed to keep its own secret schedule. He sensed that each unexpected ringing was a clue, a whisper from the past urging him to listen more closely.

Over the next hour, the clock chimed three times in rapid succession, a pattern that felt deliberate and ominous. Leo hurried to the nearest open book, his fingers trembling as he turned to the third page, as if the number itself held meaning. There, tucked between the printed lines, lay a small, weather-worn note written in a careful, looping hand that Leo did not recognize. The note read, "Seek the hidden chamber where the school's first stories were born; the key lies where silence dwells." A shiver ran through Leo's spine, for the phrasing seemed to echo the library's own whispers, hinting at a secret room long forgotten.

Leo's mind raced, connecting the note's cryptic instruction with the strange clock that had just announced its presence. He imagined a forgotten wing of the school, perhaps sealed during a renovation, its doors hidden behind rows of books that never moved. The clock's irregular chimes, he reasoned, were a coded timetable, each set of three signaling that a new fragment of the puzzle was ready to be uncovered. Yet a lingering doubt tugged at him: could a simple student truly unlock a mystery that had lain dormant for decades? He reminded himself that curiosity had always been the catalyst for discovery, and that the clock's silent promise seemed to demand action before midnight.

Determined, Leo set a personal deadline: he would decipher every clue before the clock struck twelve, believing that the final revelation could alter the school's legacy. The library's shadows deepened as the afternoon waned, and the soft glow of the setting sun filtered through stained glass, painting amber patterns on the floor. Each time the clock chimed, Leo noted the precise minute, jotting symbols in his notebook that grew into a map of temporal breadcrumbs. When the third chime sounded again, a faint rustle seemed to accompany it, as though a hidden panel had shifted somewhere behind the shelves. A sudden draft brushed his cheek, carrying the faint perfume of old leather and ink, and Leo felt the library itself urging him onward.

Following the direction of the draft, Leo traced his fingers along the spines of towering volumes until he reached a secluded alcove rarely visited by students. There, a narrow gap between two massive encyclopedias revealed a faint outline of a wooden panel, its surface dulled by years of neglect. Leo pressed his palm against the panel; it gave a soft sigh and swung inward, exposing a hidden cavity that housed the clock's twin mechanism. Inside lay a brass key, tarnished but still gleaming, attached to a folded piece of parchment that bore the words, "When the final bell tolls, push the heavy shelf." The revelation sent a thrill through Leo's veins, confirming that the clock's enigmatic messages were indeed a map to something concealed.

He scanned the surrounding rows for a shelf that matched the description, eyes narrowing as dust motes danced in the slanted light. At the far end of the aisle, a massive oak shelf stood, its doors sealed shut with iron bolts that had rusted into immobility. With a deep breath, Leo braced himself, grasped the nearest bolt, and pulled with all his strength, feeling the metal give a reluctant sigh. The shelf lurched forward, revealing a narrow doorway that vanished into darkness, the air beyond smelling of cedar and forgotten whispers. For a heartbeat, Leo hesitated, aware that stepping through might plunge him into a story older than any book he had ever read.

Summoning his courage, he slipped through the opening, emerging into a vaulted chamber lit by a single, dust-covered chandelier that flickered weakly. The walls were lined with shelves that stretched up to a ceiling lost in shadow, each shelf sagging under the weight of leather-bound chronicles. On a central table lay a mound of brittle maps, their ink faded to sepia, depicting the town as it once was, streets winding like veins. Leo's eyes were drawn to a thick ledger bound in dark green leather, its pages filled with entries penned in a hurried, almost frantic hand. As he opened the ledger, a faint humming resonated from beneath the floorboards, as if the building itself were breathing in anticipation.

The entries revealed that the school's founders had hidden a collection of original manuscripts, sketches, and early town records within this secret chamber. According to the ledger, the chamber had been sealed after a fire in 1892, its entrance concealed to protect the priceless items from further damage. Leo felt a surge of excitement, realizing that his discovery could rewrite the town's known history and give scholars a glimpse into a vanished era. He carefully lifted the oldest manuscript, its parchment fragile and scented with the faint aroma of pine resin used in its preservation. The room seemed to pulse with a quiet reverence, as if the very walls acknowledged the return of knowledge that had slumbered for a century.

With the evidence safely tucked into his bag, Leo stepped back through the concealed doorway, feeling the weight of history settle upon his shoulders. The library outside seemed unchanged, yet a new awareness tinged every echo of turning pages and every creak of the ancient floorboards. He resolved to share his findings with the school's archivist, confident that the hidden chamber's treasures would inspire future generations. As the clock's final chime faded into silence, Leo smiled, feeling as though he had stepped out of a story and into one of his own making. He knew that the whispering library clock had not only told time, but also whispered the promise of mysteries waiting to be heard.

## st-0027 — The Whispering Library Book

### A1

He knew that he must solve the final clue before the book disappeared. If he failed, he would never know what was coming next. He looked closely at the paper and saw faint words appear.

### C1
Leo slipped through the towering doors of the municipal library just as the afternoon sun began to bleed through the high stained-glass windows. The silence inside was a thick blanket, broken only by the occasional rustle of pages turning in distant corners. His fingertips brushed a pile of forgotten tomes piled in a shadowed alcove, and one volume, its cover mottled with age, caught his eye. The leather-like hide of the cover felt rough, as if the book itself resisted being opened after so many silent years. When he lifted the heavy volume, a faint scent of cedar and dust rose, whispering promises of long-lost stories.

Inside, the pages were yellowed and cramped with a hurried script that seemed to tremble under the pressure of an unseen hand. The first entry, dated with a day that had not yet arrived, warned of a bus that would careen down Main Street at dusk. A chill ran down Leo's spine as he read the precise details: the screech of brakes, the shattering of glass, the anguished cries that would follow. Compelled by a mixture of curiosity and dread, he hurried to the street, his heart hammering like a frantic drum. There, as the sun slipped behind the rooftops, the bus indeed lurched forward, its driver's eyes widening before the inevitable crash unfolded before his eyes.

Shaken, Leo raced back to the library, his mind a storm of questions that refused to settle. He flipped through the cramped pages until a new entry caught his attention, describing a fire that would engulf the town hall that very night. The description was unnervingly precise: flames licking the red brick, smoke curling into the sky, and the panicked shouts of trapped citizens. Without hesitation, Leo sprinted to the hall, his breath forming clouds as he pushed open the heavy oak doors. Guided by the book's foreboding script, he found a hidden stairwell and led a handful of frightened people to safety before the roof gave way.

Back inside the hushed library, the notes seemed to shift, their ink now curling around his own name as if the book had begun to observe him. Each subsequent page recorded events that unfolded in his life that day: the taste of bitter coffee, a sudden rain soaking his coat, and the scent of pine from a passing truck. A cold realization settled over him like a fog: the book was not merely predicting, it was actively scripting his future. He felt a tremor of fear rise, for the final page lay blank, a void that threatened to swallow whatever came next. If he could not decipher the last clue, the script might keep writing itself, and he would be forever trapped within its pages.

He pressed his forehead against the yellowed parchment, eyes narrowing until the ink seemed to stir like a waking serpent. At first nothing changed, but as he breathed slowly, a faint luminescence spread across the fibers, revealing ghostly letters. The words formed a chilling prophecy: the library would be closed forever, its doors sealed by an unseen hand. A surge of adrenaline rushed through Leo's veins, and the sound of distant sirens seemed to echo the warning. He seized the book, feeling its weight suddenly heavier, as though the future it contained pressed down upon his shoulders.

Without a moment's hesitation, Leo sprinted toward the grand entrance, the vaulted ceiling above him dimming as the chandelier's bulbs flickered out. The marble floor, polished to a mirror sheen, reflected the growing shadows that seemed to swallow the aisles. He reached the massive oak doors, their iron hinges cold as stone, and pressed his palm against the iron handle. A whispered promise rose from his throat: I will not let the silence claim this place, I will keep the light alive. As the last ember of illumination sputtered, the handle seemed to pulse, and the book in his other hand dissolved into fine ash.

When the dust settled, the library's oppressive hush gave way to a gentle breathing, as if the building itself exhaled relief. Leo placed the empty cover on the nearest table, feeling the absence of the pages like a wound that had finally closed. The strange script had vanished, leaving only the faint echo of its warnings in the corners of his mind. He stepped out into the night, the cool air brushing his cheeks, and for the first time in days he felt unburdened. Above the city's lights, the stars seemed to wink knowingly, as though they, too, had watched the book's secret unfold.

In the quiet of his apartment later, Leo replayed the events like a film, each frame sharpening his understanding of choice versus destiny. He realized that the book had been both a trap and a key, offering knowledge that could have doomed countless strangers. Yet the final blank page reminded him that not every future is predetermined; the ink stops where intention begins. He vowed to cherish the moments that were not written, to listen to the ordinary whispers that ordinary days offered. And if another curious soul ever uncovered that dusty volume, perhaps the library would keep its secret, safe within the silence of its stacks.

The city slept beneath a blanket of twilight, unaware that its most silent guardian had just reclaimed his own story. Leo smiled, feeling the weight of the past lift, and walked toward the horizon where possibilities stretched like sunrise. In that moment, the library's doors remained ajar, a promise that knowledge, once freed, could never be bound again.

Days later, when the sun painted the streets gold, Leo found himself returning to the same alcove, drawn by an inexplicable pull. The shelves stood unchanged, yet a faint hum resonated between the rows, as if the building remembered the whisper of the vanished tome. He placed his hand on the empty space where the book had rested, and a shiver travelled up his arm, not of fear but of reverence. A soft wind slipped through the cracked window, carrying with it the faint echo of pages turning, a reminder that stories never truly end. Leo smiled, understanding that the library itself was a living manuscript, its chapters written by every visitor who dared to listen.

He walked away, the city's chorus of distant horns and murmured conversations weaving around him like a new narrative waiting to be penned. The night sky stretched above, infinite and uncharted, and he felt a quiet certainty that his future would be written with his own hand. No longer bound by the ink of a stranger, Leo embraced the blank pages ahead, ready to fill them with choices, mistakes, and hope. And if someday another curious reader were to stumble upon his story, perhaps they would find not a warning, but an invitation to listen. For within every silent shelf lies a whisper, and every whisper holds the power to change the world for those who hear it.

## st-0028 — The Hidden Mountain Map

### A1

### C1
Leo, a lanky history student, and Mia, a curious literature major, spent every free afternoon in the town's ancient library. The library, with its vaulted ceilings and dust-laden shelves, smelled of parchment and time, inviting whispered explorations. One rainy afternoon, Leo brushed aside a stack of forgotten tomes and uncovered a leather-bound volume that seemed to pulse with secrecy. The book's brittle pages whispered as he turned them, and a folded parchment slipped out, its edges browned by age. Leo's eyes widened when he realized the parchment was not a simple illustration but a map, its ink faded yet unmistakably drawn.

The map traced a winding trail that disappeared into the jagged silhouettes of the distant mountains, promising a hidden cave. In the margin, a faint note warned that reaching the cavern before sunset might reveal a treasure long forgotten by men. Leo felt a shiver of excitement; the prospect of uncovering history felt like stepping into a storybook adventure. Mia, whose imagination often turned ordinary moments into epic quests, immediately suggested they follow the map at once. They tucked the fragile chart into Leo's satchel, exchanged a quick, conspiratorial grin, and stepped out into the drizzle.

The narrow road that curled up the hill felt like a ribbon of stone, edged by ancient oaks that whispered with wind. As they climbed, the air grew thinner, and the scent of pine mingled with the distant smell of wet earth. Leo consulted the map frequently, his finger tracing the faint lines as if they might reveal hidden clues. Mia, meanwhile, kept an eye on the horizon, noting the sun's slow descent behind the craggy peaks. Their breaths formed small clouds that vanished quickly, reminding them that time slipped away as surely as the fading light.

After several hours of steady ascent, the sun slipped behind the highest ridge, painting the sky in bruised purples and gold. Shadows stretched across the path, and a chill settled over the landscape, making the forest feel like a sleeping giant. Leo's shoulders tightened, a sudden worry knotting his stomach as he wondered whether the treasure would still be reachable. The map's edge seemed to blur in the dimming light, as if the paper itself resisted being read. Yet the promise of discovery urged them onward, and they quickened their steps despite the growing unease.

Suddenly, a faint luminescence caught their eyes: a line of blue stones lay embedded in the mossy ground, each emitting a soft, eerie glow. The stones seemed to pulse in rhythm with the wind, forming a natural lantern that cut through the forest's darkness. Mia reached out, her fingertips tingling as she brushed a stone, and felt a warm current travel up her arm. "These must be the guide the map promised," Leo whispered, his voice barely audible over the rustling leaves. With renewed determination, they followed the glowing trail, each step echoing softly against the cold, damp earth.

Leo's mind raced, fearing that night would swallow the treasure before they could lay eyes on it. "What if we miss it?" he asked, his voice trembling like a thin leaf in a gust. Mia turned, her eyes reflecting the blue light, and answered with a steadiness that steadied his own heart. "We cannot let fear dictate our path; the map chose us, and we must honor that promise." Their steps grew firmer, and the blue stones seemed to respond, brightening ever so slightly as they approached.

The forest thinned, revealing a sheer rock face punctuated by a narrow crevice that beckoned like a hidden mouth. Leo slipped the map into his pocket, and together they squeezed through the cold opening, the stone walls slick with condensation. Inside, the air smelled of mineral earth and faint incense, as if centuries of secrets lingered in the darkness. Their flashlights cut through the gloom, revealing walls adorned with ancient carvings that told stories of forgotten kings. Deeper within, a vaulted chamber opened, its floor scattered with golden objects that glittered like captured sunrise.

Leo knelt, his hands trembling as they brushed over a tarnished crown, its jewels dull yet still radiant with history. Nearby, a bronze chest lay open, revealing scrolls sealed with wax, their ink faded but legible to the keen eye. Mia lifted a delicate silver goblet, feeling the cool metal sing under her fingertips, a relic from a banquet long past. As they examined each artifact, a quiet reverence settled over them, replacing the earlier rush of greed. They understood that the true treasure lay not in gold alone, but in the stories these objects could tell.

Leo imagined the hands that once wore the crown, the voices that whispered over the goblet, and felt humbled. Mia thought of the countless generations that had guarded these items, each adding a layer of memory to the cave. The realization that they were the newest link in a chain of guardians filled them with quiet pride. Yet, as the last sliver of daylight vanished, the cavern grew colder, and shadows stretched like waiting specters. They exchanged a glance, both knowing the importance of leaving the site untouched for future seekers.

Their flashlights flickered as the darkness deepened, casting trembling halos over the golden relics. Leo took a final, lingering look at the crown, feeling the weight of centuries settle upon his shoulders. Mia recorded their findings in a small notebook, her handwriting steady despite the chill that crept into her fingers. The cavern's silence was broken only by the distant drip of water, a reminder of time's relentless flow. With reverence, they turned back toward the glowing stones, aware that the world outside waited, unchanged yet unknown.

Emerging from the mountain's shadow, the friends felt the night's cool air wrap around them like a familiar cloak. The blue stones faded into the forest floor, their purpose fulfilled, leaving only the memory of their gentle light. Leo glanced at the map one last time, noting that the ink had darkened, as if acknowledging their success. Mia smiled, feeling the thrill of adventure settle into a quieter, lasting satisfaction that would linger in future studies. Hand in hand, they walked downhill, their shadows merging with the night, carrying the secret of the hidden mountain map forever.

### C1
When the clouds pressed low over the town, Leo and Mia slipped into the quiet library, their usual refuge after the last school bell rang. Rain tapped a steady rhythm on the stained-glass windows, turning the aisles into a soft, amber-lit tunnel that seemed to invite hidden discoveries. Between the towering shelves they spotted a leather-bound volume, its spine cracked and dust motes dancing in the slanted light. When they opened it, a brittle map fell out, its ink faded but the lines unmistakably traced a route up the distant mountains. A faint scribble on the margin promised a secret cave hidden high above, placed there by an unknown hand many years before.

The map was drawn in trembling ink, each contour of the ridge rendered with a care that suggested reverence rather than mere curiosity. A tiny emblem, resembling a pinecone crossed with a star, marked the entrance, as if the cartographer had wanted future seekers to recognize the spot. Leo traced the route with his fingertip, feeling the raised fibers of the parchment rise like a pulse under his skin. Mia, eyes bright, imagined the wind whipping around a hidden opening, the echo of distant waterfalls guiding the way. Together they sensed that the map was not a mere curiosity, but a silent invitation to prove themselves worthy of an ancient promise.

They agreed that the cave must be reached before dusk, for once the sun slipped below the peaks the trail would dissolve into shadow. Mia slipped her jacket tighter around her shoulders, feeling the chill that hinted the mountain's altitude even before they had begun the ascent. Leo packed a small rucksack with a water bottle, a flashlight, and a notebook, each item a quiet reassurance against the unknown. The forest path ahead was a tangled carpet of moss and fallen leaves, the scent of pine mingling with the earthy perfume of rain. With hearts thudding like drums, they stepped onto the trail, aware that every footfall was a promise to the mystery that waited above.

The slope rose sharply, a slab of stone that seemed to test their resolve with each precarious step. Leo's breath came out in short clouds, the air thin enough to taste metal, yet his mind stayed fixed on the hidden entrance. Mia, though winded, kept her gaze forward, noting the way the sun's rays filtered through the trees like golden filaments. Every now and then a small bird burst into song, a fleeting reminder that life continued even in such a demanding climb. Their excitement grew with each meter gained, for the sensation of becoming real explorers outweighed the fatigue that pressed against their limbs.

At the summit, a sudden wind whispered through the crags, carrying a scent of cold stone and distant snow. The sky, once a bright blue, now wore a thin veil of amber as the sun began its slow descent. A faint glow illuminated a narrow crevice hidden behind a massive, gnarled oak, its bark scarred by time. Mia's breath hitched as she recognized the shape of the entrance described on the map, a perfect match to the drawing's crooked line. Leo brushed aside a curtain of hanging moss, revealing the dark mouth of the cave that seemed to inhale the waning light.

The interior was cool, a hush that wrapped around them like a woolen cloak, muffling every footstep. A faint drip echoed from deeper shadows, each drop a metronome marking the passage of time within stone. Leo's flashlight cut a thin cone of light, revealing a small wooden box perched on a stone slab as if waiting. The box was unadorned, its lid slightly ajar, and the air around it carried a faint metallic scent. Mia reached out, her hand trembling not from fear but from the thrill of uncovering something long concealed.

Inside lay a handful of tarnished coins, each stamped with symbols that hinted at a forgotten civilization. Beside the coins rested a vellum parchment, its edges curled like the leaves of an ancient book. The parchment bore a careful script, each line flowing with a rhythm that seemed to echo the cave's own heartbeat. At the top of the letter, a seal of wax bore the same pinecone-star emblem that marked the map's entrance. The ink, though faded, still glimmered faintly, as if reluctant to surrender its secrets to the dim light.

Leo unfolded the parchment slowly, his eyes scanning the words that spoke of a guardian who had hidden the treasure for generations. The letter declared that the riches were reserved for those who proved courage, curiosity, and respect for the land that bore them. It warned that the map would lead only to the entrance; the true test lay within, in the willingness to cherish what was found. Mia felt a shiver run down her spine, not from cold but from the realization that they had been chosen by chance and destiny alike. Together they whispered a quiet thanks to the unseen guardian, their voices mingling with the distant drip of water.

The sun, now a thin crimson slash on the horizon, reminded them that night would soon cloak the mountain in darkness. They tucked the coins into the rucksack, feeling their weight as a tangible reminder of the adventure's reward. Leo rolled the parchment carefully, sealing it inside a waterproof sleeve to protect the fragile script from the evening damp. Mia glanced back at the cave's mouth, its darkness now seeming less threatening and more like a secret kept safe. With the treasure secured, they began the descent, each step a promise to return one day and share the story.

The forest seemed to watch them, leaves rustling in a rhythm that matched the beating of their hearts. As they emerged onto the familiar path leading back to town, the sky darkened, sprinkling the world with twinkling stars. Leo felt a quiet pride, realizing that the map had been a catalyst, not just a guide, for their growth. Mia smiled, thinking that the true treasure might be the memory of the climb, the shared wonder, and the promise of future quests. They walked home under the night sky, the constellations above a silent audience to their whispered plans.

When they reached the library's stone steps, the rain had stopped, leaving puddles that reflected the lanterns' warm glow. They placed the coins on a table, their metallic clink resonating like a promise fulfilled. The letter was folded and tucked into a notebook, a secret they would cherish and perhaps one day share with other seekers. As the library's clock chimed midnight, Leo and Mia exchanged a look that said adventure was only beginning. The hidden mountain map, now a treasured relic, rested on the shelf, waiting for the next curious hearts to discover its secret.

## st-0033 — The Saturday Explorers

### A1
Leo and Mia are students. They love old stories. They visit the local library every Saturday. They look for new adventures. One day they find an old book. Inside is a secret map. It shows a path to a cave. The cave is in the high mountains.

Leo looks at the map. They want to find something special. Mia wants to start the trip now. They pack food and warm clothes. The next day they begin the climb. The mountain is high and cold. The path is very difficult. They walk and talk about the cave. They feel happy but also afraid. After many hours they reach the top.

A large stone door is in front. It has old signs on it. They look at the signs. The signs are like the stars. Leo turns the stone circles. The heavy door opens. It is dark inside the cave. They see a soft blue light. It comes from a garden. The garden has glowing crystals. The crystals grow like trees. The colors are very bright. They stay for a long time. They enjoy the magic place.

The trip back to town is long. They are happy. They keep the secret. They do not tell anyone. The place must remain safe. The experience stays with them. They put the map back. Another person can find it soon.

### A2
Leo and Mia were students who loved old stories. They went to the local library every Saturday. One afternoon, they found an old, dusty book. Inside the book was a secret map. It showed a path to a hidden cave in the mountains.

Leo looked at the map. If they followed the path, they would find something special. Mia wanted to start the journey right now. They packed bags with food and warm clothes. Early the next day, they began their long climb.

The mountain was very high and cold. The path was difficult because of the rocks. While they walked, they talked about the cave. They felt excited but also a little afraid. After many hours, they reached the top.

The garden was beautiful and quiet. Crystals grew from the ground like trees. They were surprised by the bright colors. People had never seen anything like this before. They stayed for a long time to enjoy the magic.

The trip back to the town was long but good. They decided to keep the secret of the garden. If they told anyone, the special place might get damage. They knew this experience would stay with them forever. They put the map back for the next person.

### C1
Leo and Mia, two university students with a shared obsession for forgotten legends, met every Saturday in the town's modest library. The library, with its vaulted ceiling and rows of time-worn volumes, smelled of paper, dust, and distant rain. Between the murmurs of other readers they whispered plans to uncover new adventures hidden among the brittle pages. On one rain-slick afternoon a faint glimmer caught Mia's eye from the topmost shelf, where only the most neglected books lingered. She reached for the volume, feeling the weight of centuries settle into her palms, and brushed away a thick veil of dust.

Inside, between cracked leather covers, a folded parchment lay concealed, its edges curled like the leaves of an ancient tree. The parchment was a map, drawn in ink that had faded to a melancholy amber, depicting a winding trail that vanished into the high mountains. A faint inked symbol of a compass rose marked the starting point, while a tiny illustration of a stone door hinted at a hidden destination. Leo traced the lines with his fingertip, feeling a sudden surge of curiosity that seemed to echo the map's own secret whispers. Mia's eyes widened; she could already picture the adventure, and without a word she suggested they set out at first light. When they lifted the book, a faint rustle seemed to echo, as though the parchment itself whispered gratitude for being uncovered.

Back at Leo's small attic room, they spread the map on a creaking desk, its parchment softening under the glow of a single lamp. They listed supplies-sturdy boots, a coil of rope, dried fruit, and woolen blankets-each item chosen to counter the cold they imagined waiting above. Leo, ever the cautious one, double-checked the weather forecast, noting that a thin veil of snow was predicted for the evening. Mia, whose enthusiasm rarely waned, laughed at the notion of postponement, insisting that the mountain's call could not wait for any delay. When the first pale rays of dawn slipped through the cracked windows, they slung the heavy packs onto their backs and stepped onto the winding path that led away from town.

The mountain loomed ahead, its summit lost in a veil of thin, icy clouds that glimmered like distant pearls against a bruised sky. A gust of cold wind brushed their cheeks, carrying with it the sharp scent of pine and the faint echo of distant waterfalls. The trail, narrow and strewn with jagged stones, forced them to pick their steps carefully, each footfall a small triumph over the restless ground. As they climbed, they exchanged theories about what lay beyond the stone door, weaving wild guesses that blended myth with the practical. A nervous thrill fluttered in their stomachs; the excitement of discovery was tempered by an undercurrent of fear that made the wind seem louder. The sun, a pale disc behind the clouds, cast long shadows that made the path appear like a winding serpent across the mountain's flank.

Hours slipped by, marked only by the sun's slow arc and the occasional cry of a lone mountain bird. When the path finally narrowed to a flat plateau, a massive stone slab rose before them, its surface etched with ancient symbols that seemed to pulse with forgotten memory. The symbols, spirals and constellations intertwined, formed a cryptic tableau that hinted at a celestial key required to unlock the doorway. Leo crouched, his breath forming small clouds, and began to align the carved circles with the night sky he remembered from a textbook. When the last circle matched the brightest star, a resonant rumble echoed through the mountain, and the stone door shivered before grinding open.

A cold darkness spilled out, swallowing their lantern light, and for a heartbeat they could not see more than a few inches ahead. Mia steadied the torch, its flame trembling like a frightened bird, and whispered that the map promised a hidden garden beyond the gloom. They took a cautious step forward, their boots echoing on the stone floor, until a soft blue glow began to seep from the far wall. The light revealed a cavernous chamber where crystal formations rose like translucent trees, each one humming faintly as if breathing with the earth itself. Among them, a garden of glowing crystals stretched across the floor, their colors shifting from sapphire to emerald, casting delicate shadows that danced in the air.

The silence was profound, broken only by the occasional drip of water that fell from stalactites high above, creating a gentle rhythm. Leo knelt beside a cluster of violet crystals, feeling their cool surfaces and noting how the light refracted into a thousand tiny rainbows. Mia traced a hand over a ruby-red formation, and a faint musical tone resonated, as if the stone sang a lullaby of ages past. They lingered, allowing the garden's quiet magic to seep into their thoughts, each breath feeling revitalized by the unseen energy that pulsed beneath the crystals. Time seemed to stretch, yet they knew they could not remain forever; the world outside awaited, and the secret they had uncovered needed protection. Before they left, they placed a small, smooth stone from the garden onto their pack, a silent reminder of the wonder they had witnessed.

When they finally rose to leave, the blue light dimmed gradually, as if the garden sighed, acknowledging their departure. The stone door, now ajar, emitted a cool draft that carried the faint scent of mineral and pine, guiding them back toward the mountain's outer slope. They descended with measured steps, the weight of their packs seeming lighter as the memory of the crystal garden steadied their hearts. At the base, the village lights twinkled like fireflies, and the familiar sounds of the market filled the air, a comforting contrast to the mountain's solitude. Leo placed the map back into the book's hollow, sealing it with a soft thump, and whispered a promise that no one else would ever find the hidden garden.

As they rested at the foot of the mountain, the crisp air filled their lungs, and a solemn oath formed between them, binding their discovery to silence. They agreed that the garden's fragile wonder must not be marred by curiosity of strangers, for its beauty thrived on the reverence of secrecy. A quiet promise passed like a secret handshake, each understanding that the map would return to its book, awaiting the next worthy seekers. With that resolve settled, they rose, feeling the weight of responsibility as much as the weight of their packed supplies.

Mia smiled, feeling the adventure settle into her bones, and suggested that the memory itself might be the most valuable treasure they had uncovered. They walked home under a sky turning pink with twilight, each step echoing the rhythm of a story that would live on in their minds. The town's quiet streets seemed to guard their secret, as if the mountains themselves had whispered a pact of silence to the passing wind. Later, when Leo opened the library's ancient volumes again, he imagined the map waiting patiently for the next curious pair, its story dormant yet eager. They locked the book with a worn leather strap, sealing the secret within, and felt a quiet pride in being its guardians.

## st-0040 — The Wednesday Poem
Kaynak: ozgun

### A1
