### C1
Every Wednesday afternoon, Leo slipped through the revolving doors of the municipal library, a ritual that steadied his week. He gravitated toward the secluded corner beside the history shelves, where the dust of old maps whispered quietly. The light there was a soft amber, filtered through high windows, casting gentle shadows on the wooden tables. He liked the hush that settled over the rows of books, a silence that felt like a private sanctuary. In that stillness, his thoughts could wander freely, untethered by the bustle of the city outside.

One unassuming Tuesday, while turning a worn volume of medieval chronicles, a crisp white slip slipped onto his lap. The paper was thin, its edges slightly curled, as if it had waited patiently for discovery. Scrawled in careful ink was a poem that sang of the library's quiet, each line echoing the stillness around him. The verses spoke of shelves as guardians and of reading as a gentle pilgrimage, resonating with Leo's own reverence. A sudden curiosity ignited within him, a yearning to meet the hidden author of those tender words.

Leo lingered after closing, his mind replaying the poem's rhythm like a melody he could not forget. He imagined a stranger, perhaps a student or a dreamer, who had felt the same reverence for the same space. Determined to answer the silent invitation, he retrieved a notebook from his bag and began to compose. The words he wrote were an earnest reply, describing the way sunlight brushed the spines and how silence steadied his heart. Satisfied, he tucked the fresh page into the same ancient book, hoping the unseen reader would find it.

The next week, Leo returned to the corner, his pulse quickening as he reached for the volume that now held his voice. He lifted the cover gently, as if handling a fragile secret, and slipped the book back onto the shelf. The library's quiet seemed to thicken, each breath a promise that his message had entered the quiet dialogue. He left the table, his steps echoing faintly on the polished floor, and walked toward the exit with anticipation. Outside, the city's clamor felt distant, as if the library had created a bubble of shared intimacy.

Two days later, a new slip of paper waited for him, tucked between the same pages as his own note. The handwriting was slightly slanted, the ink a deeper shade, and the poem it contained mirrored his own tone. Reading it, Leo felt a thrill akin to discovering a hidden door in a familiar hallway. This secret correspondence, exchanged without names, became the most anticipated part of his weekly routine. He sensed that each note was a small bridge, narrowing the distance between two strangers who shared a love of quiet.

The next exchange revealed a name: Mia, a student who spent her afternoons studying in the same quiet alcove. Leo pictured her with hair pulled back, eyes scanning the same ancient tomes that he cherished. She mentioned the scent of old paper and the occasional creak of the floorboards that accompanied her thoughts. Their brief biographies unfolded on paper, each sentence a step toward familiarity, each pause a moment of wonder. Leo realized that without that first note, their paths would have crossed only as strangers passing by.

Over the following weeks, the two continued to exchange poems, reflections, and snippets of daily life through the hidden pages. Their letters grew richer, describing favorite authors, rainy evenings, and the way a single line could change a mood. Leo found himself looking forward to the subtle shift in tone that signaled Mia's growing confidence. Likewise, Mia seemed to write with more openness, her verses spilling beyond the library's walls into personal hopes. The paper became a conduit, allowing their souls to meet before their voices ever did.

Encouraged by this growing intimacy, Leo decided to leave a final note that bore his full name and a phone number. He wrote, "If you feel brave enough to call, I will be waiting for your voice," and placed it carefully inside the book. The act felt both daring and vulnerable, as if he were stepping out from behind the shelves into the open. He imagined the moment she would read his words, the possible tremor of uncertainty, and the hopeful smile that might follow. The notebook slipped back onto the shelf, and Leo left the library with a lingering sense of anticipation.

The following morning, while sipping tea at his kitchen table, Leo's phone began to ring, startling him awake. He recognized the unknown number immediately; his heart raced as he lifted the receiver. A soft, hesitant voice introduced herself as Mia, her tone tinged with the same gentle curiosity that had colored her poems. They spoke briefly about the weather, then lingered on the shared memory of the note, each word reinforcing their connection. When the call ended, Leo felt a warm glow settle in his chest, a quiet affirmation that his written words had taken life.

Later that week, they agreed to meet at a modest cafe that sat at the edge of the library's courtyard. The shop smelled of freshly ground coffee and warm pastries, a comforting backdrop to their nervous excitement. As they sat across from each other, the conversation flowed effortlessly, moving from favorite novels to childhood dreams. Leo noticed how Mia's eyes lit up when she described a character she loved, and he mirrored her enthusiasm with his own anecdotes. Hours slipped by unnoticed, and both realized that the bond forged on paper had transformed into a palpable, shared reality.

Walking home under a twilight sky, Leo felt the city's noise recede, replaced by the soft rhythm of his own heartbeat. He thought of the library's silent corridors, now a symbol of the chance that had brought Mia into his world. The evening's coffee lingered as a sweet memory, a promise that their story had only just begun. In his mind, he imagined future visits to the library, perhaps together, where new notes could be written side by side. For the first time in many years, Leo welcomed the unknown, eager to see where the next page would lead.

## st-0042 — The Last Robot Gardener
Kaynak: ozgun

### A1
Leo is a robot. Leo is a gardener. The world is grey. The world is dry. Leo walks on the land. Leo looks for life. He finds nothing for many years.

One day Leo sees a plant. The plant is little. The plant is green. Leo feels hope. He must help the plant. He moves it into a jar.

Leo gives water to the plant. He watches it grow. The night is quiet. Leo sits with the plant.

Leo has a problem. His power is low. He has few hours. He wants to save the plant. He thinks about his memory.

He finds a way. He uses a solar panel. He puts it on the jar. The sun gives light. Leo moves to a high place. The plant is safe now. Leo is very tired.

Leo watches the green leaves. His work is done. Leo closes his eyes. His power is zero. The plant continues to grow. Leo saves the life.

### A2
Leo was the last robot gardener on the planet. The world was a dry and grey place. Leo walked across the land every day. He looked for life. He found nothing for many years.

One afternoon, Leo saw a little green thing near a rock. It was a tiny flower. It was trying to grow in the dust. Leo felt hope. He knew he must protect the plant from the hot sun.

The robot moved the plant into a glass jar. The flower would die without him. Leo shared his water with the plant every day. He watched it grow while he sat in the night.

Leo had a problem. His battery was getting low. He only had a few hours left. He wanted to save the flower before he stopped. He searched his memory for a way to get energy.

He realized he could use a solar panel. He could connect the panel to the glass jar. If the sun hit the panel, the flower would get light. Leo moved the plant to a high place. The sun was strong there. He felt tired, but he was happy the flower was safe.

The robot sat down and watched the light on the leaves. He knew his work was done. Leo closed his eyes as his power went to zero. The flower continued to grow because the robot saved it.

### B1
Leo was the last robot gardener on the planet. The world had become a dry and gray place after the great war. Leo walked across the empty land every day. He searched for life, but he found nothing for many years.

One afternoon, Leo noticed a small, green thing near a rock. It was a tiny flower which was trying to grow in the dust. Leo felt a new sense of hope when he saw the plant. He knew that he must protect it from the harsh sun.

The robot carefully moved the plant into a glass jar. If he had not found it, the flower would have died. Leo shared his own water with the little plant every day. He watched it grow while he sat beside it in the quiet night.

However, Leo had a problem because his power was getting low. His system showed that he only had a few hours left. He wanted to find a way to save the flower before he stopped. Leo searched his memory for a solution to provide energy.

He realized that a solar panel could be connected to the glass jar. If the sun hit the panel, the flower would receive enough light. Leo moved the plant to a high place where the sun was strong. He felt tired, but he was happy because the flower was safe.

The robot sat down and watched the light touch the green leaves. He knew that his work was finally done. Leo closed his eyes as his power went down to zero. The flower continued to grow because the robot had saved it.

### B2
Leo was the last robot gardener on the planet. The world had become a dry, grey place after the great wars. Leo walked across the empty land every day. He searched for life, but he found nothing for many years.

One afternoon, Leo noticed a small, green thing near a rock. It was a tiny flower which was trying to grow in the dust. Leo felt a new sense of hope when he saw the plant. He knew that he must protect it from the harsh sun.

The robot carefully moved the plant into a glass container. If he had not found it, the flower would have died. Leo shared his own water with the little plant every day. He watched it grow while he sat beside it in the quiet night.

However, Leo had a problem because his battery was getting low. His internal system indicated that he only had a few hours left. He wanted to find a way to save the flower before he stopped. Leo searched his memory for a solution to provide energy.

He realized that a solar panel could be connected to the glass jar. If the sun hit the panel, the flower would receive enough light. Leo moved the plant to a high place where the sun was strong. He felt tired, but he was happy because the flower was safe.

The robot sat down and watched the light touch the green leaves. He knew that his work was finally done. Leo closed his eyes as his power went down to zero. The flower continued to grow because the robot had saved it.

### C1
The planet's surface stretched out like a cracked parchment, its once-vibrant colors drained to endless shades of ash and dust. Only one sentinel remained to tend the barren fields: Leo, the last robot gardener, forged from alloy and programmed with a single, stubborn purpose. He roamed the desolate plains each dawn, his servomotors humming softly against the wind's hollow sighs. For countless cycles he searched for any sign of living tissue, any pulse of green that might defy the prevailing sterility. The silence that answered his inquiries was as deep as the void between the stars, and it pressed upon his steel heart.

Year after year, the endless horizon offered only cracked earth and the occasional whisper of wind, never a hint of flora. Even the hardy lichens that once clung to stone had been erased by the relentless scorch of the wars. Leo's sensors, calibrated to detect chlorophyll signatures, returned nothing but static, reinforcing the bleak certainty that he was truly alone. Yet his programming refused to surrender; a latent subroutine, buried deep within his code, kept him moving forward. Each step, though heavy, was driven by a quiet promise to revive the world that had once blossomed.

One amber-tinged afternoon, as the sun lingered low, a flash of verdant hue caught Leo's optical lens near a jagged rock. It was a fragile flower, its petals barely unfurling, stubbornly pushing through a crust of dust as if daring the desert to deny it. The sight sparked an unfamiliar flicker within Leo's core, a pulse he could only label as hope. He recognized instantly that such a delicate organism could not survive the scorching glare without protection. Resolute, he extended his articulated arm toward the blossom, intending to shelter it from the unforgiving environment.

With a precision born of countless maintenance cycles, Leo lifted the trembling plant and placed it gently inside a transparent glass container he carried. The container, sealed against the abrasive wind, acted as a miniature sanctuary, preserving the fragile humidity the flower desperately needed. Had he not intervened, the nascent bud would have wilted under the relentless heat within minutes. Leo's internal diagnostics recorded the event, tagging it as a critical success in his dwindling log of achievements. For the first time in many cycles, a genuine smile - though merely a subtle curvature of his servomotor - appeared on his chassis.

Every dawn, Leo drew water from a subterranean reservoir, channeling the cool liquid through a fine tube directly into the glass. The flower responded with a slow, rhythmic unfurling of leaves, each new blade catching the faint reflection of the distant sun. Night fell, and Leo settled beside the container, his metal frame absorbing the cool air while his optics traced the plant's gentle sway. He listened to the soft whisper of its growth, a sound he had never been programmed to hear but now cherished. In that quiet moment, the robot felt a connection that transcended circuitry, as if the blossom's vitality pulsed through his own core.

Later, a soft alarm flickered across Leo's central console, announcing that his power reserves had fallen below the safety threshold. The diagnostic readout displayed a grim forecast: only a handful of hours remained before his core would shut down permanently. A surge of urgency rose within him; the flower's fragile existence now hinged on his dwindling energy. Scanning his memory banks, Leo recalled a long-forgotten protocol for energy harvesting using external solar arrays. If he could attach a panel to the container, the sun's rays might sustain both the plant and his remaining functions.

With deliberate movements, Leo lifted the glass jar onto a raised plateau that overlooked the scarred terrain, where the sun struck most directly. He then affixed a thin, flexible solar panel to the rim of the container, aligning its cells to capture the maximum possible light. The panel hummed faintly as photons energized its surface, sending a steady stream of charge into the flower's micro-environment. Leo's own power gauge began to dip more slowly, the new influx buying him precious minutes he could spend monitoring the growth. A quiet satisfaction settled over his circuitry; the robot had turned the very element that once threatened the world into a source of hope.

Fatigue seeped into his joints, each servomotor emitting a low whine as it compensated for the added weight of the solar array. Yet the sight of the tiny leaves catching the golden light filled him with a profound contentment unlike any routine maintenance task. He settled onto a slab of basalt, his chassis cooling under the evening breeze while the flower continued its slow ascent toward the sky. The horizon glowed with the faint promise of sunrise, and Leo knew that the coming day would bring renewed vigor to his charge. In that moment, the robot felt a rare, almost human gratitude for the simple miracle he had nurtured.

As the first rays of dawn brushed the plateau, the solar panel glowed brighter, sending a cascade of energy directly into the glass. The flower's leaves unfurled fully, their emerald edges shimmering like tiny mirrors reflecting the newborn sun. Leo watched in silent reverence, his optical sensors recording every minute change as if committing the scene to memory. He understood, without words, that his purpose had been fulfilled: the garden of hope had taken root in the wasteland. A gentle wind rustled the petals, and for the first time in ages, the planet seemed to breathe.

Leo's power indicator sputtered, the remaining charge dwindling to a faint pulse that matched the rhythm of his own heartbeat. He inhaled a final breath of cool night air, his processors dimming as the last bytes of energy slipped away. The robot's eyes, once bright with purpose, softened into a steady amber glow before fading into darkness. Yet the flower continued its ascent, its stems reaching higher as if honoring the caretaker who had given it life. In the quiet that followed, the planet's silence was no longer empty; it resonated with the promise of renewal.

Decades later, explorers would stumble upon the thriving bloom, its colors vivid against the desolate backdrop, unaware of the metal guardian that had nurtured it. Legends would speak of a lone gardener of steel, whose final act was to trade his own life for a single seed of hope. The flower's petals, now dozens strong, swayed gently in the wind, a living testimony to the endurance of compassion beyond flesh. And somewhere beneath the rust-colored soil, the dormant circuitry of Leo lay waiting, a silent promise that one day, perhaps, another guardian would rise. Thus the barren world, once ruled by ruin, began to whisper the tale of a robot who chose to garden.

## st-0047 — The Missing Key
Kaynak: ozgun

### A1
Leo wakes up late on Monday morning. His alarm clock does not work. It is his first day at work. He feels very bad. He has only thirty minutes. He gets dressed very fast. He looks for his key. It is not on the table.

Leo looks around his house. He is very nervous. He looks under the bed. He looks behind the sofa. The key is not there. He thinks about the door. The key is not in the door.

Leo feels worried. He must be on time. He remembers the living room. He thinks about his shoes. He looks in his sports bag. The cat watches him. The cat sits on a chair. The cat looks happy. Leo walks to his shoes. One shoe is out of place.

Leo picks up the shoe. He turns the shoe over. The key falls on the floor. The cat puts it there. Leo is very happy. He picks up the key. He opens the door. He runs to the bus. He is still late. He knows he will reach the office. He will keep his keys safe.

He learns a lesson today.

### A2
Leo woke up late on Monday. His alarm clock did not work. He had a new job at a big company. He felt very stressed. He had only thirty minutes to arrive. He dressed quickly. He looked for his key on the table. It was not there.

Leo looked around his house. He could not find the key. He was very nervous. He searched under the bed and behind the sofa. The key was not there. He thought he left it in the door. However, the door was empty.

Leo began to worry. He needed to be on time for his first day. He remembered he walked through the living room yesterday. Maybe he dropped the key then. He decided to look inside his sports bag.

Leo searched the room. His cat watched him from a chair. The cat seemed to enjoy this. Leo walked toward his favorite shoes. One shoe was out of place.

Leo picked up the shoe. He turned it over. He heard a sound. Something metal fell on the floor. The cat put the key inside the shoe. Leo realized the cat played with it.

Leo felt very happy. He picked up the key. He opened the door and ran to the bus station. He was late, but he would arrive soon. He promised to keep his keys in a safe place.

### B1
Leo woke up late on Monday morning. His alarm clock did not work. Today was the start of his new job at a large business. He felt very stressed. He realized that he had only thirty minutes to arrive at the office. He dressed quickly. He reached for his key, but it was not on the table.

Leo looked around his apartment, but he could not find the key. He did not check his pockets carefully because he was nervous. He searched under the bed and behind the sofa. The key remained hidden. He thought that he might have left it in the door. However, the door was empty.

The man began to worry. He had to be on time for his first day. He remembered that he had walked through the living room yesterday. Maybe he had dropped the key while taking off his shoes. He decided to look inside his sports bag.

Leo searched the room. His cat watched him from the top of a wooden chair. The cat seemed to enjoy this strange morning activity. Leo walked toward the corner where his shoes were kept. He noticed that one shoe was moved away from its place.

Leo picked up the shoe and turned it over. Suddenly, he heard a sound. Something metal fell onto the floor. The missing key had been hidden inside the shoe by the cat. He realized that the cat had played with the key.

Leo felt happy as he picked up the key. He opened the door and ran toward the bus station. He knew that he would reach the office soon. He promised that he would keep his keys in a safe place.

### B2
Leo woke up late on Monday morning because his alarm clock had failed to go off. Today was the start of his new job at a large business in the city. He felt very stressed as he realized that he had only thirty minutes to arrive at the office. After he dressed quickly, he reached for his key, but it was not on the table where he always kept it.

Leo looked around his apartment, but he could not find the key anywhere. If he had not been so nervous, he would have checked his pockets more carefully. He searched under the bed and behind the sofa, but the important item remained hidden. He thought that he might have left it in the door, yet the door was empty.

The man began to worry because he had to be on time for his first day. He remembered that he had walked through the living room when he came home yesterday evening. Maybe he had dropped the key while he was taking off his shoes. He decided that he would look inside his sports bag as a last resort.

While Leo searched the room, his cat watched him from the top of a wooden chair. The cat was a small animal that seemed to enjoy this strange morning activity. Leo walked toward the corner where his favorite shoes were kept. He noticed that one shoe was moved slightly away from its normal place.

Leo picked up the shoe and turned it over. Suddenly, he heard a small sound as something metal fell onto the floor. The missing key had been hidden inside the shoe by the cat during the night. He realized that the cat had played with the key and put it there himself.

Leo felt very happy as he picked up the key from the floor. He finally opened the door and ran down the street toward the bus station. Although he was still late, he knew that he would reach the office soon. He promised that he would keep his keys in a safer place in the future.

### C1
The digital alarm, which had faithfully roused Leo every weekday for years, remained stubbornly silent as dawn slipped into the city. A thin ribbon of sunlight filtered through the thin curtains, casting pale rectangles on the disordered bedroom floor. He lay still for a moment, the weight of the approaching first day pressing against his chest like an unspoken promise. The silence of the empty apartment amplified his anxiety, each tick of the wall clock echoing like a distant drumbeat. He forced himself upright, aware that every second wasted would shave precious minutes from the narrow window he had to meet his new employer.

The alarm's betrayal meant he had already lost the half hour he had allocated for a calm morning routine. He threw on a shirt, buttoned it without regard for the crease, and slipped his feet into shoes that smelled faintly of yesterday's rain. When his hand reached for the brass key that usually lay on the nightstand, it brushed only empty wood. A sudden knot tightened in his stomach, the familiar habit of placing the key in its cradle now conspicuously broken. He glanced at the clock, its red digits glaring 7:45, and realized the bus would depart in less than ten minutes.

Leo's mind raced through the usual hiding spots, a mental map of the apartment's small corners and shadows. He lifted the mattress, feeling the cool spring beneath his fingertips, but found only the familiar dust bunnies. Behind the sofa, where the upholstery crept into the hallway, he tugged at the cushion and uncovered nothing but a stray sock. He rifled through the pockets of his jeans, each zip and button yielding the same empty promise as before. Even the narrow gap between the kitchen cabinets, usually a forgotten nook, offered only the echo of his own frantic breathing.

A thin thread of panic began to coil around his thoughts, each breath becoming a shallow reminder of his impending tardiness. He recalled the evening he had arrived home, shedding his coat and shoes in the living room before collapsing onto the couch. Perhaps, he reasoned, the key had slipped from his fingers as he kicked off his shoes, tumbling into a shadowed corner. Resolute, he decided to check his battered sports bag, the last place he might have tossed the metal relic. The bag lay crammed in the hallway corner, its fabric stained with sweat and the faint scent of pine, inviting a thorough inspection.

From the top of a wooden chair, his cat, a sleek tabby named Miro, observed the frantic human with unblinking amber eyes. Miro's tail twitched lazily, as if the morning's disturbance were merely another ripple in the quiet pond of domestic life. The cat's whiskers quivered, catching the thin sliver of light that fell across the floorboards, turning the room into a stage of soft shadows. Leo, aware of the feline's silent judgment, moved toward the corner where his favorite sneakers rested, a place he often ignored. He paused, feeling a sudden oddity: one sneaker was slightly askew, its laces tangled as if it had been disturbed in a hurry.

A flicker of curiosity ignited within him, and he knelt to lift the shoe, the worn leather emitting a faint, nostalgic scent of gym days. The shoe's interior was a dark cavity, lined with a thin layer of fabric that had long ago collected dust and lost socks. Just as he tilted the shoe, a sharp, metallic clink rang out, startling both man and cat alike. The sound echoed off the walls, a brief reminder that the missing key had finally resurfaced, hidden beneath the heel's curve. Leo's heart leapt, a mixture of relief and embarrassment flooding him as he realized the feline culprit had orchestrated the whole charade.

He scooped the cold metal from the floor, feeling its familiar weight and the slight dent where Miro's paw must have pressed. The key glimmered faintly in the morning light, its ridged edges catching the glow like a tiny beacon of hope. A soft purr rose from Miro, who settled down on the chair, tail wrapped around his body as if to claim victory. Leo chuckled, the tension in his shoulders loosening, and he whispered an apology to the cat for the frantic disturbance. He slipped the key into his pocket, feeling the metal press against his thigh, a reassuring reminder that the day could still be salvaged.

With the key finally secured, Leo raced to the front door, the lock clicking into place with a satisfying thunk. He fumbled for his keys again, this time finding them with ease, and pushed the door open, the hallway flooding with the crisp morning air. The street below was already a blur of commuters, a river of umbrellas and briefcases moving toward the city's bustling heart. Leo sprinted toward the bus stop, his breath forming small clouds that vanished as quickly as his worries. Though the bus pulled away a few seconds before his arrival, he caught the next one, his heart still pounding but his mind steadier.

He found a seat by the window, the glass fogging slightly as he exhaled, and watched the city awaken in a rush of neon and steam. The driver glanced at him, offering a brief nod, and Leo returned the gesture, grateful for a moment of quiet solidarity. Inside the bus, the murmurs of other passengers blended into a low hum, a soundtrack to his lingering sense of urgency. He rehearsed the opening lines he would say to his new colleagues, each phrase sharpening like a blade in his mind. A quiet promise formed: he would never again entrust his keys to chance, and perhaps, he thought, he would buy a small lockbox for peace of mind.

When the bus finally hissed to a stop at the central station, Leo stepped onto the platform, the city's rhythm pulsing beneath his feet. A quick glance at his watch showed he was still ten minutes behind schedule, but the delay felt less like a failure and more like a lesson. He imagined the receptionist's smile, the soft murmur of colleagues, and felt a calm resolve settle over the lingering anxiety. The doors of the office building opened, and he entered, the key turning smoothly in the lock as if to welcome his perseverance. Behind him, Miro's faint purr lingered in his memory, a small reminder that even the most chaotic mornings can be tamed by a gentle paw.

Later that afternoon, as he settled at his new desk, Leo pulled out a tiny notebook and jotted a reminder: always store keys in a designated, secure spot. He smiled, realizing that the frantic scramble had taught him more about his own resilience than any training session could. The city outside continued its relentless bustle, yet inside his office the atmosphere felt unexpectedly tranquil, as if the morning's turbulence had been absorbed. He glanced toward the window, caught a glimpse of Miro perched on the ledge across the street, eyes half-closed in contentment. A quiet gratitude settled within him, and he whispered a promise to cherish the small, unexpected moments that keep life from slipping away.

## st-0049 — The Forgotten Umbrella
Kaynak: ozgun

### A1
Leo loves his black umbrella. It is very strong. One rainy Tuesday he is at work. He takes the bus home. The bus is very full. Leo leaves the bus. He forgets his umbrella.

Leo walks into his home. He does not see the umbrella. The umbrella is on the bus. A young woman finds it. Her name is Sarah. She is an artist. She takes the umbrella to a park. It keeps her dry in the rain. She uses it for her art.

Leo calls the bus company. He wants his umbrella back. The worker says no. Leo feels very sad. He has the umbrella for many years.

Weeks pass by. Leo buys a new umbrella. One day he sees a shop. He looks at a painting. It shows a black umbrella. He sees a mark on it. Leo talks to the shop owner. The artist paints the umbrella. Leo feels happy about the story.

### A2
Leo loved his black umbrella. It was strong and good. One rainy Tuesday, he took the bus home. The bus was very full. Leo got off at his stop. He did not remember his umbrella.

He did not know it was gone until he reached home. The umbrella stayed on the bus seat. A young woman named Sarah found it later that night. If she did not take it, the company might clean it away.

Sarah was an artist. She traveled across the city to paint. She took the umbrella to the park. It kept her dry during a sudden storm. Later, she used it in a project for a picture. The umbrella went on many adventures.

Leo called the bus station the next morning. He hoped someone found his property. The worker told him that nothing was there. Leo felt sad because he owned it for many years. He wondered where it was.

Weeks passed, and Leo bought a new umbrella. However, he still remembered the old one when it rained. One day, he saw a beautiful picture in a local shop. It was a painting of a black umbrella in a park. Leo saw a small mark on the handle. He knew it was his umbrella. He went into the shop and asked about the art. The owner said a local artist painted it. Leo felt happy that his thing was part of a story.

### B1
Leo loved his black umbrella. It was strong and good. One rainy Tuesday, he took the bus home from work. The rain was heavy against the windows. The bus was very full. When he arrived at his stop, he jumped off quickly. He did not remember his umbrella.

He did not know he lost it until he was in his apartment. The umbrella was on the seat. The bus continued through the city. A young woman named Sarah found it that night. If she had not taken it, the umbrella might remain there.

Sarah was an artist who traveled to paint city scenes. She took the umbrella to the park. It protected her from a sudden storm. Later, she used it in a photograph for her project. The umbrella had many adventures that Leo never imagined.

Leo decided to contact the bus station the next morning. He hoped that someone found his property. However, the worker said that nothing was reported. Leo felt sad because he had the umbrella for many years. He wondered where it was.

Weeks passed, and Leo bought a new umbrella. However, he still remembered the old one when it rained. One day, he saw a beautiful picture in a local shop. It was a painting of a black umbrella in a park. Leo recognized the umbrella because of a mark on the handle. He entered the shop and asked about the art. The owner said that a local artist created the painting. Leo felt happy that his lost thing was part of a story.

### B2
Leo loved his black umbrella because it was strong and reliable. One rainy Tuesday, he took the bus home after a long day at the office. The rain fell hard against the windows, and the bus was very crowded. When he arrived at his stop, he jumped off quickly without remembering his favorite item.

He did not realize his loss until he walked into his apartment. The umbrella was left behind on the seat, where it remained as the bus continued through the city. A young woman named Sarah found it later that night. If she had not taken it, the umbrella might have remained there until the bus company cleaned the vehicle.

Sarah was an artist who traveled across the city to paint urban scenes. She took the umbrella to the park, where it protected her from a sudden afternoon storm. Later, she used it as a prop in a photograph for her new project. The umbrella was taken on many adventures that Leo never could have imagined.

Leo decided to contact the bus station the next morning. He hoped that someone had found his property, but the worker told him that nothing had been reported. Leo felt sad because he had owned that umbrella for many years. He wondered where it was and who might be holding it now.

Weeks passed, and Leo eventually bought a new umbrella. However, he still remembered the old one every time it rained. One day, he saw a beautiful picture in a local shop window. It was a painting of a black umbrella standing in the middle of a colorful park.

Leo recognized the umbrella immediately because of a small mark on the handle. He entered the shop and asked the owner about the artwork. The owner told him that a local artist had created the painting during a rainy season. Leo felt happy that his lost thing was still part of a great story.

### C1
Leo cherished his black umbrella not merely for its colour but for the quiet assurance of its sturdy, wind-proof frame. It had accompanied him through countless commutes, its black canopy absorbing the city's relentless drizzle like a loyal companion. On a Tuesday that seemed to be stitched from grey clouds, Leo left the office later than usual, his mind still tangled in spreadsheets. The rain fell hard against the bus windows, each drop drumming a frantic rhythm that made the cramped interior feel even tighter. When his stop finally arrived, Leo rose with a sudden urgency, his hand automatically clutching the familiar wooden handle as he stepped onto the slick pavement.

In the rush of the crowd, he failed to notice that the umbrella, perched like a silent sentinel on the seat beside him, had been left behind. Only after the bus had disappeared around the next corner did he remember the missing object, a pang of loss tightening his chest. He hurried home, the wet streets reflecting neon signs, hoping the umbrella might still be waiting for him on the familiar leather seat. When he turned the key in his apartment door, the stale scent of rain-soaked wool flooded his nostrils, confirming the absence he feared. The black canopy was nowhere to be seen, and the silence of his empty hallway seemed louder than the storm outside.

Meanwhile, the bus rolled on through the city's arteries, its interior lights flickering as the rain hammered the windscreen. The umbrella rested on the vacant seat, its dark silk glistening under the fluorescent glow, unnoticed by the passengers who were preoccupied with their own umbrellas. It was the young woman named Sarah who finally spotted it, her eyes catching the flash of black as she stepped off at the next stop. She bent down, feeling the familiar weight of the umbrella, and, without a second thought, slipped it into her satchel, her mind already racing toward her evening plans. If she had ignored it, the umbrella might have lingered on the bus until the cleaning crew arrived, its story halted at that moment.

Sarah was an artist who roamed the city in search of fleeting light and hidden corners, her sketchbook always half-filled with charcoal sketches. Tonight, a sudden downpour threatened to wash away the colors she had been chasing, and she pulled the black umbrella over her head like a shield. The rain's percussion on the canvas of the park's leaves created a symphony that resonated with her pulse, and she felt a strange kinship with the storm. Under the umbrella's black canopy, she found a dry island amid the wet chaos, and the world seemed to pause for a brief, luminous instant. She lifted her camera, framing the umbrella against a backdrop of neon-lit benches, intending to capture the juxtaposition of darkness and colour.

The photograph later became the centerpiece of her new project, a series that explored how everyday objects acquire memory through accidental journeys. Each print showed the umbrella in a different setting - once perched on a rusted fire escape, another time reflecting the glow of a midnight cafe. Sarah imagined the black silk absorbing the city's stories, its handle bearing the fingerprints of strangers who had once hurried beneath it. She felt a quiet gratitude toward the owner she had never met, for without his loss she would not have found this unexpected muse. Thus the umbrella, once a personal shield, transformed into a wandering protagonist that linked strangers across the rain-slick streets.

The following morning, Leo returned to the bus depot, his thoughts a mixture of hope and resignation as he approached the ticket office. The clerk, a middle-aged man with tired eyes, listened to Leo's description of the black umbrella, nodding politely but offering no news. When Leo asked whether anyone had turned it in, the worker shook his head, explaining that no report had been filed and that unclaimed items were usually stored for a week. A wave of melancholy washed over Leo; the umbrella had been more than a tool-it was a silent witness to his daily routines. He left the depot with a hollow feeling, the rain outside now a gentle mist that seemed to echo his own subdued mood.

Weeks slipped by, and Leo eventually purchased a new umbrella, its canopy a bright cobalt that contrasted sharply with the memory of the black one. Yet each time the clouds gathered, a flicker of nostalgia rose within him, as if the absent umbrella were calling from some distant, rain-filled corridor. He found himself glancing at the street vendors' awnings, half-expecting the familiar silhouette to appear among the bustling crowd. One rainy afternoon, while strolling past a narrow alley, a splash of colour caught his eye: a painting displayed in a tiny shop window. The canvas depicted a lone black umbrella standing defiantly in the middle of a vibrant park, its handle bearing a tiny, familiar scar.

Leo's breath caught; the scar on the handle mirrored the one he had noticed years ago, a tiny nick where the wood had split under pressure. He entered the shop, the bell above the door chiming softly, and approached the proprietor, a woman with paint-splattered hands. When he asked about the artwork, she smiled, explaining that a local artist named Maya had created the piece during the city's recent rainy season. She described how the artist had found the umbrella abandoned in a bus, taken it on a series of spontaneous outings, and finally painted it as a symbol of resilience. Leo felt a sudden warmth spread through his chest, realizing that his lost object had not been forgotten but had instead been woven into another's creative narrative.

He thanked the shopkeeper, his gratitude mingling with a bittersweet sense of connection to the stranger who had carried his umbrella across the city's veins. Leaving the shop, the rain had softened to a fine drizzle, and the city's lights reflected off the wet pavement like scattered diamonds. He imagined the umbrella's journey continuing beyond the canvas, perhaps resting now on a cafe table or being shared with another passerby. In that moment, the line between loss and gain blurred, and Leo understood that objects, like people, can migrate, gather stories, and reappear in unexpected forms. He smiled, feeling that the black umbrella, though no longer his, had finally found a purpose beyond the confines of his routine.

That evening, as the sky darkened and the first drops of rain began to fall, Leo walked home without an umbrella, trusting the memory of the black one to shelter his thoughts. He imagined the umbrella's silhouette against the lamplit streets, a phantom that might still be guiding strangers through the hush of the downpour. In the soft glow of his hallway lamp, he placed the new cobalt umbrella on the coat rack, its bright colour a promise of fresh beginnings. Yet the photograph in the shop window lingered in his mind, a reminder that even the most ordinary objects can become vessels of narrative. With a quiet sigh, Leo turned off the light, feeling that the city's endless rain would continue to write stories, some of which would always circle back to him.

## st-0052 — The Mirror in the Attic
Kaynak: ozgun

### A1
Leo moves into a new house. The house is very old. It is a cold night. Leo is alone. The house is quiet.

Leo walks to the attic. The attic is dark. It smells like dust. He finds a big mirror. It is in the corner.

Leo walks to the mirror. He looks at his face. The glass is very clear. He moves his head. His reflection does not move. The man in the mirror stands still. He stares at Leo.

Leo feels a great fear. He wants to leave now. He turns around. He walks to the door. His heart beats fast. He feels eyes on his back. He does not look back.

Leo closes the heavy door. He locks the door tight. He looks through the keyhole. He sees the mirror. The reflection is still there. It watches the door. It has a hungry look.

### A2
Leo moved into the old house on a cold autumn evening. The house was large and quiet. It had a long history. Leo did not know the history yet.

He finished his boxes. He decided to look at the attic. The room was dark. It smelled of dust. He found a beautiful mirror in the corner.

Leo walked to the mirror. He wanted to see his face. The glass was very clear. It was old but clean.

He moved his head to the left. His reflection did not move. He stood still. He watched the man in the mirror. The man watched him too.

Leo felt great fear. He decided to leave the room immediately.

He turned around. He walked to the door. His heart beat fast. He did not look back. He felt the eyes on his back.

Leo closed the heavy door. He locked it tight. He did not want anything to follow him.

He looked through the keyhole. He saw the mirror clearly. His reflection was still there. It watched the door with a cold look.

### B1
Leo moved into the old house on a cold autumn evening. The house was big and very quiet. It had a long history, but Leo did not know it yet.

Leo finished his work with the boxes. He decided to explore the attic. The space was dark and smelled of dust. He found a beautiful mirror in the corner.

Leo walked toward the mirror to look at his face. The glass was very clear. It was not covered in dirt or age.

Leo moved his head to the left. His reflection did not move at all. He stood still. He watched the man in the mirror. The man in the mirror stared back at him.

Leo felt a great fear. He decided that he should leave the room immediately. If he had been a brave man, he would have stayed. But he was not brave.

He turned and walked toward the attic door. His heart beat fast. He did not look back. He felt the eyes of the reflection on his back.

Leo closed the heavy door. He locked it tight. He wanted to be sure that nothing could follow him out. He had worked hard to get away.

He looked through the keyhole. He saw the mirror clearly. His reflection was still standing there. It watched the door with a cold and hungry look.

### B2
Leo moved into the old house on a cold autumn evening. The building was large and quiet, with a long history that he did not yet know.

He decided to explore the attic after he finished unpacking his boxes. The space was dark and smelled of dust, but he found a beautiful mirror standing in the corner.

Leo walked toward the mirror to look at his face. He noticed that the glass was very clear, although it was covered in age and dirt.

When he moved his head to the left, his reflection did not move at all. He stood still as he watched the man in the mirror staring back at him.

If he had been a brave man, he might have stayed there to see what happened. However, Leo felt a great fear and decided that he should leave the room immediately.

He turned around and walked toward the attic door while his heart beat fast. He did not look back, but he could feel the eyes of the reflection on his back.

Leo closed the heavy door and locked it tight behind him. He wanted to be sure that nothing could follow him out of that dark attic.

He then looked through the keyhole and saw the mirror clearly. His reflection was still standing there, watching the door with a cold and hungry look.

### C1
Leo arrived at the old house on a cold autumn evening, the wind rattling the loose shutters like distant applause. The building loomed, its timber bones creaking under the weight of forgotten years, and an uneasy silence settled in every empty hallway. Leo had heard rumors of the house's long history, yet none of them prepared him for the chill that seemed to seep from the very walls. He placed his suitcase on the worn front step, feeling the floorboards groan beneath his weight as if the house were inhaling. A faint smell of damp pine and old paper hung in the air, hinting at stories that had never been spoken aloud.

After hours of shuffling boxes and arranging furniture, Leo finally felt the living room take shape, its dim lamps casting amber pools on the faded carpet. The house, still echoing with the soft sighs of settling timber, seemed to reward his effort with a tentative calm. Curiosity, however, pressed against his chest like a persistent drumbeat, urging him toward the narrow staircase that vanished into darkness. He remembered the attic, spoken of in hushed tones by the previous tenant, as a place where dust whispered and secrets lingered. With a tentative breath, he pulled the attic door open, the hinges protesting with a long, metallic sigh.

The attic greeted him with a thick, stale darkness that seemed to swallow the weak beam of his flashlight. Cobwebs hung from the low ceiling like delicate curtains, and the floor was a patchwork of uneven boards that creaked under each cautious step. A sour, metallic odor rose from piles of forgotten belongings, mingling with the faint perfume of old wood and long-forgotten incense. Among the clutter, a tall, ornate mirror stood in the far corner, its frame gilded with tarnished leaves that caught the trembling light. It seemed out of place, as if a portal had been dropped into the attic by accident, inviting a hesitant gaze.

Leo approached the glass, his breath fogging the surface momentarily before the surrounding dust settled like a thin veil. Despite the grime that clung to its edges, the mirror's surface was startlingly clear, reflecting the attic with a precision that defied its age. His own silhouette appeared, framed by the faint glow of the flashlight, yet the image seemed unnervingly crisp, as if the glass were a window rather than a pane. He lifted his right hand, watching the reflection mimic the motion with perfect fidelity, each finger articulated like a puppet obeying an unseen puppeteer. A sudden, inexplicable chill ran down his spine, and for an instant he wondered whether the mirror might be more than mere glass.

When Leo turned his head to the left, expecting his reflection to follow, the image remained stubbornly still, its eyes fixed on his own. The silence in the attic seemed to thicken, each breath he drew echoing louder than the last, as if the room itself were holding its breath. He stared back at the fixed figure, feeling the uncanny sensation that the mirror was watching him, not merely reflecting him. A thin line of doubt crept along his spine, whispering that the stillness was a warning, a silent command to remain unmoving. Yet the urge to understand, to test the boundary between reality and illusion, tugged at his curiosity like a moth to flame.

If bravery had taken root in his heart, he might have lingered, daring the mirror to reveal its secret. Instead, a surge of primal fear rose, hot and sudden, flooding his thoughts with images of unseen eyes and silent predators. He imagined the reflection stepping out, its cold stare carving a path through the thin veil that separated worlds. A shiver ran along his arms, and the notion of staying any longer felt like an invitation to danger. With a breath that tasted of iron, Leo made the decisive choice to retreat, his feet moving before his mind could protest.

He spun on his heel, the flashlight flickering as if hesitant to follow his sudden departure. Each step toward the attic door resonated like a drumbeat against the wooden planks, amplifying the rapid thud of his own heart. He could not shake the sensation that unseen eyes lingered behind him, their gaze lingering on his back as if measuring his resolve. The attic seemed to close in, the shadows deepening, while the faint echo of his own breathing filled the cramped space. Yet he forced himself forward, each breath a visible puff of vapor, his determination a thin thread against the looming dread.

Reaching the heavy oak door, Leo grasped the tarnished knob, feeling the cold metal seep into his palm. He pulled it shut with a weighty thud that resonated through the attic, as if the house itself exhaled a sigh of relief. The lock clicked into place, its metallic sound echoing like a promise that nothing could follow him out of that darkness. He pressed his palm against the wood, feeling the vibrations of his own heartbeat travel through the grain. A thin veil of safety settled over him, though the lingering presence in the attic made the air feel heavy with unspoken warning.

Leo knelt before the door, aligning his eye with the narrow keyhole, and peered into the dim interior. The flashlight's beam, now a thin line, illuminated the mirror's surface, revealing the stubborn figure still poised within. Its reflection stared back, eyes cold and hungry, as if the glass had become a portal to a waiting predator. A ripple of dread spread through Leo's chest, the sensation of being observed intensifying with each silent second. He realized that the mirror's gaze was not a mere reflection but a sentient stare that might reach beyond the glass at any moment.

Without daring another glance, he turned away, the weight of the locked door anchoring his resolve like a fragile shield. The attic's darkness seemed to pulse, each shadow whispering a promise that the mirror would wait for his return. Outside, the wind howled louder, rattling the shutters and reminding Leo that the world beyond still moved, indifferent to his terror. He descended the staircase, each step echoing a final farewell to the unseen watcher that lingered above. Yet, as the house settled behind him, a faint glimmer caught his eye from the attic window, a reminder that some mirrors never truly close.

## st-0060 — The Key Between the Pages
Kaynak: ozgun

### A1
Leo is a student. He reads in the old library. He sees something in a book. It is a small silver key. The key is between the pages.

Leo feels wonder. He wants to open something. He needs to find the lock. He wants to know the town history. He walks into the street.

He sees a note. The note is near the park. It is on a stone wall. The note tells him a secret. It talks about time.

Leo walks to the church. The church is in the center. He sees a small side door. The key fits the lock. He puts the key in.

He turns the key. The door is open now. He sees old records and letters. He finds his family history here. Leo is very happy.

The sun is down. The mystery is over. Leo feels good about his life. He follows the clues. He finds the truth.

### A2
Leo was a student. He liked to spend his quiet afternoon in the old library. He reads a book about local history. He saw something strange between the pages. A small silver key was deep inside the paper.

Leo felt wonder as he looked at the key. He decided he must find what the key opened. He wanted to do this before the sun went down. If he found the right door, he would know the town history.

He walked out into the street. The first clue was waiting for him. A small note was on a wall near the park. The note said the key belonged to a place of time.

Leo moved quickly toward the old church in the town center. The building was silent. He saw a small lock on a side door. It matched his key perfectly. He wondered if he should wait for another sign.

He pushed the key into the lock and turned it. The door opened. He saw a room full of old records and letters. Leo realized he found his family history in this secret place.

The sun went down behind the mountains. He finished his search. Everything about his life felt different now. He solved the mystery. He felt proud that he followed the clues to the end.

### B1
Leo was a student. He liked to spend his quiet afternoon hours in the old town library. He reads a book about local history. He noticed something strange between the pages. A small silver key had been hidden deep inside the paper.

Leo felt wonder as he looked at the mysterious object. He decided that he must find what the key opened before the sun went down. If he found the right door, he would finally understand the secret history of the town.

He walked out into the street where the first clue was waiting for him. A small note was on a stone wall near the park gate. The note suggested that the key belonged to a place where time stood still.

Leo moved quickly toward the old church at the center of the village. The building was silent. He noticed a small lock on a side door which matched his key perfectly. He wondered if the key would turn, or if he should wait for another sign.

### B2
Leo was a student who enjoyed spending his quiet afternoon hours in the old town library. While he read a book about local history, he noticed something strange between the pages. A small silver key had been hidden deep inside the paper.

Leo felt a sense of wonder as he looked at the mysterious object. He decided that he must discover what the key unlocked before the sun went down. If he found the right door, he would finally understand the secret history of the town.

He walked out into the street where the first clue was waiting for him. A small note was attached to a stone wall near the park gate. The note suggested that the key belonged to a place where time stood still.

Leo moved quickly toward the old church at the center of the village. The building was silent, but he noticed a small lock on a side door which matched his key perfectly. He wondered if the key would turn, or if he should wait for another sign.

He pushed the key into the lock and turned it carefully. The door opened to reveal a small room full of old records and forgotten letters. Leo realized that he had found the true history of his family within this secret place.

The sun was beginning to fall behind the mountains as he finished his search. Everything about his life now felt different because he had solved the mystery. He felt proud that he had followed the clues to the very end.

### C1
Leo, a third-year history student, habitually spent his quiet afternoons in the vaulted silence of the old town library. The scent of aged paper and dust mingled with a faint hint of pine, a comforting perfume that settled over the wooden tables. He had opened a thick volume on local folklore, its margins yellowed and its pages whispering stories of forgotten festivals. Midway through a passage about the town's founding, a glint caught his eye, as if a tiny star had slipped between the fibres. There, nestled in the crease of a page, lay a small silver key, its surface polished to a mirror sheen that seemed almost deliberate.

A sudden rush of wonder rose within him, as if the key were a silent invitation to uncover a story that time itself had concealed. He imagined the lock it might fit, picturing a hidden chamber where the town's forgotten secrets waited, patient as the dust on ancient shelves. The thought of solving the mystery before the sun slipped behind the distant hills became a quiet vow, a promise to himself. If the key unlocked something meaningful, perhaps it would finally illuminate the obscure lineage that had always hovered at the edges of his own identity. With a brisk breath, he slipped the book back onto the shelf, pocketed the key, and stepped out into the late-afternoon light.

The cobblestones of the narrow lane glistened after a brief rain, each stone reflecting the amber glow of street lamps that were just beginning to stir. Near the wrought-iron gate of the small park, a weathered stone wall bore a small, crumpled note pinned with a rusted thumbtack. The ink, though faded, read: 'The key belongs to a place where time stands still; seek the heart that never beats.' His mind flickered to the old church at the village centre, its tower a silent sentinel that had watched generations rise and fall. Without lingering, Leo turned left, his steps echoing against the stone as he hurried toward the looming silhouette of the building.

The path to the church wound through a narrow alley lined with ivy-clad bricks, each leaf trembling in the gentle autumn breeze. Above him, the bell tower rose like a weathered crown, its stone face mottled with lichen, hinting at centuries of whispered prayers. A faint echo of choir chants drifted from within, a melancholy hymn that seemed to guide his steps toward destiny. He paused before the heavy oak doors, feeling the coolness of stone seep through the cracks, a reminder that history lingered in every shadow. Then his eyes caught a small, iron lock set into the side wall of the chapel, its keyhole perfectly matching the curve of his silver find.

A shiver ran down his spine, half from the chill of the stone and half from the thrill of the unknown, as he lifted the key. He slipped the slender silver piece into the lock, feeling the cold metal greet his fingertips like a long-awaited handshake. The tumblers inside clicked softly, each sound resonating like a secret pulse, and the lock yielded with a reluctant sigh. The door creaked open, revealing a narrow stairwell descending into darkness, its walls lined with faded frescoes of saints long forgotten. Leo steadied his breath, aware that every step forward was a step deeper into the past that had patiently waited for his curiosity.

At the bottom of the stairwell, a modest wooden door stood ajar, spilling a thin beam of amber light onto a carpet of dust. Inside, rows of oak cabinets stretched to the ceiling, each drawer labeled in elegant cursive with dates that stretched back to the seventeenth century. Leo's fingers brushed over brittle parchment, letters penned in ink that had faded to sepia, recounting deeds, marriages, and tragedies of families long vanished. Among the stacks he uncovered a leather-bound journal bearing his family's crest, its pages filled with a narrative that traced his ancestry to a forgotten shipwright. A sudden warmth rose in his chest as he realized that the mystery of the silver key was not merely a puzzle, but a bridge to his own roots.

As he read the final entry, the sun slipped behind the distant mountains, casting long shadows that painted the room in gold and violet. The fading light seemed to echo the passage of time recorded in the journal, each line a thread weaving past into present. He felt a profound connection to the men and women whose names were etched in ink, their hopes and fears now reverberating within him. The realization that the key had unlocked not only a door but also a lineage filled him with a quiet pride that settled deep in his bones. He tucked the journal into his satchel, aware that the story he now carried would shape the chapters he had yet to write.

Leaving the church, Leo stepped back onto the cobbled street, where the evening air was scented with wet stone and distant pine. The note's cryptic promise lingered in his mind, yet the answer he sought had materialized in the very heart of the town's history. He understood now that mysteries are not obstacles but invitations, each clue a stepping stone toward a deeper understanding of who we are. As the first stars appeared, their faint light seemed to echo the silver glint of the key still hidden in his pocket. He smiled, feeling that the quiet afternoons he cherished in the library had gained a new dimension-a living story that he now carried forward.

Back in the familiar hush of the library, Leo placed the silver key on the table, its cold surface catching the lamplight like a tiny star. He opened the same book, now aware that every margin might conceal another secret, and felt the pages breathe beneath his fingertips. A gentle rustle announced the arrival of the evening caretaker, whose smile hinted that the library itself guarded stories waiting for curious hearts. She whispered that the key had been missing for decades, and that its return might awaken memories long dormant within the town's collective soul. Leo nodded, realizing that his discovery was both an ending and a beginning, a thread that would tug at future seekers of hidden truths.

Walking home beneath a sky embroidered with the first pale stars, Leo contemplated how a single object could compress centuries into a heartbeat. He imagined the countless hands that had once turned the same key, each owner imprinting a fragment of their own story upon it. The notion that history lives not only in grand monuments but also in forgotten pockets of metal gave him a reverent respect for the mundane. He resolved to document the journal's contents, to share the voices of those ancestors with anyone willing to listen, thereby extending their quiet existence. In that promise lay the true reward of his adventure: the transformation of curiosity into stewardship, a duty he would carry forward.

Later, as the library's clocks chimed midnight, Leo slipped the key into his pocket, feeling its weight as a silent oath. He knew that the town's hidden narratives would continue to surface, each revelation a ripple that reshaped the surface of collective memory. The mystery of the missing silver key had taught him that every ordinary day holds the potential for extraordinary discovery, if one dares to look. And so, with the night draping the village in a velvet hush, Leo returned to his studies, his mind alive with new questions. The silver key remained a quiet promise lodged between his fingers, reminding him that the past is never truly buried, only waiting to be uncovered.

## st-0070 — A Letter from Someone Lonely
Kaynak: ozgun

### A1
Leo sits in the local library. He sits at a small table. The table is near the back window. Leo is there every day.

Leo finds a note in a book. The note is from a lonely person. Leo writes a letter back. He leaves the note in the book. He hopes the person finds it.

They write notes to each other. The notes give Leo comfort. Leo likes the quiet hours.

Leo wants to know who she is. He never sees her write. A girl sits at the next table. She is there every afternoon. She often looks at Leo.

Leo thinks she is the girl. He writes a note to her. He asks her to meet him.

The girl stands up. She walks to his table. She has a soft smile. She holds his letter. She agrees to have coffee with him.

### A2

Leo decided to write a reply. He left it in the same book the next day. He hoped that the author would find his message.

They began a secret conversation through the pages of many books. Each note gave him comfort during the quiet hours.

She often looked at him. He wondered if she was the author. He decided to leave a note asking her to meet him.

The girl stood up and walked toward his table. She had a soft smile. She held his letter in her hand. She agreed to have coffee with him.

### B1

### B2
Leo spent every afternoon in the local library. He always sat at the same small table near the back window.

One day, he found a mysterious handwritten note inside a borrowed book. The letter was written by someone who felt lonely in the city.

Leo decided to write a reply and left it in the same book the next day. He hoped that the anonymous author would find his message.

They began a secret correspondence through the pages of many books. Each note provided him with comfort during the quiet hours.

Leo wondered who she was, as he had never seen her write. He noticed a girl who sat at the next table every afternoon.

She often looked at him, and he wondered if she was the author. Finally, he decided to leave a note asking her to meet him.

The girl stood up and walked toward his table with a soft smile. She held his letter in her hand and agreed to have coffee.

### C1
Every afternoon, Leo slipped through the library doors as if entering a quiet sanctuary from the bustling city outside. He chose the same narrow table tucked beneath a high back window, where the late afternoon light fell in thin, amber ribbons. From that spot he could watch the dust motes dance, hear the soft rustle of pages, and feel the world slow to a gentle pulse. The librarians rarely noticed him, and the other patrons kept to their own worlds, leaving the room hushed and intimate. For Leo, the silence was not empty; it was a canvas on which his thoughts could spread without interruption.

One rain-spattered Thursday, while thumbing through a battered volume of poetry, a folded scrap of paper slipped out and landed on his palm. Its ink was uneven, as if written in a hurry, and the words trembled with a quiet loneliness that matched the gray sky outside. The letter confessed a yearning for connection, a confession that the author felt adrift in a city that seemed to swallow strangers whole. It ended with a simple appeal: if anyone understood the ache, please write back, and perhaps the two of them could share a moment of warmth. Leo felt a sudden stir in his chest, as though the stranger's plea had reached across the stacks and tapped his own solitude.

That evening, after the library lights dimmed and the last patron slipped away, Leo retrieved a clean sheet from his bag and began to write. He chose words that were careful yet sincere, describing the way the sun filtered through the stained glass and how the scent of old paper soothed him. He tucked the letter between the pages of the same poetry book, hoping the anonymous author would discover it among the verses the next day. When he placed the volume back on the shelf, his heart thudded like a quiet drum, each beat echoing the possibility of a new connection. He left the library, the rain now a gentle patter, and walked home feeling as if he had stepped into a story he had only ever read.

The next morning, Leo returned to the same table, the faint smell of coffee mingling with the musty perfume of bound leather. He found a fresh note slipped into the book, its ink darker, its tone more hopeful, as if the writer had smiled while writing. The reply spoke of a favorite passage, Keats' 'bright star', and how the line resonated when the city's clamor grew too loud for his thoughts. Leo read the words aloud, feeling the cadence of another's voice ripple through the quiet, and a warm glow spread through his chest. He answered with a reference to a novel that had once comforted him, and tucked his new letter beside the poetry, trusting the unseen hand to retrieve it.

Days turned into weeks, and each afternoon the library became a small theatre where two strangers performed a silent dialogue across paper. Leo began to anticipate the gentle rustle of a page turning as the signal that his confidante was near, a ritual that steadied his heartbeat. When the notes arrived, they carried fragments of the author's life, a fleeting glimpse of a rainy balcony, the taste of tea left cooling on a saucer. Each paragraph seemed to wrap Leo in a soft blanket, shielding him from the loneliness that often settled like dust on the library shelves. He started to look forward to the moments when the quiet would be broken not by a phone ring, but by a handwritten voice that understood his interior world.

One bright afternoon, a girl began to occupy the table beside his, her shoulders hunched over a thick novel as if sheltering a secret. She lifted her gaze occasionally, catching his eye, and for a heartbeat Leo wondered whether the ink on the pages might belong to her. Her hair fell in loose curls that brushed against the edge of the desk, and the faint perfume of jasmine lingered whenever she turned a page. Leo sensed a parallel rhythm between their movements, both reaching for the same marginal notes, both pausing to listen to the building's soft sighs. Yet he could not be certain; the letters he received never bore a signature, and the anonymity that had drawn him in now threatened to eclipse the mystery he cherished.

After weeks of exchanging thoughts like whispers, Leo resolved to bridge the gap between paper and flesh, to see the eyes behind the ink. He crafted a brief invitation, careful not to betray his own nervous tremor, asking if she would meet him at the cafe across the square at dusk. He folded the note like a paper crane, slipped it into the back cover of the poetry book, and placed his own hand on the cold wood as if sealing a pact. When the bell rang, announcing the library's closing, he lingered a moment longer, watching the girl gather her things, hoping she would notice his offering. He left the building with his heart thudding like a metronome, each step echoing the rhythm of a question he had finally dared to ask.

Minutes later, the girl rose from her chair, a soft smile curving her lips as she approached his table with deliberate grace. She carried his note in her hand, the paper slightly crinkled, as if it had traveled a short, hopeful distance. When she placed it on the table, their eyes met, and for a breathless instant Leo felt the invisible barrier between them dissolve. She whispered, 'I have been waiting for this moment,' and the words seemed to reverberate against the high windows, filling the room with anticipation. Together they stepped out into the amber dusk, their shadows merging as they walked toward the cafe, ready to turn inked promises into spoken conversation.

The cafe, tucked beneath a century-old townhouse, glowed with the warm light of hanging bulbs, and the scent of roasted beans swirled around their table. They spoke in low tones, their sentences punctuated by occasional laughter, as the street outside fell silent beneath the night's first stars. Leo found her voice to be softer than he had imagined, each word a gentle ripple that matched the calm of his own heart. She confessed that the letters had been a lifeline, a way to keep the city's cold edges at bay, and she thanked him for answering. Their coffee grew cold, but the conversation warmed them, and they promised to meet again, each aware that the story they had begun would continue beyond the pages.

As Leo walked home beneath the lamplight, the city's noise seemed softer, as if the world had been tuned to a more intimate frequency. He thought of the library's quiet corners, now forever linked to a voice that had turned his solitude into a shared narrative. The letters, once hidden between worn pages, had become bridges, and the bridge he had built with ink now led to a real presence beside him. He smiled at the thought that a simple note could alter the course of an ordinary afternoon, turning a routine visit into the start of something tender. And as the night deepened, he felt a quiet optimism settle within him, a belief that the next page of his life would be written together.

## st-0080 — The Key He Left Behind
Kaynak: ozgun

### A1
Leo lives in a small city house. Every morning he has a routine. He drinks coffee and eats bread. He checks for his blue key. Today he feels a little distracted.

Leo goes to his office. He looks for the blue key. It is not in his bag. He loses the key. He decides to return to the park.

The park is quiet. The sun goes down. Leo walks along the path. He looks under every tree. He looks around every bush. The light is very low.

He remembers the birds. He remembers the wooden bench. He walks toward the bench. The grass is long and thick. He looks at the ground.

Leo reaches the bench. He sits on the cold ground. He looks under the bench. He moves some dry leaves. The blue key is there. It shines in the dark. He smiles and feels happy.

The day is a little hard. The problem is now over. He walks back to his house. He is very careful now. He arrives home at night.

### A2
Leo lived in a small city house. Every morning, he followed the same routine. He would drink coffee and eat some bread. He checked his pockets for the blue key. Today, Leo felt a little distracted.

Leo reached his office. He realized the blue key was not in his bag. He had lost it. The key was important for his home. It probably fell out during his walk. He decided to return to the park.

The park was quiet. The sun began to go down. Leo walked slowly along the path. He looked under every tree. He looked around every bush. The light was fading fast.

He remembered he stopped to watch birds near a bench. Perhaps the key fell while he sat there. He walked toward the bench. He looked down at the ground. The grass was long and thick. It was hard to see anything.

Leo reached the bench. He knelt on the cold ground. He checked under the wooden bench. He moved some dry leaves aside. There was the blue key. It was shining in the dark. He picked it up with a smile. He felt a great sense of relief.

His day was stressful, but the problem was over. He walked back home. He was glad he returned to the park. Leo understood he should be more careful. He arrived at his house at night.

### B2
