### C1
The night air was crisp as Max and his longtime companion, Leo, set up their modest tent beneath a canopy of towering pines. A thin veil of mist curled around the forest floor, muffling the distant hoot of an owl and the rustle of unseen creatures. They had planned a weekend of simple hikes and campfire stories, hoping the solitude would sharpen their minds after a hectic semester. Max checked the weathered map of the region, its edges frayed like the bark of the ancient trees surrounding them. Leo laughed softly, teasing that the forest might hide treasure, but both men felt a quiet anticipation rising in their chests.

After a short trek, a glint caught Leo's eye near a moss-covered stone, prompting him to brush aside the damp lichen. Embedded in the soil lay a parchment, its surface darkened by age, unmistakably a map that had been forgotten. The ink, though faded, traced a winding river that vanished into a cluster of symbols resembling crumbling towers. Max's fingers trembled as he lifted the fragile sheet, feeling the weight of history settle into his palm. A sudden chill ran down his spine, as if the forest itself were urging them to follow the hidden path.

The map revealed a concealed city, nestled deep within the woods, shielded by a wall that rose like a silent sentinel. Every line seemed to whisper promises of mystery, and the two friends exchanged a glance that spoke louder than words. Leo, ever the pragmatic one, suggested they mark the route with stones, lest they lose their bearing after dusk. Max, however, felt a surge of curiosity that eclipsed any fear; he imagined ancient streets lit by torches, echoing forgotten chants. A pact formed in that moment: they would uncover the city's secrets together, no matter the obstacles that lay ahead.

They pressed onward, the forest growing denser as the sun slipped toward the horizon, painting the canopy in amber hues. Each step was accompanied by the soft crunch of fallen leaves, a rhythmic reminder of the distance they still had to travel. When twilight settled, a cool dampness settled on their skin, and fatigue settled deep in their muscles, yet resolve hardened like iron. "We can't turn back now," Max whispered, his breath forming fleeting clouds, and Leo nodded, eyes reflecting the faint glow of fireflies. Their shadows merged with the darkness, but the map's promise guided them onward, each heartbeat echoing the drum of adventure.

At the edge of a clearing, the trees gave way to a formidable stone wall, its surface slick with moss and time. It rose abruptly, twenty meters high, its stones arranged in a pattern that hinted at an ancient code. Leo ran his hand along the cold surface, feeling the faint vibrations of a long-forgotten heartbeat within the masonry. "There must be an entrance," Max murmured, eyes scanning for any irregularity that might betray a hidden doorway. A narrow slit of light flickered at the base of the wall, as if inviting them to discover what lay concealed beyond.

Behind the slit, a rusted iron door stood ajar, its surface etched with symbols that matched those on the map. A heavy lock barred the entrance, its bolt stubbornly refusing to yield to any force. Leo's eyes fell on a small, brass key half-buried in a crevice, glinting like a promise. With a careful twist, the key turned, and the lock clicked open, releasing a sigh of ancient air. The door groaned as it swung inward, revealing a dim corridor whose stone floor was slick with centuries of forgotten rain.

Inside, darkness pressed against their eyes, broken only by the faint glow of bioluminescent fungi clinging to the walls. A distant, echoing clatter sent a shiver down their spines, suggesting that they were not alone in the forgotten city. Max swallowed hard, his mind racing through possibilities: a wild animal, a wandering specter, or perhaps the city's own restless guardians. They moved with deliberate silence, each footfall softened by the thick carpet of moss that covered the ancient stones. A faint, rhythmic ticking resonated from deeper within, urging them onward toward a room that promised answers.

The door to the chamber stood framed by towering pillars, each bearing intricate mosaics of celestial bodies and mythic creatures. Within, a series of stone tablets lay arranged like a puzzle, their surfaces etched with riddles that demanded wit and patience. Leo traced his finger over the first inscription, noting that the answer lay hidden in the pattern of the constellations above. Max, recalling the map's symbols, aligned the tiles accordingly, feeling the room's atmosphere shift as each piece clicked into place. When the final piece settled, a concealed compartment opened, revealing a chest that glittered with the promise of untold wealth.

Inside the chest lay a trove of gold coins, their surfaces stamped with symbols of a civilization long vanished. The sight of the gleaming treasure sparked a brief, ecstatic grin on their faces, a reward for their perseverance. Suddenly, a resonant voice echoed through the stone, commanding them to leave the city at once, lest they incur its ancient wrath. The tone was neither angry nor kind; it was a solemn warning that seemed to emanate from the very walls themselves. Without hesitation, Max and Leo gathered the gold and hurried back through the corridor, their hearts pounding with both triumph and urgency.

Emerging into the fading twilight, they found the city's wall receding behind them, its silhouette merging with the encroaching darkness. The forest seemed to welcome them, the rustle of leaves now a gentle applause for their safe return. Back at their campsite, the fire crackled, casting warm light over their tired faces, and the scent of pine soot soothed their nerves. They exchanged a quiet acknowledgment of the teamwork that had carried them through each obstacle, the bond between them now forged like steel. Looking at the gold they had secured, they realized that the true treasure was the confidence they now possessed in one another.

As dawn broke, Max and Leo packed their gear, already dreaming of the next expedition that would test their courage anew. The forest had taught them that perseverance could transform fear into curiosity, and that every hidden path held a story waiting to be uncovered. They promised each other that no matter how daunting the challenge, they would face it together, trusting the map of friendship more than any parchment. With the sunrise painting the canopy gold, they set off toward the horizon, their steps light, their spirits unbreakable. The memory of the lost city lingered like a secret, reminding them that the world still held mysteries worth seeking, and that they were ready to answer its call.

### A1

### B2
Lily was at the park with her bike on a truly nice day. The sky was a clear blue, and the bright sun gave a pleasant heat. She went around on her bike, feeling the wind against her face, and a sense of freedom filled her. The park was full of life. Children played, and people walked along the paths. A friendly dog was near a public seat, observing Lily with interest. This dog, who was a good friend to Max, moved its tail gently, seeming to offer a silent greeting. Max, the owner, stood near the dog. A warm smile was on his face as he watched the park activity. Lily felt a simple joy in the moment, enjoying the fresh air and the open space.

After some time, Lily decided to rest for a moment. She left her bike by a tree, thinking it would be safe there. When she looked back a few minutes later, the bike was not in its place. A wave of worry came over her. "No," she thought. "My bike is gone." She looked around, her heart starting to beat faster. She walked quickly to Max, who was still with his dog. "Max," she asked, her voice a little not sure, "did you see my bike?" "It was just here?" Max looked at the spot where the bike had been. "I am sorry, Lily," he said. "I did not see your bike go away from this area." Lily felt a drop of fear. If she had been more careful, the bike would not have been taken.

Lily and Max decided they must look for the bike right away. They began to walk around the park, their eyes scanning every corner. They asked several people who were walking by, hoping for some help. Each person they asked said they had not seen a bike like hers. Lily started to feel very sad. As they continued their search, a small child, who was playing near a group of trees, called out. "I saw a bike far away!" the child said. "It was near that big tree, over there." The child pointed toward a distant part of the park. Lily and Max quickly went to the direction the child had shown. They walked through a wide grass area, and there, near a tall tree, was Lily's bike. The bike was found by Max, who had a sharp eye. It was brought back to Lily, and she felt a great sense of relief. Lily thanked Max for his important help, feeling truly grateful. Max felt good that he had been able to help Lily in her time of need.

From that day, Lily and Max became good friends. They spent some time talking about their lives. They made plans to play in the park together again soon. As the sun began to set, painting the sky with warm colors, they said goodbye to each other. Lily started her journey home on foot. She had her bike with her once more. She felt a deep sense of happiness. Her bike was safe, and she had made a new friend. She went home with a calm mind. She knew she was safe. The day, despite the brief problem, had ended well. Lily thought about all that had happened. She felt truly good about the experience. She knew, more than ever, that help could come from a friend when it was most needed.

Max was known for his kind nature. If someone in the park needed help, they often asked Max. He was always ready to offer his support. Max would always say he could help, no matter the situation. The park was a special place where people could meet, form connections, and help each other. The dog, Max's true friend, always stayed close to him. It would often move its tail with quiet joy. It looked at people with friendly eyes. Lily smiled at the dog and then at Max. She felt that the world was kind. It was full of good people, even with its small problems. She hoped that each day would be good, just like this one had turned out to be.

The park itself was a place of beauty and peace. It had many tall trees and a small, clear lake. The lake was always calm, and its surface showed the blue sky above like a mirror. A small bird often went over the lake. Children loved to play on the soft grass, their laughter a happy sound in the air. A game, perhaps a ball, sometimes rolled near a public seat, where people rested. This seat was close to a winding path, which went through the many trees. People walked slowly on the path, talking and enjoying the calm atmosphere. The air was wonderfully fresh and cool. The sun was warm and gave gentle light to all the park's natural features.

Max was a student, working part-time. He also had a bike, which he used daily for his travel to work and school. He loved the park very much and made sure to visit it often. He truly thought the park was a good place to learn about life and about people. If Max were busy with his studies or his part-time work, he might not come to the park. However, if he were not busy, he would always be there, ready to help anyone who needed it. His commitment to the park and to helping others was a strong part of his character.

As Lily got ready to leave with her bike, Max saw something small under a public seat. "Wait a moment, Lily," he said, and went to look. They looked under the seat and found an item. The item was a head covering, and it belonged to Lily. The head covering was very near where her bike had been. Lily took the head covering and quickly put it on her head. She felt a new sense of safety and was ready for her journey home. Max saw Lily smile, and he felt good inside.

Lily knew that if her bike had been away for good, she would have felt very bad. But because the bike was found, she felt really good. The bike was safe now, and it was ready for her to ride it home. Lily planned to go home and rest. She also planned to go back to school tomorrow, feeling ready for a new day. She knew that the day would end well. The story of her lost and found bike was simple. It showed a clear lesson. Help could always be found, especially from a kind friend.

The day in the park was coming to an end, and both Lily and Max felt a quiet joy. The experience had brought them closer. This was a good outcome for a day that started with some worry. They knew they would meet again very soon.

Looking ahead, they knew the park would always be there for them. If a storm came, the park might be shut for a short period. However, people who loved the park would simply wait for the sun to return. When the sun did return, bright and warm, the bike could be ridden again. Lily might ride her bike with Max, enjoying the paths together. They might even share the bike for fun, taking turns to ride and push. The dog, Max's true friend, might run and play with them. The park would be bright when the sun came up each morning. Birds would call to each other, going over the trees, and the air would be fresh and cool. Many children would come to play games.

In the coming days, Lily and Max would meet again and share many stories. Lily would tell her friends about the day her bike was lost and then found. Max would listen, a good smile on his face. The dog, ever true, would move its tail with gentle happiness. The day would always end with a calm sky. Night would appear. Lily would sleep, thinking of the park and her new friend. She would wake with a smile, feeling good. Her bike would be ready for another ride. Their friendship would continue to grow strong.

### C1
The sun hung high in a cloudless sky, spilling warm light over the city park where Lily had come with her bicycle. A gentle breeze brushed her cheeks, carrying the faint scent of freshly cut grass and distant blossoms. She pedaled along the winding path, feeling the rhythmic hum of wheels against pavement and a sudden, liberating sense of freedom. Around her, children laughed as they chased one another, while joggers moved in measured strides, their breath syncing with the rustling leaves. Near a wooden bench, a friendly golden-retriever wagged its tail, eyes bright with curiosity, as its owner, Max, watched the scene with a calm smile.

After a few leisurely laps, Lily grew thirsty and chose a large oak to set her bike against, confident that the sturdy trunk would keep it safe. She slipped off the saddle, stretched her arms, and inhaled the fragrant air, feeling a brief moment of tranquility. When she returned a handful of minutes later, the space beside the tree was empty, and her heart thudded with sudden alarm. A cold knot formed in her stomach as she whispered, 'No, my bike is gone,' while scanning the grass for any clue. She hurried toward Max, whose dog rested obediently at his feet, hoping the kindly man might have seen something.

Max turned his head, his eyebrows lifting in surprise, and said, 'I thought it was right here; I saw no one approach.' Lily's mind raced, replaying each second, wondering whether she had been careless enough to leave the lock unattended. She nodded, voice trembling slightly, and asked, 'Shall we look together? I don't want to waste any more time.' Max agreed without hesitation, his expression softening, and together they set off, eyes scanning every bench, shrub, and pathway. Passersby glanced at the duo, offering polite nods, yet none could provide the missing information they desperately needed.

First they approached a couple strolling with a stroller, and Lily politely inquired whether they had noticed a red bicycle. The woman shook her head, apologizing, and continued her walk, her shoes clicking softly on the paved trail. Next they queried a teenage boy perched on a low wall, who shrugged and muttered that he had been listening to music. A sense of frustration settled over Lily, but she forced herself to stay hopeful, recalling that the park often held hidden helpers. Just then a small voice rang out from near a clump of birches: 'I saw a bike near the big tree!'

The child, a freckled girl no older than seven, pointed eagerly toward a towering maple whose leaves whispered in the wind. Lily and Max hurried across the open meadow, their footsteps stirring the tall grass, each blade bending under their hurried stride. There, tucked against the trunk, lay Lily's bicycle, its frame glinting faintly as the sun caught its chrome surface. Max knelt, lifted the bike gently, and handed it to Lily, whose eyes filled with relief and gratitude. She slipped her fingers onto the handlebars, feeling the familiar grip, and whispered a quiet thank-you to the unseen kindness around her.

The two settled on the same bench where Max's dog had rested, the golden retriever nudging Lily's shoe with a playful paw. They exchanged stories about school and hobbies; Lily spoke of her love for sketching, while Max described his part-time job at the library. As the sun dipped toward the horizon, the sky transformed into a canvas of amber and rose, painting everything with a gentle glow. Both felt a quiet contentment, aware that a simple mishap had forged a bond stronger than either had expected. When the last golden rays faded, they stood, exchanged a warm handshake, and Lily began the walk home, her bike safely beside her.

Word of Max's helpful nature traveled through the park like a gentle breeze, and many visitors sought his assistance without hesitation. He was often found sitting on that same bench, reading a novel or polishing his own bicycle, ready to lend a hand. The park itself was a sanctuary of tall elms and a crystal-clear lake that mirrored the sky, reflecting clouds like soft cotton. Birdsong floated above the water, while children's laughter bubbled from the grassy lawns, creating a symphony of ordinary joy. Even the winding footpaths, edged with mossy stones, seemed to guide visitors toward calm moments and unexpected friendships.

Max balanced his university studies in environmental science with a part-time shift at a nearby cafe, cycling each morning to save both money and carbon. He cherished the park as a living laboratory, where observation of trees and people alike taught him lessons no lecture ever could. When his schedule grew tight, he would skip the park, but he always returned as soon as he could, driven by a quiet devotion. His reliability had become a quiet cornerstone for many regulars, who knew that a simple request would be met with a reassuring smile. The dog, ever faithful, never strayed far from his side, its tail swaying in rhythm with Max's steady footsteps.

Just as Lily prepared to leave, Max's eyes caught a glint beneath the bench, prompting him to call, 'Wait, Lily, look here!' Together they knelt, lifted the wooden slat, and discovered a small, folded scarf that matched the color of Lily's favorite hat. She slipped the soft fabric over her ears, feeling instantly more secure, as if the park itself were offering protection. Max watched her smile, a quiet pride blooming inside him, knowing that a simple act had eased her lingering worry. With the bike and scarf in hand, Lily felt a renewed confidence, ready to face the walk home and the challenges ahead.

The evening air grew cooler, and the park's lanterns flickered on, casting soft amber pools on the pathways. Lily thought about the day's events, realizing that misfortune could be transformed into an opportunity for connection. She promised herself to be more vigilant with her belongings, yet also to trust in the goodwill of strangers. Max, feeling the quiet satisfaction of a day well spent, looked forward to future encounters, perhaps sharing a ride or a coffee. Both knew that the park would remain a constant refuge, its trees standing ready to shelter anyone who sought solace.

As night fell, the sky turned a deep indigo, dotted with stars that seemed to watch over the sleepy benches. Lily arrived home, locked her bike in the garage, and placed the scarf on her pillow, a reminder of the day's gentle lesson. She fell asleep with a smile, dreaming of the park's rustling leaves and the promise of many more sunny afternoons. Max, after a long day, returned to his modest apartment, the dog curling up beside him, both content and hopeful. In the quiet of the night, the park's memory lingered like a soft melody, assuring that kindness, once sparked, never truly fades.

---
